TWO UNSOUGHT OBSERVATIONS FROM THE SAME RUN: `profile_home_key` is not merely unread but MOSTLY UNSET -- `USER LIST` shows two of six members carrying a profile -- and since `user_profile_root()` maps an empty name to "default", wiring authority 3 to authority 2 today would silently place FOUR OF SIX MEMBERS IN ONE SHARED HOME while appearing to scope them, an argument against the obvious wire independent of the vanishing-postures one; and the owner seat is UNSECURED (`bootstrap: no password set`), which gates `USER AS` and is a deployment state rather than a source defect. `USER AS` changes who RBAC thinks you are (`identity_admin.cpp:430,:435`) and does NOT touch the lock owner, which is a per-process `host:pid:ms` memoised in a function-local static -- so two members in one process share ONE lock identity and the second can release the first's locks, while one member in two processes gets TWO and can block themselves. `LOCK WHO <n>`, whose usage says it "reports the owner of record n", prints `host:pid:ms`: A COMMAND NAMED WHO THAT CANNOT SAY WHO. THE FINDING ARGUES AGAINST ITSELF DELIBERATELY: process-scoped lock identity is NOT simply wrong -- classic xBase locks per workstation and `host:pid` is the right token for LIVENESS, which stale-lock reclamation needs and which AIF-116/AIF-031 already hardened once (locale digit grouping produced `16,984` and made every live lock look stale). The defect is narrower -- THE MEMBER IS NOWHERE IN THE LOCK RECORD -- so the fix is BOTH, not INSTEAD. MAIN NEGATIVE RECOMMENDATION: do NOT wire `current_user_name()` to the live actor, tempting though the wire is (the layouts already match -- `user_profile_root()` builds exactly the `dottalkpp/user/<key>/` that `profile_home_key`'s Contract 5 comment names). It feeds `resolve_workspace_file_path()` and therefore BOTH `catalog_dir()` and `WORKSPACE SAVE`, so during an agent session every posture and the whole catalog would resolve under a different root and APPEAR TO VANISH with no error -- an empty result is not a measurement, applied to a data root. Second trap in the same header: `workspace_search_roots()` is a SEARCH list, correct for FINDING a posture and ambiguous for choosing where a NEW row is WRITTEN. LATENT THREADING DEFECT NAMED WHILE STILL LATENT: `acting_member_key()` and `principal_key()` return `const std::string&` into a mutable global written by SIX sites, so a reader holding that reference across a login or `USER AS` is not racing on a value -- string assignment can reallocate the buffer -- which is UB at 12 sites; NOT a bug today, measured: `src/gui/core/session.cpp` has no `std::thread`, `QThread`, `std::mutex` or `invokeMethod`. WHY THIS BLOCKS GUI THREADING, AND IT IS NOT THE RACE: all five authorities are PROCESS-scoped, and the GUI became a first-class engine consumer at AIF-078 step 2b, so the day two sessions share a process they share ONE lock owner and CANNOT CONTEND -- every lock between them succeeds and excludes nothing, a mutex that was never there. Normalizing identity is therefore not a threading fix, it is the PREREQUISITE: who-am-I must become per-SESSION before it can become per-thread. THREE RULINGS STATED AND NOT TAKEN: whether `Owner::operator==` folds in the member (it compares the whole id string, so folding it in decides whether a process may release locks it took before a `USER AS`); whether the path resolver may follow the live actor (argued NO, or not before search roots are separated from the write root); and what becomes of the `SECURITY` legacy selector, which to its credit DECLARES its own limits in its usage block -- the good version of R5 -- though the fence is prose and nothing enforces it. NOT CLAIMED: that any of the five is individually wrong (each is defensible in its own scope; the defect is that there are five), that data has been lost, that `USER` can drive `current_user_name()` today (measured: they do not speak), or any sizing of the work. Owner `member.derald`; steward `member.ai.claude.cowork`. |

INERT TODAY ONLY BECAUSE NOTHING INCLUDES IT (`git grep -l` over `*.cpp` and `*.hpp` returns nothing), which is coincidence rather than structure: the file is named `sessions.hpp`, sits in `include/` beside headers people do include, and contains exactly what someone searching for shell settings wants. HOW IT GOT ITS SUPPORTED STAMP, and this is the reusable part: `3706da78c`, the AIF-050 `@dottalk.file` backfill across 1034 files, applied it. The sweep asked whether the file carried a header block, not whether the file was reachable -- R75 in a new place, and worse than uninformative because the output is an AFFIRMATION. A METADATA SWEEP CONVERTS ABSENCE-OF-METADATA INTO PRESENCE-OF-A-CLAIM, AND THE CLAIM INHERITS THE SWEEP'S BLINDNESS. THREE OPTIONS RECORDED AND NOT RULED: delete it (nothing includes it); neutralise it in place with a `#error` or a redirect to the real header so the mistake is loud; or demote the metadata -- the third is listed so it can be REJECTED EXPLICITLY, because it feels like housekeeping and changes nothing (a `#include` does not read a header block). NOT CLAIMED: that a bug exists today, that `sessions.hpp` is the older fork, or that the three named members are the only differences -- member NAMES were compared; default values, alignment and the `detail::` initialiser helpers were not exhaustively diffed, so the layout difference is proven and its full extent is not. `include/**` is engine and any fix wants an explicit go. | AIF-142 | A deleted row is ABSENT FROM AN ORDERED TRAVERSAL and merely FILTERED from a physical one: `DELETE` captures and removes the record's index keys, so an ordered walk never yields it, while a physical walk yields it and the consumer's filter rejects it; Claude/Cowork 2026-08-27, found while proving the repaired GPS when the first deleted-record test returned the reason nobody predicted | source_defect, engine, deletion_semantics, traversal, set_deleted, r5, runtime_proven | DotTalk++ `src/cli/cmd_delete.cpp` index delete snapshot, `src/cli/cmd_recall.cpp` best-effort reindex, `src/cli/order_iterator.cpp` traversal, `include/cli/settings.hpp` `deleted_on` | claim `coordination/aif/AIF-142.claim`; finding `docs/maintenance/AIF142_FINDING_A_DELETED_ROW_IS_ABSENT_FROM_AN_ORDERED_TRAVERSAL_V1.md`; runtime transcript in that finding sec 1; run `COWORK-20260827-001` | MIXED BASIS -- sec 1-4 and 6 runtime-proven, sec 5 (`SET DELETED OFF`) SOURCE-EVIDENCED ONLY and NOT RUN; measured, not fixed; no fix authorized | The engine ALREADY STATES THIS in `cmd_recall.cpp:22` -- "active indexes normally contain only live records and would otherwise hide deleted rows" -- but files it as RECALL's private workaround for its own target selection, where no reader of LIST, COUNT, GPS or BROWSE would look; a system property discovered while fixing one command got documented as that command's problem. MEASURED SIDE BY SIDE on recno 21 of STUDENTS the same day: ordered gives `none (record not present in the active order)`, physical gives `none (record is filtered out)` -- one record, one state, two engine facts at two different layers, and a consumer can change its filter but cannot change what is not there. `order_iterator.cpp` has NO deleted handling of any kind, so every difference comes from index contents or the consumer's own filter. PREDICTED NOT MEASURED (sec 5): `SET DELETED` is read only by FILTERS (`browse_filters.cpp:26`, `cmd_count.cpp:437`, `filter_registry.cpp:287`/`:316`), so with it OFF the physical path can show deleted rows and the ordered path structurally cannot -- a capability divergence wearing the costume of a policy setting, inert in a way that reads as compliance. One test closes it and is written out in sec 8. CONSEQUENCE ALREADY REACHABLE: RECALL's reindex is `mutates_index_entries: best-effort` by declaration, so a record can be LIVE IN THE TABLE and ABSENT FROM EVERY ORDERED TRAVERSAL; the repaired GPS's new `not present in the active order` arm is the DETECTOR for that state and should not be softened back into a number. REPRODUCIBILITY: sec 1 requires a build carrying the repaired `src/cli/cmd_gps.cpp`, repaired in the same session -- a stock GPS printed the visible-record COUNT for both cases, indistinguishable from "you are on the last row". NOT CLAIMED: that removing keys at DELETE is wrong, that `SET DELETED` is broken, anything about PACK, or that the best-effort reindex fails in practice -- only that the contract permits it and the state is now observable. A repository-wide citation grep was rejected because tests and range examples intentionally contain upper-bound sentinel identifiers. Owner `member.derald`; steward `member.ai.codex`. **RECURRED 2026-08-30, A THIRD TIME.** `claim-aif` minted AIF-043 again for run `COWORK-20260830-001`. **The alignment described in this row is NOT PRESENT in the development tree at `a83d8f9e2`** -- there is no `--backfill-existing` flag and `claim_aif` still walks `range(AIF_LO, AIF_HI + 1)` taking the LOWEST FREE GAP rather than max+1, so historical holes are the first thing it hands out. This row's own status reads "implementation verified locally; review before commit", which is consistent with the fix never having been committed. The claim was released with `release-aif` and AIF-149 taken explicitly via `--number`. Note also that the AIF-collision gate had been printing `AIF-043` as a standing advisory on every commit that day -- **a live warning about the exact number about to be requested, read as backlog noise.** **CAUSE CORRECTED 2026-08-30, SAME DAY: THE SENTENCE ABOVE BLAMED THE LOWEST-GAP RULE AND THAT WAS ONLY THE AMPLIFIER.** Measured after it was written, same repository and same minute, two interpreters: on Linux `git_committed_aifs` returned 154 entries and the lowest free number was 150; on Windows via `$py12` it returned **ZERO** and the lowest free number was 043. `git_committed_aifs` is the ONLY source carrying a number that is CITED BUT NOT ROWED -- `working_tree_aifs` is row-anchored and AIF-043 has no row, `claimed_aifs` had no file for it -- so when that scan dies the universe loses its only sight of 043. It died because `subprocess.check_output(..., text=True)` decodes with the LOCALE codec: UTF-8 on Linux, cp1252 on Windows, and one byte in `docs/` (0x81 at offset 338792) raised `UnicodeDecodeError` straight into `except Exception: return set()`. **A SILENT, PLATFORM-DEPENDENT COLLAPSE OF AN ALLOCATOR'S AUTHORITY, INDISTINGUISHABLE FROM AN EMPTY CORPUS** -- the AIF-118 shape inside the tool whose entire job is preventing collisions. FIXED: explicit `encoding="utf-8", errors="replace"`, and the failure is no longer swallowed (exit 1 is an honest empty; exit 128 or a missing git is an honest empty that is now ANNOUNCED; anything else raises). Verified on the interpreter that failed: 154 entries, 43 present, lowest free 150. **THE ALLOCATION RULE IS UNCHANGED AND STILL NEEDS THIS LANE'S RULING** -- `max+1` over THIS universe yields a number past one million, because `git_committed_aifs` greps all of `docs/` and R126's allocator-range examples put its six-digit range sentinel in scope. The two tools do not share a universe, so they are not interchangeable. |

| AIF-017 | Pinocchio stress-test lane, 2026-07-15 | proof_candidate, workflow_pattern, performance_benchmark | new lane `dottalkpp/{scripts,data/scripts}/pinocchio`; `docs/maintenance/PINOCCHIO_STRESS_TEST_PLAN_V1.md` | `docs/maintenance/PINOCCHIO_STRESS_TEST_PLAN_V1.md` | promoted -- Phase 1 PASSED at 1M/5.5M | **Engine stress-test lane — "can the educational DB be a real boy?"** Phase 1 = performance at real-world scale (~1M students / ~5–8M enrollments). Path: generate seeded MCC-shaped CSVs (canary `50000000 Taylor Quinn`) → `CREATE X64` (advanced field types, `SID I`) + `IMPORT` (maintainer-confirmed mature) → `CDX` + `BUILDLMDB MAPSIZE nG` → timed read-only battery (`SET TIMER ON`). `AUTODBF` deliberately off the critical path (needs more testing). Own disposable lane; never the MCC foundation. Phase 1 PASSED at 1M/5.5M: correct COUNTs, canary first by SID, SEEK lands on parent (501) and child (2749), LNAME order, fresh indexes, no MDB_MAP_FULL. Key fix: `SID I` truncated the 8-digit key (`I(4)`), switched to `N(8)`. **(Root-caused + fixed 2026-07-19 by FIELDTYPE M1 — `I` is now a real 4-byte int32 (±2.1 B), so a compact `SID I(4)` key is viable again instead of the `N(8)` workaround; see AIF-030.)** Timing (Phase 1.1 DONE, captured via top-level REPL + SET ALTERNATE): `TOP` is O(n) and owns ~115s of a ~116s battery (19.5s/23.2s @1M, 72.2s @5.5M) while SEEK 3-18ms, COUNT ~1.5ms, SMARTLIST 50-350ms. `TOP` positioning logged as a 4th runtime finding — biggest at-scale win. (Nested SET TIMER under DOTSCRIPT never fired = finding 3.) Three runtime findings logged to `CONTRACT_INTAKE_QUEUE_V1.md` (I wide-key truncation; SMARTLIST NEXT restarts from top after SEEK; SET TIMER not per-command when nested under DOTSCRIPT). Remaining: per-command timings (Phase 1.1, needs REPL) and Phase 2 fault injection. See `SESSION_CLOSEOUT_PINOCCHIO_PHASE1_2026-07-15.md`. Phase 2 (fault injection: concurrency, crash/recovery, durability — the Unverified areas) defined and deferred. **Phase-1 follow-up — RESOLVED (dev) 2026-07-18:** the `TOP` O(n) finding is fixed — CDX/LMDB ordered `TOP`/`BOTTOM`/`SKIP`/`GO`/`GOTO` now read the LMDB index cursor instead of scanning+sorting the DBF (root cause: `IndexFormat::CDX` fell through the CNX DB-scan path). `TOP` 66s → ~1ms @5.5M ENROLL; large-N `SKIP` 87s → 21ms; correctness canaries green. Dev-only, not promoted. See `SESSION_CLOSEOUT_PINOCCHIO_NAV_PERFORMANCE_2026-07-18.md`. **Phase 1.3 scale-verb hardening — RESOLVED (dev) 2026-07-18/19:** the scale sweep's remaining O(n) verbs were fixed — `SEEK` now uses the LMDB keyed range-seek (57s → ms; `SESSION_CLOSEOUT_PINOCCHIO_SEEK_FIX_2026-07-18.md`, companion `SET FILTER` string-literal normalize), `DELETE FOR`/`ALL` + `RECALL ALL` under an active order use a chunked bulk write-transaction (65.4s → 37.8s; `SESSION_CLOSEOUT_PINOCCHIO_DELETE_BATCH_2026-07-19.md`), and `AGGS ALL` collapses COUNT/SUM/AVG/MIN/MAX to one scan (~4×; `SESSION_CLOSEOUT_PINOCCHIO_AGGS_ALL_2026-07-19.md`). All hash-bound, dev-only, not promoted. **Phase 2 durability slice — DELIVERED (dev) 2026-07-19:** the crash/recovery + durability portion of the deferred fault-injection phase is now covered by the table-buffer write-ahead log — see AIF-023. Concurrency fault injection remains deferred. |

| AIF-018 | Messaging Normalization lane, 2026-07-15 | workflow_pattern, integration, normalization, i18n | `docs/maintenance/MESSAGING_NORMALIZATION_LANE_PLAN_V1.md`; `src/cli/command_output.*`; `message_catalog.*`; `LOCALE_PHASE23*` | `docs/maintenance/MESSAGING_NORMALIZATION_LANE_PLAN_V1.md` | active -- lane opened, wiring pending | **Registry-wide output-normalization lane; messaging is the delivery vehicle for finishing the `cmd_` list.** The localized, severity-aware channel already exists (`cli::cmdout` + `message_catalog` + Phase 23 locale spine) but is ~half-adopted: 88/184 `cmd_*.cpp` route through `cmdout`, 98 raw `std::cout`, 0 use severity-coded `print_error/print_warning`, and `cmdout` is not yet wired to `set_last_error`. The integration insight: routing a command's diagnostics through `cmdout::print_error(cmd, code)` pays four dividends at once — regional language (catalog), severity (`xbase::error::code`), `SET ERRORSTOP` for free (via `set_last_error`), and consistent `cmd:` formatting. **Order is load-bearing:** wire `print_error/print_warning` → `set_last_error` FIRST, then send the agent through the registry so each converted command becomes ERRORSTOP-aware immediately. Per-command definition-of-done and the boundary (localize diagnostics, NOT tabular payload — `LIST`/`DUMP`/`SMARTLIST` stay on the data channel) in the lane plan. Acceptance triple per command: forced failure prints the localized message, sets `ERROR_STATUS`, and trips `SET ERRORSTOP ON`. Pairs with the ERRORSTOP and messaging rows in `CONTRACT_INTAKE_QUEUE_V1.md`. Edits source (rides dev→staging→GitHub + source-mutation gate), unlike Pinocchio's disposable data lane. |

| AIF-021 | Claude Cowork report `AIPR-20260716-001`; Codex corrective audit `AIPR-20260716-002`, 2026-07-16 | drift_or_risk, workflow_pattern, agent_instruction, proof_correction | AIF-018 Messaging Normalization; AI Portal closeout and local-access gates | `docs/maintenance/SESSION_CLOSEOUT_CLAUDE_MESSAGING_CORRECTIVE_AUDIT_2026-07-16.md`; `docs/agents/HANDOFF_CLAUDE_MESSAGING_CORRECTIVE_AUDIT_2026-07-16.md`; `labtalk/proofs/runs/20260716_regression_language_corrective_audit_v1.txt` | anchored — constructive transgression recorded; safe corrections applied; one error-code decision remains review-needed | **Audit-envelope success is not whole-work compliance.** The implementation built and the locale canary rendered its target text, but the session crossed the result/message boundary by cataloguing `INDEXSEEK(): <recno>`, skipped the mandatory source-mutation closeout record and dashboard Session Log row, omitted the DotScript readiness block and retained transcript, understated the actual diff, listed a non-retained scratch tool as a development change, and called surfaces ERRORSTOP-ready before any `print_error` adoption. Corrective action restored all five INDEXSEEK result emissions to the data channel, removed the unused result identity after confirming it was uncommitted/unpromoted and absent from the active DBF catalog, completed the missing AI-facing records and readiness block, retained and reviewed a full corrected-binary launcher transcript, and amended the original closeout. Converting comments to `*` is retained as cross-path hardening, not a correction of Claude's valid reading of `DOTSCRIPT USAGE`; the initial Codex attribution is corrected under AIF-022. The proof also distinguishes compiled validation (1323 messages / 2599 text rows) from the unsynchronized active DBF provider (1006 / 1270); no DBF/CDX/LMDB writeback was authorized. `DOTSCRIPT OUT` did not capture every `cmdout` line and is not accepted alone for this proof. The remaining `EXPORTFUNCTIONS` failure must receive a maintainer-reviewed canonical I/O error code before error-state/ERRORSTOP claims. | AIF-022 | Claude audit response + Codex two-path proof, 2026-07-16 | drift_or_risk, help_runtime_drift, proof_correction, workflow_pattern | DotScript execution semantics; CLI top-level launcher; REGRESSION proof lane | `docs/maintenance/DOTSCRIPT_COMMENT_PREFIX_EXECUTION_PATH_DRIFT_V1.md`; `src/cli/cmd_dotscript.cpp`; `src/cli/init_script_runner.cpp`; `src/cli/script_reader.cpp`; `labtalk/proofs/runs/20260716_dotscript_comment_prefix_path_drift_v1.txt` | anchored — runtime-proven split; source correction decision pending | **Semicolon comment handling differs by script entry path.** `DOTSCRIPT USAGE`, `cmd_DOTSCRIPT`, and `REGRESSION -> cmd_DOTSCRIPT` agree that trimmed `;` lines are comments. Top-level `dottalkpp.exe --script`, used by launcher/bridge flows, sends the same line through `init_script_runner` without that filter and emits `Unknown command: ;`; both paths still exit 0. The initial Codex audit wrongly attributed the direct-launch failure to Claude even though Claude followed valid HELP for the intended regression path; AIF-021 and the handoff are amended accordingly. `*` remains the cross-path-safe authoring convention. | AIF-023 | Table-buffer WAL/durability session `AIPR-20260719-003`, 2026-07-19 | proof_candidate, durability, workflow_pattern, crash_recovery | DotTalk++ table-buffer transaction path; Pinocchio Phase 2 durability (AIF-017) | `docs/maintenance/SESSION_CLOSEOUT_TABLE_BUFFER_WAL_2026-07-19.md`; `docs/maintenance/TABLE_BUFFER_WAL_DESIGN_2026-07-19.md` | anchored — runtime-proven dev-only; not promoted | **Durable, crash-recoverable table-buffer transactions.** `COMMIT`/`ROLLBACK` were RAM-only (buffer applied straight to the DBF with no log and no `fsync`), so a crash mid-commit could leave the durable LMDB index ahead of the data. Added a text `.tbj` sidecar redo log next to the DBF with the standard redo-WAL protocol: append `U`/`D` records during buffered writes, write a `C <count>` marker and **`fsync` before** the DBF apply loop, delete the log after apply; `ROLLBACK` discards it; recovery-on-open (`USE`) replays a `C`-marked log (idempotent) and discards an unmarked one. Three teed-transcript phases: A durable writer (`938D4EC3…`), B recovery-on-open (`16B938E4…`), C buffered `DELETE` routed through the log (`FBA1FC92…`). **Preservation constraint honored:** multiple-retained-edits-per-field is kept — every buffered write is one full-fidelity record carrying its `<priority>` and `H`/`S` mode, not a last-write-wins snapshot; added the missing `TABLE BUFFER HISTORY ON\|OFF` command. Enabled only under `TABLE BUFFER PERSISTENT` (RamJournal); default RamOnly COMMIT/ROLLBACK is byte-for-byte unchanged; the Phase 1.3d write-through batched-index `DELETE` is unregressed (buffered path forks before it). **Scoped gain, not full ACID** (`acid-and-glass-box` remains Unverified). Open hardening: DBF `fsync` after replay/commit (fstream doesn't expose the OS handle portably), and CDX/LMDB index reconciliation after a buffered commit/recovery (needs a `REINDEX`). Dev-only in `D:\code\ccode`; requires a maintainer rebuild + proof rerun before any promotion. |

| AIF-024 | Maintainer directive, 2026-07-19 | workflow_pattern, agent_instruction, drift_or_risk | `AI_PORTAL.md` (companion to AIF-006); session-closeout convention (AIF-008) | `AI_PORTAL.md` -> "Document As You Work (AIF-024)" | anchored — source-defined | **Document-as-you-work gate.** Codifies that documentation is written continuously as material steps land, not reconstructed at closeout. A step is material when it changes source/data/an AI-facing doc, produces a build/proof/hash/measured number, or makes a constraining decision or finding — recorded then in the running closeout/progress log, an intake or contract row, or a hash-bound proof transcript. Evidence not captured at the moment it is produced is treated as not proven. Complements AIF-006 (which fixes *what* to update at closeout) by fixing *when* (throughout). Precedent: the DOCFLUSH living-progress record ("future gates must be recorded while work proceeds") and the AIF-021 corrective audit (deferred records → understated diff, missing Session Log row, premature readiness claims). Adds no new artifact; reuses the AIF-006 / session-closeout destinations. Not yet runtime-enforced (no automated check); a process gate like AIF-006. |

| AIF-026 | Engine limits session `AIPR-20260719-005`, 2026-07-19 | build_boundary, proof_candidate, workflow_pattern | DotTalk++ runtime limits; `dottalkpp/data/scripts/limits/` LIMITS canaries | `docs/maintenance/SESSION_CLOSEOUT_ENGINE_LIMITS_AND_CORRECTIONS_2026-07-19.md` | runtime-proven (dev); not committed/promoted | **Engine limit raise + two shakedown-found fixes.** `MAX_AREA` 256→512; x64 table/field name ceilings 128→256 (defaults held at 128, `x64_*_name_fits` repointed at the ceiling); explicit record-size guardrails — 16 MiB hard ceiling (`X64_MAX_RECORD_SIZE`, enforced in `dbf_create` create + `dbf_file` open buffer) and 64 KiB soft advisory (`print_note` on `CREATE`). Two corrections the first shakedown surfaced: the name-too-long warning displayed the effective default (128) not the ceiling (256) — fixed; and `CLOSE ALL` closed only the current work area, stranding files — fixed to close every open area (`close_one_area` over `shell_engine()` slots, `filename()` open-truth). Built green; four teed canaries prove each (`tmp/limits/*.log`): `SELECT 512` bounded; 130-char name authoritative + 260-char warning now shows 256; 64 KiB advisory warns-not-blocks; `CLOSE ALL` erases all areas cleanly. Honest reach: 16 MiB hard ceiling unreachable via `CREATE` (parser caps X64 `C` at 4096) — backstop needs a unit test; `DOTSCRIPT TRACE` ignores `;` continuation (interactive honors it). RECNO64 end-to-end 64-bit widening is a separate lane (M1-M4 now implemented — see AIF-027). **Follow-up (2026-07-20, dev):** `MAX_FIELDS` also raised 128→256 (`include/xbase.hpp`; only two guard checks reference it and the table-buffer `kMaxFields` bitsets were already 256, so it just removes an inconsistency) — build pending. These raises plus the RECNO64 widening status were documented on x64base.com, dev-stage framed (source `9d3cd1f6` published the RECNO64 pages; the MAX_AREA/MAX_FIELDS/name-ceiling/16-MiB updates are staged locally pending a re-publish). The ceilings are noted as deliberately round engineering choices centralized in `include/xbase.hpp`, with a future **configuration layer** (SAP-style profile parameters or an `xbase.meta` descriptor) as the intended home for `MAX_RECORDS`/`MAX_RECORD_SIZE`/`MAX_FIELDS`/`MAX_AREAS`. Uncommitted in `D:/code/ccode`. |

| AIF-028 | RECNO64 M3 buffer-proof run, 2026-07-19 | drift_or_risk, source_test, field_type_coercion | DotTalk++ x64 field-value coercion (`cmd_replace`); x64 field-type codecs (`I` integer, date) | `src/cli/cmd_replace.cpp`; `dottalkpp/data/scripts/suites/table_buffer.dts` (run transcript); cf. AIF-017 `SID I` truncation | review-needed — dev-observed, **not** an M3/RECNO64 regression | **Two x64 field-type value-handling defects surfaced during the RECNO64 M3b buffer proof (orthogonal to record-number width).** Running `suites\table_buffer.dts` on x64 STUDENTS, every **date** REPLACE failed with `REPLACE: invalid date for field.` — `REPLACE DOB WITH 19560214`, `REPLACE ENROLL_D WITH 20240923`, and blank `REPLACE DOB WITH "        "` — while the **string** REPLACEs (FNAME/LNAME) succeeded through the identical buffered `add_change` path and COMMIT applied them physically. So the fault is field-value coercion in `cmd_replace`, **upstream** of the (now 64-bit) buffer key, not a RECNO64 regression. Review item (1): x64 date-field REPLACE rejects an 8-digit numeric date literal and a blank/space literal — pin the accepted input form for x64 date fields (numeric `19560214` vs quoted `"YYYYMMDD"` vs blank-clear) and reconcile the coercion with the x32 path. Review item (2) — **x64 integer type**, per maintainer request, look at the same time: the x64 `I` integer field's width/range/coercion needs review across CREATE + REPLACE + IMPORT; precedent AIF-017 found `SID I` (4-byte `I`) truncated an 8-digit key and forced a switch to `N(8)`. Both are dev-observed only; no source change yet. RECNO64 M3 itself proved green (see AIF-027). **Update (2026-07-19, dev): review item (2) and half of item (1) RESOLVED by FIELDTYPE M1 (AIF-030).** Root cause of both the `I` truncation *and* the date-numeric rejection was one upstream defect: `cmd_replace` formatted the numeric RHS through a `std::ostringstream` without imbuing the classic locale, so the process locale injected thousands separators (`50000000`→`"50,000,000"`, `19560214`→`"19,560,214"`) — which then failed `parse_i32_strict` and `YYYYMMDD` parsing respectively. Fixed (integral→`std::to_string`, fractional→`std::locale::classic()`); `REPLACE SID WITH 50000000` now round-trips (proven), and the 8-digit numeric date literal now parses. **RESOLVED by FIELDTYPE M3 (2026-07-19, dev):** blank/space date literal now **clears** the field (`normalize_date_value` blank guard), and validation now **canonicalizes the stored value** (was computing the normalized `YYYYMMDD`/`T`-`F` form and discarding it, storing raw text truncated to field width) — `validate_field_value_for_store` writes the canonical form back. Proven live: `REPLACE DOB WITH 19560214`⇒`19560214`, `REPLACE DOB WITH "        "`⇒blank, `REPLACE FLAG WITH TRUE`⇒`T`. Canonical stored date is **date8** (maintainer: "normally stored date value is date8 or an 8-digit char field from the .32 days"). Unquoted slashed dates (`02/14/1956`) are read as arithmetic division by the expression evaluator (expected; date8 is the norm); quoted `"02/14/1956"` canonicalizes to `19560214`. AIF-028 fully retired. |
| AIF-029 | Maintainer design discussion during the RECNO64/limits documentation, 2026-07-20 | architecture_plan, configuration, workflow_pattern | New "engine limits configuration layer" lane; `include/xbase.hpp` constants; future `xbase.meta` descriptor | `docs/maintenance/RECNO64_END_TO_END_64BIT_ADDRESSING_LANE_V1.md`; AIF-026; x64base.com `x64-capacity-math` "Where these limits live" note | design/intake — proposed, not started | **Engine-limit configuration layer / `xbase.meta`.** The engine's hard ceilings — `MAX_AREA`, `MAX_FIELDS`, `X64_MAX_RECORD_SIZE`, the x64 name-length ceilings, and the conceptual `MAX_RECORDS` — are **deliberately-round engineering choices, to some degree arbitrary**, currently centralized as compile-time `constexpr` constants in `include/xbase.hpp` (plus the table-buffer `kMaxFields` bitset width in `include/cli/table_state.hpp`). Raising one is a single-point edit + rebuild. Maintainer's design direction: give these a real home — a **configuration layer** in the style of enterprise systems (SAP-style profile parameters) and/or a per-database **`xbase.meta`** descriptor — so a deployment can set its own ceilings (`MAX_RECORDS` / `MAX_RECORD_SIZE` / `MAX_FIELDS` / `MAX_AREAS`) without a recompile. Open scope decisions: which limits stay compile-time vs become runtime-tunable vs per-database; where `xbase.meta` lives (sidecar file vs DBF_64 header extension); how validation/guardrails interact (e.g., the 16 MiB record-size hard ceiling should remain a safety backstop even if the soft ceiling is configurable); and versioning/migration. Already surfaced (dev-stage) on the x64base.com capacity page. Not started; no source yet beyond the existing constants. |
| AIF-030 | Field-types investigation, 2026-07-20 | architecture_plan, drift_or_risk, source_test, proof_candidate | New FIELDTYPE codec lane; `record_view.cpp` load/store seams; `include/xbase/field_codec.hpp` | `docs/maintenance/FIELD_TYPE_CODEC_LANE_V1.md`; `src/xbase/record_view.cpp:142,188`; `src/xbase/dbf_create.cpp:562`; `src/cli/cmd_create.cpp:343`; `src/cli/cmd_replace.cpp:362,500` | **M1–M5 proven (dev; both VFP interop directions)**; not committed/promoted | **Field-type codec architecture — make binary types real + a custom-type model.** Investigation from the trinity headers found the engine stores **every field as fixed-width text** (`loadFieldsFromBuffer`/`storeFieldsToBuffer`) — no binary encode/decode. So the VFP binary types are a façade: `I` is a 4-byte field holding **4 ASCII chars (max 9999)** — the confirmed root cause of the `SID I` truncation (AIF-017, `50000000`→`5000`); `B`/`Y`/`T` are 8 ASCII chars, not IEEE-754 / int64-scaled currency / julian+time, and `T` gets no write validation. Create allows `C N F D L M` (classic) / `+I B Y T` (VFP/X64) and sizes I=4/B=8/Y=8/T=8, but nothing encodes them. Design (per maintainer directive that field-type handling **be the model for custom field types**): a **codec registry keyed by type char** — `Codec{decode,encode,name}`, `codec_for(type)`, `register_codec(char, Codec)` — dispatched at the two storage seams; built-in and custom types are the same object. Milestones: M1 codec seam + text default (byte-for-byte unchanged for C/N/F/D/L/M) + `I` int32-LE codec (retires AIF-017); M2 `B`/`Y`/`T` codecs + boundary tests; M3 `D` date blank/numeric coercion (AIF-028); M4 custom field-type registration API + worked example; M5 VFP binary round-trip interop gate before any "VFP binary-compatible" website claim. **M1 PROVEN (dev, 2026-07-19):** `CREATE X64 T1 (SID I)` → `REPLACE SID WITH 50000000` → `? SID` ⇒ `50000000` (was `5000`); AIF-017 retired. Shakedown also fixed an upstream locale thousands-separator defect in `cmd_replace` numeric formatting (see AIF-028 update, AIF-031) that had masked the codec and blocked date-numeric literals. **M2 PROVEN (dev, 2026-07-19):** `B` (8-byte IEEE-754), `Y` (8-byte int64 scaled 10⁴, exact decimal parse), `T` (8-byte JDN+ms datetime, `YYYYMMDDHHMMSS`) codecs + the missing `T` write-validation; `src/tests/test_field_codec.cpp` (`dottalkpp_field_codec_test` 1/1) round-trips all four at boundaries and anchors the date math to a known JDN (1970-01-01 = 2440588). Test also caught + fixed an M1 `i32_encode` strictness gap (accepted `"12x"`). **M4 PROVEN (dev, 2026-07-19):** demonstrative custom type **`X` = pronoun** (depth-8 stack of pronoun-set codes ↔ `"she/her; they/them"`) end-to-end — `CREATE X64 PEOPLE (NAME C(12), PN X)` → `REPLACE PN WITH "she/her; they/them"` → `? PN` round-trips; `"THEY/THEM"`→`they/them`; `"foo/bar"`→clean rejection; `STRUCT` shows `X 8`. REPLACE validates *through the codec itself*; `register_codec('~')` unit test proves runtime registration is the extension point. **M4 exposed the friction the maintainer flagged**: adding one type touched **5** hardcoded gates (`kTypeIndex` catalog, ×2 `supports_type_now`, `cmd_create` width, `cmd_replace` validation) — target of **M4b** (single-registration model: registry-fed validation chain, then delete the 5 edits and re-prove). **M4b PROVEN (dev, 2026-07-19):** the registry now carries per-code metadata (`register_field_type(type, Codec, {fixed_width, formats, display_name})`); the CREATE chain (`cmd_create` validity+width) and `cmd_replace` (generic `default` arm validates any registered type via its codec) consult it; the **five hardcoded `X` gates were deleted** (catalog, ×2 `supports_type_now`, create-width, replace `case 'X'`). `X` now lives in ONE file (`field_codec.cpp`) and still works end-to-end live (`CREATE X64 PEOPLE (... PN X)` → REPLACE/`? PN` round-trip → `foo/bar` rejected → `STRUCT` X 8). A new custom type = one `register_field_type` call, zero switch edits. **M5 read-direction PROVEN (dev, 2026-07-19):** `CREATE VFP` writes a genuine VFP file (v0x30 + 263-byte DBC backlink), and an **independent spec-based reader** (`src/tests/vfp_field_interop.py`, shares no engine code) decoded every binary field exactly — `I`=50000000, `B`=3.140625, `Y`=1234.5678, `T`=2000-01-01 12:00 (+ INT32_MIN / -99.9999 / 1970 row). VFP datetime epoch matches (`JDN(2000-01-01)`=2451545). Fixture `dottalkpp/data/scripts/fieldtype/vfp_types_make.dts`. Gate caught + fixed a **precision** defect (see AIF-031). **Reverse direction also PROVEN (dev, 2026-07-19):** the engine `USE`d an externally-authored VFP file (`EXTVFP.dbf`, spec header + backlink, written outside the engine) and read every binary field exactly (`I`=424242, `B`=2.71875, `Y`=777.7777, `T`=2024-07-04 09:00). Round-trip holds both ways. Optional remainder (non-code): capacity/website spec update before any public "VFP binary-compatible" claim. Dev-only; uncommitted in `D:/code/ccode`. |
| AIF-031 | FIELDTYPE M1 shakedown, 2026-07-19 | drift_or_risk, source_test, i18n | numeric-formatting audit across CLI commands; `cmd_replace.cpp` (fixed) | `src/cli/cmd_replace.cpp` (K_Number RHS); precedent `src/cli/expr/rhs_eval.cpp` `scalar_to_string` (imbues `std::locale::classic()`) | **fixed in `cmd_replace`; sweep pending** | **Locale thousands-separator hazard in engine-internal numeric formatting.** A `std::ostringstream` that formats an engine-internal numeric value **without** `imbue(std::locale::classic())` inherits the process/global locale and can inject grouping separators (`50000000`→`"50,000,000"`), corrupting values that must stay in canonical "C" form for parsing/storage. Found and fixed in `cmd_replace`'s REPLACE RHS path (masked the `I` codec + blocked numeric date literals — AIF-028); `rhs_eval.cpp` `scalar_to_string` already guards against exactly this with an explicit comment, and `cmd_calcwrite` was already type-aware. **Follow-up: sweep the CLI/engine for other `ostringstream`/`to_string`-adjacent numeric formatters that lack the classic-locale guard** (candidates: any command emitting stored/keyed numeric text — REPLACE MULTI, CALC/computed writes, index key encoders, export/DUMP numeric columns). Prefer `std::to_string` (locale-independent) for integral canonical text, or `imbue(classic())` for streams. **Related precision defect (found + fixed via M5 VFP interop, 2026-07-19):** the *fractional* K_Number path in `cmd_replace` formatted with the default ostream precision (6 significant figures), silently truncating B/Y REPLACE values with more precision (`3.140625`→`"3.14062"`, `1234.5678`→`"1234.57"`) and corrupting the stored double/currency — an independent VFP reader caught it. Fixed with `std::to_chars(..., std::chars_format::fixed)` (shortest round-trip, exact, locale-independent). The pending sweep should also flag fixed-precision numeric formatters that can drop significant digits, not only locale-grouping ones. **SWEEP DONE (dev, 2026-07-19):** root cause confirmed — `runtime/utf8_init.hpp` sets `std::locale::global(std::locale(""))` at startup (for UTF-8), so on a US/EU host every un-imbued numeric stream groups thousands. Audited the value/expression/storage/`STR()`-family formatters (display-to-terminal left as-is) and added `imbue(std::locale::classic())` to eight that were exposed: `expr/fn_numeric.cpp` (`STR`-family `format_number`), `xexpr/expr_value.cpp` (`Value::display`, both int/frac paths), `row_codec_fixed.cpp` (fixed-row `cell_to_string` — also `setprecision(15)` so a large double no longer becomes `"5e+07"` before `digits_only` — and `NumericFixed`), `cmd_calcwrite.cpp` (`to_string_trim`, computed CALCWRITE→stored), `expr/fn_string.cpp` (`STR(n,w,d)`), `shell_api.cpp` (`format_eval_number`), `cmd_calc.cpp` + `edu/edu_formula.cpp` (`format_number`). Nine files already imbued classic (the reference pattern). **date8 addendum:** the same grouping locale also corrupts date8/year integer formatting — fixed three more sites the numeric pass missed: `row_codec_fixed.cpp` ×2 (build `YYYYMMDD` by streaming the year int → `1956`→`"1,956"`→`"1,9560214"`) and `edu/edu_formula.cpp` (`std::cout << date8` int → `"19,560,214"`). Confirmed safe (untouched): `date_utils.cpp` (imbues its year/time building streams; concatenates strings), `cmd_calcwrite::format_date8_from_number` + `expr_value` Date case + `cmd_calc` date print (all `std::to_string`). Dev-only, build-pending; these are post-promotion changes not yet re-synced to `C:\x64base`. |

| AIF-036 | DotScript stop_on_error, 2026-07-20 | feature_request, runtime_behavior, source_change | `include/xbase_error_context.hpp`; `include/xbase_error_codes.hpp`; `src/cli/command_output.{hpp,cpp}`; `src/cli/cmd_stop_on_error.cpp`; `src/cli/cmd_set.cpp`; `src/cli/init_script_runner.cpp`; `src/cli/cmd_dotscript.cpp`; `src/cli/shell_commands.*`; `include/dotref.hpp` | `docs/maintenance/DOTSCRIPT_STOP_ON_ERROR_LANE_V1.md` | **`stop_on_error[severity]` flag -- implemented dev-only, build+prove pending.** Maintainer-requested DotScript flag to abort a running script when an error at/above a severity threshold is recorded; "errors derive from messaging." Finishes the planned **SET ERRORSTOP** hook (AIF-018/AIF-021) as a severity threshold. Surface: native `STOP_ON_ERROR [OFF\|WARNING\|ERROR]` command + `SET ERRORSTOP [TO] <sev>` alias + `DOTTALK_ERRORSTOP` env default. Mechanism: a thread-local `errorstop_level` + an error **generation counter** (so the sticky thread-local last-error can't false-trip); a run aborts when a *new* `set_last_error` at/above the threshold is recorded on a line -- keyed on the severity carried by the messaging-recorded canonical error code (`emit_error`->`set_last_error`), not an ad-hoc flag. Hooks in both script loops (`run_script_file`, DOTSCRIPT); REGRESSION spec-runner needs none. Self-contained proof `dottalkpp/data/scripts/errorstop/stop_on_error_regression.dts` (OFF continues past an error; ERROR aborts). Not built here (no MSVC); STOP_ON_ERROR status lines use `print_info` pending a localized-`MessageId` fast-follow (severity decision already derives from messaging). Foundation under the DotScript-array spec H3. Dev-only, uncommitted, not promoted. |

Extends the authority chain to "source teaches." Enforced as a standard: duplication a review would flag is a *teaching defect*; unavoidable shortcuts must be explicitly labeled non-exemplary with a tracked follow-up. Recorded as a portal doctrine section (sibling of AIF-006/AIF-024). **First application — DONE (2026-07-20):** consolidated the six drifting comment/line-lexing helpers (`begins_with_comment` ×3 in shell.cpp/shell_api.cpp/cmd_init.cpp, `looks_like_comment_or_blank` in cmd_dotscript.cpp, `strip_hash_comment` ×2 in shell.cpp/script_reader.cpp) into one canonical module `src/cli/dotscript_lexing.{hpp,cpp}` (`strip_inline_comment` / `is_comment_line` / `is_comment_or_blank`); all six are now thin delegates. Vocabulary (also AIF-036): full-line `*`/`REM` canonical + `#`/`//` tolerated; inline `&&`/`#` (single `&` macro preserved); `;` continuation. **Proven** by a standalone unit compile (`g++` on the self-contained module) — all assertions pass incl. quoted-token and single-`&` cases — plus an engine-level regression `dottalkpp/data/scripts/lexing/comment_handling_regression.dts`. New files need a CMake reconfigure; dev-only, not promoted. |

Immediate target: `MAX_FIELDS`/`MAX_AREA`/`MAX_ROWS` + x64 name vectors. **GATE #1: preserve 512** (adopting the inert 256/128 would silently narrow capacity). Case-ghost + promotion-sensitive. | AIF-046 | Scan-evaluator optimization session `AIPR-20260722-007`, 2026-07-22 | performance, source_change, proof_candidate, workflow_pattern | scan/expression evaluator + core record-read path; `value_eval`/`scan_selector`/`glue_xbase`/`record_view`/`cmd_aggs`; `PHASE0_DECODE_COST` benchmark | `docs/maintenance/SESSION_CLOSEOUT_SCAN_EVALUATOR_LANE_2026-07-22.md`; `src/AIPortal/sessions/2026-07-21_claude_recno64_indextxn_onboarding/PROJECT_LANE_SCAN_EVALUATOR_OPTIMIZATION_V1_20260722.md` | **M0–M4 complete, committed dev-only; mirror has M0 only; not promoted/pushed** — parent `project.x64base.runtime` | **Scan/expression-evaluator optimization — the redirect from the AIF-043 Ticket B Phase-0 KILL.** Phase-0 proved the in-memory *store* was not the bottleneck; the **per-row expression evaluator** was (~1000× slower than a fixed-width scan). This lane fixed it in Option A, no tuple-core changes. **M0** — reliable in-script timing (`SECONDS()` → ms via `std::chrono`; `SET TIMER` moved into the canonical `shell_execute_line` so scripts self-time) + a self-timed baseline benchmark registered as `PHASE0_DECODE_COST` (exempt from `REGRESSION ALL`); floor DEC1 ~38.5s / DEC3 ~70.5s over 1M pinocchio STUDENTS; committed `eba9a7012`, benchmark page published to the site. **M3** (done first — per-row recompile dominated) — `compile_bool_predicate`/`eval_bool_compiled` compile the FOR predicate once (AST + reusable live `RecordView`), evaluated per row in `collect_selected_recnos`; parity-safe no-op-preprocessing/non-`$`-bridge gate, else per-row `eval_bool` fallback. **M1** — per-view field-name→index cache in `make_record_view`. **M2** — root finding: `readCurrent()` eagerly decodes *every* field to `std::string` each row; added additive `DbArea::readCurrentRaw()`/`decodeFieldFromBuffer()`/`fieldNumFromBuffer()` + a selective-decode record view (`make_record_view_raw`), used by the scan when a predicate is compiled and no `SET FILTER` is active — a 1-field predicate scan then beat a full-field aggregate scan; committed with M1/M3 as `0f06d1060`. **M4** — extended selective decode to `cmd_aggs` (SUM/AVG/MIN/MAX + AGGS ALL), removing the aggregate full-decode tax (within-run `SUM/DEC1` 1.078→0.999). **Result:** predicate scans ~2×–2.8× vs floor, aggregates no longer full-decode; all counts/values unchanged (`1000000`, `2.99933e6`); `REGRESSION ALL` + `SCAN_PARITY` green each milestone. Selective decode is gated (physical eval, compiled predicate, no active filter); ordered/bridge/filtered paths keep the full-decode path. **Not the ≥40× target** — the residual is now per-row record I/O (`gotoRec`+seek+read), a different subsystem chartered as the **STAGED Bulk Record-I/O lane** (`PROJECT_LANE_BULK_RECORD_IO_SCAN_V1_20260722.md`, Phase-0 go/no-go owed). Mirror `C:\x64base` promotion of M1–M4 owed (M0 promoted surgically). |

| AIF-051 | HELP DATA phantom/mis-keyed topic keys, 2026-07-23 | bug, help_system, data_quality, harvester | `src/help/helpdata_source_miner.cpp` (`command_from_file_or_usage`); surfaced by `HELP GIANT ALL` (AIF-047 M5) | `docs/maintenance/HELP_COMMAND_UX_LANE_V1.md` | **FIXED dev-only (g++-proven); MSVC rebuild + HELP DATA regenerate to confirm in-corpus.** Root cause found and repaired. Surfaced by the AIF-047 M5 exhaustive view: a family of junk topic keys carried stray mined rows instead of a real command -- `DOT\|PRINT` (a catch-all of unrelated `[SYNTAX]` lines: BETA/CODASYL/DRAWIO/DOTSCRIPT/PROJECTS/RULE/TUPEXPORT/...), plus `DOT\|PRINT *`, `DOT\|SHOW SQL`, `DOT\|SHOW PSHELL`, `DOT\|SHOW BROWSER COMMAND`, `DOT\|WITH`. **Cause:** the miner derived the topic key from generic presentation-helper C++ function names (`print_usage`, `show_sql_help`, `show_pshell_help`, `show_browser_command_usage`) and let that helper-derived name win over the file's *real* registered command. **Fix:** `command_from_file_or_usage` now trusts a function-name-derived command only when the file has no registered command context or the derived name actually matches one of the file's registered commands; otherwise it re-homes the mined block onto a real command (a registered command named by a syntax line in the block, else the file's primary command). Behaviorally proven with a focused harness: the 5 phantom cases re-home to HELP/PSHELL/BROWSER; the legit cases (SEEK, SET ORDER, USER) are unchanged. `helpdata_source_miner.cpp` passes `g++ -std=c++20 -fsyntax-only`. Acceptance: after an MSVC rebuild + HELP DATA regenerate, `HELP GIANT ALL` shows no `DOT\|PRINT*` / `DOT\|SHOW SQL` / `DOT\|WITH` keys and `CMDHELPCHK` stays clean. Residual (unchanged, expected): helper-only files not named `cmd_*` (no registry) keep the old heuristic; and a few real topics (e.g. `RECNO`) still show two near-duplicate SUMMARY lines from dotref vs command-doc wording that exact-match dedup cannot collapse. Owner: Claude/Cowork. Committed dev-only, not promoted. |

| AIF-060 | Agency model + reports: name the capacity/accountability frame the system already implements, Cowork 2026-07-25 | doctrine, teaching, identity, rbac, onboarding, reports, doc_change | `docs/ai-friendly/AGENCY_MODEL_V1.md` (new), `labtalk/lessons/student/agency_who_may_act_v0.md` (new), `labtalk/registries/{concepts,lessons,portal,proofs}.yaml`, `labtalk/ai_portal/AI_ENGINEERING_STANDARDS_SEED_V1.md` (sec 5b), `tools/reports/build_reports.py` + `docs/reports/*` | `docs/ai-friendly/AGENCY_MODEL_V1.md`; `docs/reports/REPORTS_PUBLICATION_NOTE_V1.md` | source-defined | Agency = capacity to act PLUS accountability for having acted. Four legs: identity (SYSMEMBER), authority (SYSMEMROLE->SYSROLEPERM->SYSPERM), authentication (Argon2id/AUTH), accountability (owner/committer in ai_runs.yaml). Exclusions carry the weight: capability is not agency (Ollama has no member row -- correct, not an omission); influence is not authority (ChatGPT planned the RBAC, member.derald committed it -- the attribution block exists because git records one name where the truth had three); access is not agency. The system had already IMPLEMENTED all of this and never named it, so the mechanisms read as unrelated choices and had to be rediscovered each session -- ignorance-bleed in a different coat. Also lands the human-readable reports (AI Portal / BBS boards / BBS access) generated from live DBF + registries, with per-item publication sensitivity (access report = PRIVATE, an auth-surface map). Declared gaps: no per-session identity, no token expiry, git agency serialized by convention not mechanism, no durable Decision audit trail. |
| AIF-061 | Memo/WAL atomicity: close the last store; correct the stale 'stubs' comment on a shipped WAL, Cowork 2026-07-25 | design, durability, wal, transactions, memo, doc_correction | `include/cli/table_state.hpp` (stale comments corrected), proposed: `src/cli/table_state.cpp` (M record), `src/cli/cmd_replace.cpp` (writes_memo/clears_memo_field call sites) | `docs/maintenance/AI_MEMO_WAL_ATOMICITY_LANE_V1.md` | design-intended | SURVEY CORRECTION: x64base already HAS a write-ahead log for DBF record writes -- `<dbf>.tbj`, TBJ1 format with U/D records carrying priority + H\|S retention, single fsync of a C marker BEFORE the DBF apply, idempotent replay on open. The header labelled it 'stubs ... intentionally no-op placeholders', which was stale by several milestones; a partner surveyed the header, correctly trusted it, and reported no WAL existed. Comments fixed; lesson added to the standards seed (when a placeholder becomes an implementation, the comment is part of the change). REAL GAP: an x64 memo REPLACE converts text to a stored object-id and the DBF field holds the id, so the WAL journals the id and never the content -- a crash between the durable commit and the memo write leaves a record referencing a possibly-absent object, silent inconsistency the log cannot detect. Fix is additive: `M <id> <len> <hex>` written before the referencing U record, replayed memo-first, TBJ2 version bump still accepting TBJ1. Closing it retires COMMIT's own disclaimer that it is not atomic across DBF, memo and index. Also refreshed seed section 1 (@dottalk.file coverage 22% -> 100%). |
| AIF-062 | Evidence layer versioning: blanket `*.log` was hiding the entire proof registry, Cowork 2026-07-25 | infrastructure, gitignore, evidence, traceability, correction | `.gitignore` (targeted negations), `labtalk/proofs/**` (81 artifacts), `docs/maintenance/SESSION_CLOSEOUT_*` (57), `TABLE_BUFFER_WAL_DESIGN_2026-07-19.md`, `labtalk/registries/proofs.yaml`, standards seed sec 5c | `docs/maintenance/AI_EVIDENCE_LAYER_VERSIONING_LANE_V1.md` | runtime-observed | MEASURED: 71 proof artifacts on disk / 0 tracked; 7 proofs.yaml rows citing files absent from a clone; 57 untracked closeouts vs 18 tracked. `runtime_observed` was unverifiable by anyone not at the machine. Surfaced when a partner reported no WAL existed -- the Table-Buffer WAL was built, crash-proven in three teed phases (Phase B replayed an uncommitted record after a simulated crash) and closed out 2026-07-19, but design doc, closeout and transcripts were all untracked. The partner then filed its proof at source_defined, wrong in fact: invisible evidence produces wrong records that propagate. Fixed with scoped negations (verified runtime traces stay ignored), evidence committed, proof.wal.dbf_record corrected to runtime_observed with SHAs. Rule in seed 5c: a proof row must cite a COMMITTED artifact; never blanket-ignore an extension evidence uses. Follow-on: a proofs.yaml validator (paths resolve AND are tracked), sibling of source_census --strict. docs/datadict/specs (543 files) left untracked pending a maintainer call on active-vs-superseded -- deliberately not swept in blind. |

| AIF-065 | LMDB mapsize override: BUILDLMDB's documented size ladder is not honoured, Cowork 2026-07-26 | defect, index, lmdb, storage, usage_contract, benchmark_blocker, source_followup | `docs/maintenance/LMDB_MAPSIZE_OVERRIDE_LANE_V1.md`; `labtalk/registries/lessons.yaml`; PDLC empirical lane storage caveat | `src/cli/cmd_buildlmdb.cpp` (writer, honours the ladder); `src/xindex/cdx_backend.cpp:189` and `src/xindex/lmdb_backend.cpp:80` (attach paths, hardcode 1 GiB); `tools/fullstack_docs/prune_lmdb_archives.ps1` | source_defined | MEASURED: `BUILDLMDB` documents TINY 32M / SMALL 64M / MEDIUM 128M / LARGE 256M / XL 512M / HUGE 1G plus `MAPSIZE <n>[K\|M\|G]`, parses all of it correctly, enforces the 8 MiB floor and echoes the chosen value -- then two attach-path call sites hardcode `mdb_env_set_mapsize(env_, 1 GiB)`, so an env grows to 1 GiB on first index attach whatever was requested. The whole ladder is cosmetic. Every `data.mdb` in `dottalkpp\data\lmdb` holds one of exactly two sizes -- 128 MiB (71 live) or 1 GiB (41 live) -- the two constants in the source, nothing between; lanes attached through the CDX backend are uniformly 1 GiB, lanes read by other paths uniformly 128 MiB. CONSEQUENCE: ~8x storage inflation, a 99 GB lmdb tree, and the x64base-vs-SQLite BENCHMARK STORAGE AXIS IS BLOCKED -- a 30,124-row and a 9-row table both occupy 1,073,741,824 bytes, so any footprint comparison would measure attach history. Separate but compounding: `BUILDLMDB CLEAN` archives each superseded env with NO RETENTION LIMIT (~50 GB across four lanes since 2026-06-25); this filled the disk and aborted a SRC* reload mid-backup. HONESTY BOUND: effect measured, cause INFERRED from source + bimodal distribution -- two `stat` calls around `BUILDLMDB CLEAN TINY YES` are owed before promotion to runtime_observed. Proposed fix is to DELETE both reader-side calls so LMDB adopts the meta-page mapsize, pending confirmation of that adoption rule against the linked LMDB version. Found by a disk filling, not by audit: `stack_audit_v1` and the census compare docs against tables and source against contracts, but NOTHING compares a documented option against its observable effect -- so every option in every `@dottalk.usage` block is currently an unverified claim. Lesson: `lesson.career.a_documented_option_is_not_an_honoured_option`. |
| AIF-066 | Locale spine survey: messaging mature, HELP preview fixture stranded, manuals greenfield, Cowork 2026-07-27 | survey, locale, i18n, messaging, help, manualgen, drift_detection, source_followup | `docs/maintenance/LOCALE_SPINE_STATE_AND_HELP_PREVIEW_DRIFT_LANE_V1.md`; companion policy `docs/maintenance/LANGUAGE_REGION_DOCUMENTATION_BOUNDARY_v1.md` | `src/cli/cmdhelp.cpp` (locale-preview contract + reader, lines 1434/1602/1820/1851); `src/cli/cmd_set.cpp:599,679`; `dottalkpp/data/locale/SYSTEM_LOCALE*.dbf`; `dottalkpp/data/messaging/SYSTEM_MESSAGE*.dbf`; `dottalkpp/data/help/HELP_*_LOCALE.dbf`; `tools/manualgen/manual_assembly_manifest.yaml:205-209` | source_defined | TWO locale efforts, not one. MESSAGING SPINE IS MATURE and matches doctrine: 5 locales, 1006 messages, 1270 text rows = en-US 1006 plus 66 each in de/es/fr/it -- 6.6% coverage, exactly the boundary note's Priority 2 "essential operator messages first". HELP LOCALE PREVIEW IS A LIVE MECHANISM, NOT ABANDONED: `cmdhelp.cpp` carries `@dottalk.locale-preview-contract v1 PHASE23T`, `CMDHELP <topic> PREVIEW LOCALE <locale>` is explicit-only, DRAFT_PLACEHOLDER/NEEDS_REVIEW fall back to source by design, and `help_guard_v1` watches the companions. Its fixture is 25/25/75/25 rows = 5 topics x 5 locales (AREA, ABOUT, CMDHELP, SET LANGUAGE, SET LOCALE -- the machinery documenting itself) with `[es draft] AREA` placeholders; a placeholder that falls back correctly IS the test, so reading it as unfinished translation is a mistake. CONFIRMED DEFECT: `HELP_TOPIC_LOCALE` has 13 columns including SOURCE_HASH; `HelpTopicLocaleView` reads SIX and SOURCE_HASH is not among them -- the string appears nowhere in src/ or include/. The drift-detection column is written and never compared, and the failure is invisible because a STALE row and a DRAFT row both fall back to source text identically. Same shape as AIF-065: two things that never compare themselves, except here the comparison was designed for and simply not wired. DRIFT LIKELY BUT UNPROVEN: locale companions dated 2026-06-11 (RUN_ID PHASE23J-F7C54B7D07BB2118), HELP core rebuilt 2026-07-22 -- mtimes prove the core was rewritten, not that the five fixture topics changed; confirming needs a SOURCE_HASH comparison, recorded unproven per lesson.career.a_script_never_run_is_not_evidence. PRIORITY INVERSION: the boundary note calls manuals/education the strongest near-term multilingual investment (Priority 3) ahead of runtime translation, but `manual_assembly_manifest.yaml` has `binding: {exists: false}` / `status: greenfield` -- the locale spine is NAMED as a source of record for a manual section never built. HELP-first was likely pragmatic (HELP already had topic/section/line tables to hang a companion from) but should be a recorded decision, not an accident. OWED: wire SOURCE_HASH or delete it; prove/disprove the drift; locate PHASE23J (PHASE23T names the contract, PHASE23J names the data run, no doc found); decide the manuals question. |

| AIF-067 | Subcommand identity: `@dottalk.subusage` contract, generated SYSSUBCMD, derived `SET USAGE`, Cowork 2026-07-27 | defect, usage_contract, subcommand, help, metadata, drift_detection, source_followup | `src/cli/cmd_set.cpp` (33-arm ladder + `print_set_usage`); `src/help/helpdata_messages.cpp:9613` (`SetUsageText` literal); `dottalkpp/data/metadata/SYSSUBCMD.dbf` (12 scratch rows, 21-field schema); `dottalkpp/data/scripts/metadata/SYSSUBCMD_SEED_CANDIDATES_v1.csv` (37 rows, 10-field schema); `tools/fullstack_docs/stack_audit_v1.py` (`SUBCOMMAND_ONLY`) | `docs/maintenance/SUBCOMMAND_IDENTITY_CONTRACT_LANE_V1.md` | source_defined | DotTalk++ has no way to DECLARE a subcommand, so a command handled inline in a parent's dispatcher is invisible to the whole documentation chain: no contract, no SYSCMD/SYSSUBCMD row, no HELP topic. Observed four times -- `SET LANGUAGE` and `SET LOCALE` stranded as orphaned locale-preview fixture topics (AIF-066), `REL ENUM` / `SET VAR` / `SET VAR!` listed in `dotref.hpp` with nowhere to resolve (`stack_audit` `SUBCOMMAND_ONLY`). MEASURED DRIFT: `cmd_set.cpp` dispatches 33 ladder options; `SET USAGE` renders `MessageId::SetUsageText`, a hand-typed ~40-line string literal, listing 30 -- `ERRORSTOP` and `INDEXTXN` work but are undiscoverable (`SETCASE`/`SETNEAR` are benign alias spellings). That list is a MESSAGE-CATALOG entry, so it is translatable -- but CORRECTED 2026-07-27: the localization cost is PROSPECTIVE, not incurred. An earlier draft asserted the drift was "replicated into every locale" as though measured; it was not. `SetUsageText` exists in `en-US` ONLY (de/es/fr/it carry 290 messages each and this is not among them), consistent with AIF-066's 6.6% spine coverage. The drift exists once. That strengthens the case for generating the string now rather than after four translators copy its errors, since translated drift is harder to retire than English drift -- retiring it invalidates paid work. Recorded because "it must be worse across the locales" is exactly the plausible inference that gets written down as a finding without anyone running the grep. Meanwhile the metadata layer already anticipated this and went stale unobserved: `SYSSUBCMD.dbf` holds 12 rows = 3 blank plus the SAME three rows three times (`SET PATH`, `SET ORDER`, `REL ENUM`, one batch `ACTIVE=F`) -- shape-test scratch, not a seed -- and the 37-row harvest CSV carries a 10-field schema against the table's 21-field schema, so it cannot load. THIRD INSTANCE of the AIF-065/AIF-066 shape: two things that never compare themselves (here THREE -- ladder, usage text, table). DECIDED (member.derald, 2026-07-27): contract is authority, table is generated. `@dottalk.subusage` blocks sit adjacent to their ladder arm -- NOT a file per subcommand, since `SET LANGUAGE`'s code genuinely belongs in `cmd_set`'s dispatcher and `cmd_set_language.cpp` would be a fiction; identity composes from `parent:` + `sub:` into `QUAL_NAME`, a field the existing 21-field schema already provides alongside `DISP_STYL: routed`. SYSSUBCMD becomes generated, `SET USAGE` renders from SYSSUBCMD (true by construction), and `stack_audit` gains a three-way ladder/contract/table check whose acceptance test is catching the `ERRORSTOP` + `INDEXTXN` drift measured here. Contrast with AIF-066's `AREA51` remedy: that was an inline TOP-LEVEL command and the fix was to give it a file; this is the subcommand case and the fix is to give it a block. |

NO LANE EXISTED and the engine has no representation for one: ONE `XBaseEngine` (`shell.cpp:527`, only instantiation in the tree), flat `_areas[MAX_AREA]`, alias == `logicalName()` == DBF stem, no owner back-pointer; `WORKSPACE` names the whole set of open slots, and `WORKSPACE LOAD` closes everything then restores (swap, never co-residency). THE OBJECT IS ALREADY WRITTEN AND UNWIRED: `reference/data_address.hpp:104-137` carries the full five-level address (WorkspaceIdentity -> DbAreaIdentity -> TableIdentity -> RecordSelector -> FieldIdentity), `qualified_reference.hpp:73` is an unlimited-depth dotted parser, and `src/CMakeLists.txt:46-49` says why nothing calls it -- "Compile-only foundation; no DbArea/tuple/expression/array integration yet"; sole consumer is `test_pdlc_foundation_smoke.cpp`. QUESTION WAS MIS-SCOPED, IN THE OWNER'S FAVOUR: `SQLSEL_PDLC_LANE_V1.md:112` shows AIF-074 P4.1 ALREADY OWNS table-reference qualification ("Adds table ALIASES (`FROM STUDENTS S`) and QUALIFIED column names (`S.LNAME`), which every later slice needs"), so the live decision is not whether to add a workspace level but whether the qualifier P4.1 is about to author is ONE level deep or TWO -- a design choice on a grammar not yet written. ASYMMETRY: ~3 surfaces now (SQLSEL has NO AST at all -- all statement state is function-local in `try_execute_select`; P2 not started; `FROM STUDENTS S` still rejected at `sqlsel_statement.cpp:186-190`, so the grammar seat is empty and reserved) vs ~10 surfaces plus re-running every closed SQLite-oracle gate if deferred past P4.7. R27 WRINKLE: "any two OPEN tables join... with no registry lookup" is unambiguous at one workspace; at several with no scope, every join site inherits `find_open_area_by_name_ci`'s silent first-match, and a wrong join resolution returns plausible WRONG ROWS, not an error. COST IS NOT IN THE OBJECT (registry ~1 KB) but in table-alias uniqueness weakening from globally-unique to unique-per-workspace, which three places assume. MEASURED (compiled probe, g++/libstdc++; MSVC UNVERIFIED = gate G0): `sizeof(DbArea)`=1088, `AreaState`=176, ~1296 B/slot, paid EAGERLY (`dbf_file.cpp:409-411` constructs every slot at engine construction, no lazy path) -> 4096 = 5.06 MB idle / 32 KB stack; 65536 = 81 MB / 512 KB stack against MSVC's 1 MB default with NO `/STACK` override in the build. OWNER RULING ACCEPTED: 512 is arbitrary -- `build_vectors.cmake:8-12` states it is an AIF-044 compatibility default, no upper bound at all; an earlier draft that rationed it per workspace was WRONG and is recorded as such. DEFECTS SURFACED (none fixed): D1 two resolvers/two semantics in one query (SQLSEL resolves FROM, stringifies it back at `:288,:303`, and `tuple_builder` re-resolves with a DIFFERENT matcher that DOES detect ambiguity where the first silently takes the first match); D2 `slot_of_area_ptr` (`set_relations.cpp:171-178`) is an O(MAX_AREA) scan called per join field per row and per scanned child record, reached from the CURSOR HOOK so it fires on ordinary navigation, at 512, today; D3 no `static_assert(max_areas <= INT_MAX)` though `xbase.hpp:43` does `static_cast<int>`; D4 `schema_workspace.cpp:259-269` drops `alias` (does not round-trip); D5 `DTSHEMA` version drift (2 vs 3, two writers one format name). D1+D2 are the FOURTH instance of the AIF-065/066/067 shape: two things that never compare themselves. RECOMMENDATION -- BUY THE OPTION, NOT THE FEATURE: land three items that each pay for themselves alone (store the slot in `DbArea`, killing D2 and 19 duplicated scan sites and unlocking BOTH the cap raise and any partition; consolidate to one ambiguity-detecting resolver, killing D1; design P4.1's qualifier two-level with the outer level defaulted and unreachable), add a scope clause to R27, raise MAX_AREAS to 4096 with both missing guards, and DO NOT build the workspace runtime (no demand case; `PSEUDO_CHAT_RETURN_LANE_V1.md:151-164` already records cross-workspace addressing as deferred). Lane closes at G4 (design accepted into the AIF-074 charter before P4.1 opens). ALSO DELIVERED: `COST_BENEFIT_GATE_DOCTRINE_V1.md` per owner ruling "cost/benefit is now a life lesson in our design" -- cadence "Estimates suggest. Probes measure. Surfaces count. Timing multiplies. Reversal decides.", six-question gate on lane/phase open, and cost figures inheriting the evidence tiers (estimated `sizeof(DbArea)` 800B-1KB vs measured 1088 -- probe took under a minute; measured on the wrong compiler is still not measured). GOVERNANCE: the AIF-075/076/077 rows are WRITTEN BUT UNCOMMITTED -- `git show HEAD:` on this file ends at AIF-074 while the working tree carries 074..078, so the collision gate (which reads the working tree) sees them but a clone or `git pull` does not, and the same numbers re-derive as free from committed state; that is exactly how this session's own AIF-077 survey came back stale, and it is AIF-062's invisible-evidence lesson in a new coat. Practical consequence: this file cannot be committed as part of the AIF-078 slice without fusing three other sessions' rows -- maintainer call. Also `IMPLEMENTATION_PLAN_SQLSEL_V1_20260729.md` is cited as AIF-074's plan of record at `SQLSEL_PDLC_LANE_V1.md:8` but IS NOT IN THE TREE (lives only in the un-applied outputs package) -- same shape as AIF-062's invisible-evidence finding. Second correction recorded: this session reported AIF-077 free from a grep that was already stale (Codex housekeeping claimed and closed it the same day); `claim-aif` allocated 078 correctly, which is AIF-050 working as designed -- grep is not an allocator. PRIOR ART MISSED, AND THE MISS IS THE FINDING: `docs/maintenance/external_ai_intake/virtual_workspaces_memo_resident_2026-07-28/` holds a Grok external design intake for **Virtual Workspaces & Memo-Resident Mini-Databases** (concurrent named workspaces, per-area `kind`, scoped `WORKSPACE SAVE`, extended DTSHEMA v4) whose local assessment drafted an **AIF-070** row that WAS NEVER COMMITTED -- no `coordination/aif/AIF-070.claim`, zero AIF-070 rows in this queue, zero AIF-070 references under `docs/`, `labtalk/`, `coordination/`; the only trace is an aside inside the AIF-071 row ("AIF-070 deliberately left free for the Grok Virtual Workspaces / Memo-Resident lane"). This session surveyed the tree, opened a lane on the same subject, and INDEPENDENTLY PROPOSED `DTSHEMA 4` -- the same design arrived at twice, neither aware of the other. It surfaced only when `audit_trail.py` ran for verification and flagged advisory findings against a package nobody had cited. Recurrence of the failure AIF-071 closed: the landing zone is indexed, but nothing POINTS to it from the ledger or this queue because the row was drafted and never landed. A design registered nowhere is a design that will be done twice. Complementary, not duplicate: AIF-070 owns WHAT A WORKSPACE IS (named, concurrent, memo-resident, hydratable, area-budgeted); AIF-078 owns HOW A NAME RESOLVES once more than one can exist, and only AIF-078 is on AIF-074's clock. | AIF-079 | Declared-but-unreferenced capability detection (validator tier); opened from a house-index investigation, Cowork 2026-07-30 | validator, evidence_taxonomy, xindex, index_maintenance, regression_harness, lmdb, source_change, doc_change, runtime_proven, review_needed | `docs/maintenance/DECLARED_CAPABILITY_VALIDATOR_LANE_V1.md` (lane, planned); `tools/` scanner (NOT WRITTEN); bears on `XIDX-TXN-02` (instances 1, 2, 4, 5), `AIF-043` (instances 3, 7) | `docs/maintenance/DECLARED_CAPABILITY_VALIDATOR_LANE_V1.md`; `docs/maintenance/AIF_043_V6_ROUTING_BOUNDARY_HARDENING_V1_20260730.md`; `docs/maintenance/SESSION_CLOSEOUT_HOUSE_INDEX_VDISK_AND_CAPABILITY_VALIDATOR_2026-07-30.md`; `src/AIPortal/sessions/2026-07-30_cowork_house_index_vdisk/`; commits 3435d2eb9, 4d738be24, d26b10d0e, 7c51e3a30; `coordination/aif/AIF-079.claim` | mixed -- lane `planned` (no tool landed, zero runtime evidence); the engine slice that produced it is runtime-proven | ORIGIN: owner asked whether the LMDB tag-update algorithm would work for the house CNX/V32 index too. IT ALREADY DOES: `apply_replace_snapshot` is backend-neutral and `cdx_document.hpp:20` literally includes `cnx_document.hpp`, so the work is implementing two virtuals (`CnxBackend::upsert`/`erase`, still stubs -- XIDX-TXN-02), not porting an algorithm. The trace of `cmd_replace` -> `DbArea::replaceFieldStored` -> `xbase::index_hooks` produced findings A-E, three of which landed this session. **LANDED AND RUNTIME-PROVEN:** (A) `apply_replace_snapshot` now diffs before/after tag snapshots and emits only moved `(tag,key)` pairs -- a single-field REPLACE on an N-tag table drops from **2N committed LMDB write transactions to 2** (each `upsert`/`erase` outside bulk mode opens its own txn, reopens the DBI, commits, and resets the RO txn, so the old cost was 2N transactions plus 2N RO resets, not 2N map ops); proven by `REGRESSION RUN IDXDIFF` with `DOTTALK_INDEX_TRACE=1`. (C1) `VALIDATE UNIQUE ... REPAIR` routed through `replaceFieldStored` instead of `set()`+`writeCurrent()`, which carry NO index hook -- it rewrote a uniqueness-candidate field (exactly the kind likely to be tagged) and left the tag on the old key with nothing marked stale; proven by `REGRESSION RUN VUREPAIR` (VUR_T2/VUR_T3). (E) index-maintenance failure now surfaces as "written, index not maintained" (true return plus non-empty err) rather than failing a write that did happen; **still source-only, needs a forced failure to be runtime-evidenced.** Plus a phantom-tag defect found en route: `CdxBackend::setTag` fell back to `mdb_dbi_open(..., MDB_CREATE)` in BOTH branches, so any field name minted an LMDB sub-database, diverging the `.cdx` tagdir from the env and burning toward `maxdbs=128` against `DOTTALK_MAX_FIELDS=256` (and `cmd_buildlmdb.cpp:611` uses 1024 for the same env -- unreconciled, NOT fixed here; phantom DBs already written into existing `.cdx.d` environments are also NOT cleaned up). **THE LANE ITSELF:** the class is a capability declared at the interface and absent at the leaf -- the symbol exists, so the declaration reads `source-evidenced` while the behavior is `planned`. Found TWICE IN ONE DAY in unrelated subsystems by two investigations, NEITHER LOOKING FOR IT; seven source-verified seed instances span `xindex`, `cnx`, `cli`, `memo` and form the known-answer set (a v1 tool that misses any of the seven has not earned M1). Detectors D1-D5: unreferenced public capability, unbranched enum value, stub return, write-only state, config key without enforcement. D1/D4 caught the most and are cheapest. Instance 2 (`CNX_HDRF_DIRTY`) is why D4 matters: `set_dirty` DOES have callers, so a caller-count check passes -- the defect is one level in, at the consumer that never reads the flag. SUPPRESSION should reuse `@dottalk.contract`/`@dottalk.usage` `status:` rather than a bespoke allowlist, which inverts the cost correctly: an unimplemented capability must DECLARE itself unimplemented, strengthening annotation coverage instead of competing with it. Open M0 sub-question: file-level vs symbol-level `status: planned` granularity. **AN EIGHTH INSTANCE, NEW CATEGORY, FOUND BY THIS SESSION'S OWN TOOLING:** `kRegressionSpecs` stores subdirectory paths with Windows backslashes; on Linux a backslash is an ordinary filename character, so **22 of 32 specs and 5 of 8 DEFAULT-suite specs were unresolvable and `REGRESSION ALL` printed "script not found" WITHOUT failing the run** -- a green-looking Linux parity pass was covering 3 of 8 default suites, and nothing in the evidence system objected. That is not declared-but-unimplemented; it is **a test that reports success while not running**, same shape (declaration present, behavior absent), and it should be seeded as instance 8 with the harness rule "unresolved script is a hard failure, not a printed note." Fixed by normalizing in `resolve_regression_script_path` (not by editing 22 string literals; `/` works on Windows too, so it resolves both directions); after the fix 0 of 32 unresolvable, 8 of 8 default suites execute, ALL GREEN -- the five that had never run on Linux needed no repair once reachable. **ALSO DELIVERED:** AIF-043 milestone V6 (routing boundary hardening, findings R1-R6) -- the sharp one is R2, `VDISK UNMOUNT` with an open area splits a live area into a phantom RAM buffer and a disk-seeking metadata path because `is_virtual()` flips false while the stream buffer survives; and a `wsl-lean` build preset that needs NO `vcpkg.json` <-> `vcpkg-wsl.json` swap because `VCPKG_MANIFEST_FEATURES=index` already gates tvision/wx/pybind11 -- `vcpkg-wsl.json` is redundant with the canonical manifest's features block and is the root cause of both the swap dance and the wx build time (learned destructively: an unswapped run reconciled the installed tree, "Removing 53/53 tvision:x64-linux"). **PROCESS FINDINGS:** onboarding at the AI Portal happened LATE, and the framing error that MSVC was required on a cross-platform architecture cost most of a session's runtime evidence -- the `wsl` preset carries `DOTTALK_INDEX_MODE: LMDB`, so item A was provable on Linux all along. Also: this session's first working-tree survey reported "4 other-session modified files" when the real count is ~20, because the scoped diff only walked the nine paths the session expected to touch and the result was reported as if it covered the tree -- the same shape as the harness defect above, in the session's own reporting. No harm done: every `git add` named exact paths, so the sixteen unseen files could not be fused regardless. **NOT DONE / OWED:** the scanner itself (M1); the six triage rulings in sitting 3, notably the XIDX-TXN-02 sequencing decision; item E runtime evidence; trace noise (`setTag tag=X fail=...` prints twice per capture for every untagged field on a CORRECT path); a dedicated warning message id for REPLACE/CALCWRITE success-with-warning; `maxdbs` reconciliation. Fixing the seven instances is explicitly OUT of this lane -- each belongs to its own lane, and folding them in would destroy the validator's own proof set before the validator exists. Owner `member.derald`; steward `member.ai.claude.cowork`; `coordination/aif/AIF-079.claim`. |
| AIF-080 | Index container/engine orthogonality: separate container FORMAT from maintenance ENGINE so both are selectable, Cowork 2026-07-30/31 | design, xindex, cnx, cdx, cdx_native, lmdb, capacity_gate, messaging, source_change, doc_change, runtime_proven, review_needed | `docs/maintenance/INDEX_BACKEND_ORTHOGONALITY_LANE_V1.md`; bears on `src/cli/cmd_setindex.cpp`, `src/cli/cmd_setorder.cpp`, `src/xindex/index_manager.cpp`, `src/cli/cmd_setcnx.cpp`, `XIDX-TXN-02` | `docs/maintenance/INDEX_BACKEND_ORTHOGONALITY_LANE_V1.md`; commits a09b6a2e1 (charter), df14d828e (M1 capacity gate); `coordination/aif/AIF-080.claim` | mixed -- M1 landed and source-evidenced with a green parity run; F3 message split DEFERRED; format-gate relocation reclassified as hygiene | TWO AXES ARE CONFLATED: container FORMAT (`.inx`/`.cnx` RUN1 uint32 vs `.cdx` RUN8 uint64) and maintenance ENGINE (native batch plus rebuild vs LMDB transactional). The v64-requires-CDX rule enforces both at once, so "a native, LMDB-free index on a v64 table" is unreachable even though `CdxNativeBackend` exists and is exactly that. **F1 -- the rule is enforced at TWO command surfaces, both on the live path:** `cmd_setindex.cpp:276-282` (`is_x64_cdx_area` -> `SetIndexV64RequiresCdxText`) and `cmd_setorder.cpp:437` (`validate_explicit_container_for_flavor` -> `SetOrderV64RequiresCdxText`). OWNER CORRECTION RECORDED VERBATIM IN THE CHARTER: `SET CNX` / `SET CDX` are LEGACY and predate the current workflow, which is `SELECT n` / `USE <table>` / `SET INDEX TO <indexname>` (defaults to the table name; extension defaults to `.cdx`) / `SET ORDER TO TAG <tag>` / `SEEK <value>`. On the path in actual use the rule IS enforced at both surfaces that matter, so the legacy gap is HYGIENE, not a correctness fire; it folds into M2, which opens `openCdx` anyway. An earlier draft of this lane called it a back door and was wrong. **F2 -- declared-but-uncalled capacity check:** `recordNumberFitsBackend()` / `backendMaxRecordNumber()` were declared at `index_manager.hpp:86-90` with ZERO call sites since introduction -- an AIF-079 D1 instance found independently. **M1 LANDED (df14d828e):** `openCnx` now gates on capacity after the backend opens and refuses a table whose record count exceeds what CNX can address, with a corrective message naming CDX, instead of silently binding an index that cannot represent its own record numbers. This is the FIRST call site for both functions. Uses `area_.recCount64()`, NOT `recCount()`: `recCount()` returns `int32_t` (`xbase.hpp:274`, whose own comment defers to `recCount64()` as authoritative), so using it to detect a 32-bit overflow would TRUNCATE THE VERY QUANTITY BEING CHECKED and the gate could never fire. Placement verified on the live path -- `cmd_setindex.cpp:576` routes v32 to `openCnx` (reachable from `SET INDEX TO`), `:535` routes v64 to `openCdx`, which needs no ceiling since its record numbers are uint64. **RUNTIME:** `REGRESSION ALL` green, 8 of 8 default suites on the wsl-lean build, no new output -- the correct result for a pure addition that refuses only above 2^32 records, and a deliberately boring one. Because `ninja` reported "no work to do" and `ABOUT` shows two different build stamps, the binary was verified to CONTAIN the gate (object `03:15:49` postdates the source edit `03:10:23`; gate string present exactly once in the linked ELF) rather than assuming the build picked it up. **F3 DEFERRED:** the refusal message names a FORMAT when it means an ENGINE capability; splitting it is not a one-line edit because the text also lives in `SYSTEM_MESSAGE_TEXT_IMPORT_v1.csv` across five locales with content hashes. **FLAGGED FOR THE MESSAGING/USAGE LANE, NOT FIXED:** `cmd_setcnx.cpp` carries `status: supported` while no longer being the current workflow; "supported but superseded" has NO representation in the usage contract, so the reflection report emits `supported`, manualgen consumes `supported`, and the manual publishes a legacy surface as current -- the documentation loop propagating a stale fact WITH provenance, which is worse than an undocumented one because it arrives with authority. **GOVERNANCE -- THIS ROW IS ITSELF THE FIX:** the pre-push gate advised "claim(s) with no intake row (abandoned/demo -> release-aif): AIF-068, AIF-070, AIF-080" on both AIF-080 commits. The charter and the engine change were committed while the number still read as ABANDONED from HEAD, because the claim file was untracked and no queue row existed. Same invisible-evidence shape as AIF-062 and AIF-078, this time authored by the session that had just finished writing about it.
