| AIF-081 | Output capture completeness: `DOTSCRIPT ... OUT` discards the entire user-facing command surface, found by dogfooding the engine's own transcript facility, Cowork 2026-07-31 | defect, cli, output_router, shell_transcript, transcript, evidence_integrity, documentation_loop, diagnostics, doc_change, runtime_proven, review_needed | `docs/maintenance/OUTPUT_CAPTURE_COMPLETENESS_LANE_V1.md`; bears on `src/cli/shell_transcript.cpp`, `src/cli/output_router.cpp`, `src/cli/command_output.cpp`, `src/cli/cmd_dotscript.cpp` (help text), `src/xindex/index_manager.cpp:449`, `src/cli/append_support.cpp:74`, `src/cli/dotscript_lexing.cpp` | `docs/maintenance/OUTPUT_CAPTURE_COMPLETENESS_LANE_V1.md` (charter, commit 49dfec789); `docs/maintenance/AIF_081_OUTPUT_CAPTURE_RUNTIME_PROOF_V1_20260731.md` (F1 runtime proof, commit 0803f0f13 -- figures, full one-directional difference and deterministic reproduction are carried IN-DOCUMENT so the transcripts stay disposable); runtime artifacts `dottalkpp/data/tmp/idxstale_transcript.log` (42 lines) and `dottalkpp/data/tmp/alt_probe.log` (89 lines), throwaway and NOT committed by design; `coordination/aif/AIF-081.claim` | findings recorded, NO source change landed -- F1 runtime-proven and source-evidenced, F3 hazard not observed | ORIGIN: the owner asked to stop pasting console output and instead route a script through dottalkpp to a file the agent could read -- dogfooding the engine's own capture rather than shell redirection. The first attempt worked mechanically and immediately exposed a defect IN THE FACILITY BEING USED. **F1 -- MEASURED, same script and same binary:** `DOTSCRIPT ... OUT` produced 42 lines, `SET ALTERNATE` produced 89; set-differenced BOTH directions, the only line unique to `DOTSCRIPT OUT` is its own banner. `SET ALTERNATE` is a STRICT SUPERSET and `DOTSCRIPT OUT` contributes nothing. The line it loses is the one the proof exists to demonstrate -- "REPLACE: record written, but index update failed; REINDEX/REBUILD needed." -- along with `Created`, `Opened`, `Appended blank record N`, `REPLACE: Replaced field #N`, `CNX: created`, `REINDEX CNX`, `REBUILD: done`, `SET ORDER: CNX TAG`, `Recno:`, `Found at 1.`, `Closed.` and `ERASE:`. **MECHANISM, four sites:** the router installs its own streambuf as `std::cout`'s buffer at startup (`output_router.cpp:503-504`); `MultiBuf::overflow`/`xsputn` tee to the alternate file AFTER dispatch (`:326`, `:360`), so ALTERNATE sits at the BOTTOM of the stack and sees everything reaching `MultiBuf` by either route; `shell_transcript` tees one level HIGHER at `std::cout` (`shell_transcript.cpp:87-89`); and `cmdout` never passes through `std::cout` at all, writing to `OutputRouter::out()` (`command_output.cpp:47`) which returns `impl_->routed_stream` (`output_router.cpp:386-388`), bound directly to `MultiBuf`. ONE SENTENCE: `routed_stream` is a SIBLING of `std::cout`, not a child of it -- both terminate at the same `MultiBuf`, only one passes through the transcript tee, and every message a user sees takes the sibling path. **F2 -- the documented contract is false:** the DOTSCRIPT help asserts "tees full command output to a transcript file"; it does not. `shell_transcript.cpp:20` is narrower and accurate ("output emitted through std::cout"), so the accurate comment and the false help text describe the same code without either catching the other. **F3 -- rdbuf ownership hazard (not observed):** `OutputRouter` keeps a `cout_redirect_stack` (`:503`, restored `:512`) and `shell_transcript` keeps `old_cout_` (`:87`, restored `:123`); each guards its OWN nesting, nothing guards interleaving, and a router pop crossing an active transcript restores the wrong buffer. **F4 -- two diagnostic trace families are ON BY DEFAULT:** `index_trace_enabled_()` (`index_manager.cpp:449-457`) returns TRUE when the env var is UNSET ("diagnostic drop-in: on by default"), and `append_trace_enabled()` (`append_support.cpp:74-82`) is the same shape ("silence after diagnosis"). Both are deliberate, both are temporary by their own wording, and the diagnosis window has closed: `[APPEND TRACE]` fires on ordinary user APPENDs, and the AIF-079 IDXDIFF spec text (`cmd_regression.cpp:278`) says "run with DOTTALK_INDEX_TRACE=1", teaching opt-IN for a variable that is opt-OUT. These defaults are ALSO why the broken transcript looked populated -- silence both and the same `DOTSCRIPT OUT` run yields six marker lines and nothing else. **BLAST RADIUS:** a proof asserting on engine messages, captured with `DOTSCRIPT OUT`, with the trace families silenced, produces an evidence file that exists, is non-empty, carries markers, and omits every fact it was written to record. That is the AIF-079 class one layer out -- instance 8 was a TEST that reported success while not running; this is a CAPTURE that succeeds while capturing nothing that matters. It lands squarely on the house thesis that documentation consumes and proves the database, because a transcript IS the consumption step. **FIX SEAM:** the architecture already has the correct one and ALTERNATE stands on it -- `DOTSCRIPT OUT` should drive the router's alternate sink or register a second file sink in `MultiBuf` instead of swapping `std::cout.rdbuf()`, which DELETES code rather than adding it; the help-text correction must land in the SAME commit or the catalog keeps teaching the old behaviour into manualgen. **ADJACENT, EXPLICITLY OUT OF LANE (F5):** `;` in DotScript is position-dependent -- TRAILING and unquoted it is a line continuation (`script_reader.hpp:18`), LEADING it makes the line skippable (`dotscript_lexing.cpp:84`), so a marker line ending in an unquoted `;` silently swallows the line beneath it; and inside the module whose stated purpose (`dotscript_lexing.hpp:15-20`) was to end five drifting copies of this logic, `is_comment_or_blank()` treats leading `;` as skippable while `is_comment_line()` does not list `;` at all. Nesting was also confirmed while authoring the repro: main plus exactly one subscript, declared at `cmd_dotscript.cpp:61` and enforced at `:491`. **METHOD NOTE -- TWO STEWARD ERRORS RECORDED:** (1) predicted the two facilities would capture DISJOINT halves, reasoning from ALTERNATE's NAME to its level; measurement says strict superset, and the rule re-earned is that a facility's coverage is a property of WHERE IT TAPS THE STREAM, never inferred from its name or its documentation. (2) asserted traces were on "only because this script enables them"; the script enables nothing and both families default ON. Both were caught by reading source BEFORE writing the lane doc rather than after. Owner `member.derald`; steward `member.ai.claude.cowork`; `coordination/aif/AIF-081.claim`. |

CO-RESIDENCY FINDINGS (owner theory pause, not built): name collision is the real hazard, not memory -- R27 first-match resolution returns plausible WRONG ROWS when two workspaces share a table name (exactly the "open related schemas together" case, e.g. MCC x64 + x32 both carrying STUDENTS); RAM adds a second collision the disk case lacks (hydration flattens to filenames under one root, so two workspaces both want data/ram/STUDENTS.dbf -- per-workspace RAM subroot is cheap now, a migration later); relations are engine-global, so a refresh has no group scope. Sharing needs no new lock design: ramfs is process-global, not cross-process, so cross-user sharing IS writeback or send, which is why the locks sit at the disk boundary only. **THE CHARTERED DESTINATION LANDED THE SAME EVENING (owner: 'do it'):** MINIDB 1 -- a whole database inside one memo field. WORKSPACE SAVE <name> MEMO MINIDB writes a length-prefixed binary-safe container (posture + every table's and attached index's bytes; residence-aware reads so RAM working sets save whole); WORKSPACE LOAD <name> MEMO RAM hydrates FROM THE PAYLOAD with zero disk reads; plain MEMO load refuses with instruction. Runtime-proven FIRST TRY build 18:35:35: mcc_db 94200 B oracle-verified as a unit, clean-RAM hydration 24 files / 65.5 ms (faster than disk-sourced), rows + CDX from memo-carried bytes. Promoted as WORKSPACE_MINIDB (spec 42). DEEP RECORD (owner-ordered, mechanism over chronology): `docs/maintenance/MEMO_RESIDENT_MINIDB_V1.md` -- container format rationale, composition table (every hard problem pre-solved by a part built for another reason), residence map, seven-lane crossing map incl. the NAMED Part B coupling (sidecar carriage) and the AIF-078 co-residency block, honest non-claims (no atomic hydrate, no LMDB, no size governance, supersede retains old tokens -- compaction is a chartered ruling), and five open questions. STILL OPEN IN-LANE: COMMIT/DISMISS writeback cycle (three owner rulings pending), Part B MCC regeneration with NOTES M on STUDENTS/TEACHERS, flavor seed rows, SCHEMA_NAME christening, whitepaper reconciliation STILL OWED (.docx not in tree). **WRITEBACK ARM COMPLETED BY A COWORKER, 2026-08-12.** `member.ai.claude.cowork` joined as **coworker** on this lane (owner rule: a coworker may finish another member's lane provided they add themselves and leave a note). Lane claim UNCHANGED and still held by `member.ai.grok.xai` -- this is participation, not reallocation. Delivered: `WORKSPACE WRITEBACK <name> [TO <root>] [WITH INDEXES] [CONFIRM]`, the return leg that closes disk -> memo -> RAM -> disk, with the POSTURE as enumeration authority (never the session's attach order, which silently wrote 15 of 27 files on the first cut); shortfall ABORTS and leaves the filesystem untouched including empty directories; gather-all-before-writing, zero-byte-source abort, CONFIRM + `.__wbak`, oracle byte-compare per landed file. Proven on BOTH toolchains -- WB_T1..WB_T6 green on Linux g++ 11 and Windows MSVC, same 15 files, `[oracle OK]` each, refusal arm writing nothing; two transcripts committed. Proving it required two owner-ruled vocabulary additions the same day because the markers could not otherwise assert: `FILE(<path>)` and `ERASE DIR <path> CONFIRM`. Records: closeout `docs/maintenance/SESSION_CLOSEOUT_WORKSPACE_WRITEBACK_REGRESSION_2026-08-12.md`; proof row `proof.workspace.writeback`; run `AIPR-20260812-002`; commits `b3f648288`, `360203598`, `1ac39c517`. STILL OPEN and deliberately NOT taken: `WORKSPACE COMPACT`, the FMT-mismatch check, `build_minidb_container`'s order-dependent index enumeration (the same defect shape writeback v1 had), the WITH INDEXES arm and the CONFIRM/collision/`.__wbak` gates (built, never exercised), plus everything above that predates this note. **Note to the claim holder:** the writeback item in the STILL OPEN list above is now closed; the rest of the lane is untouched and yours. |

Result: **68 files + 3 renames** (R099/R096/R095, history follows), R1 struck through in place and superseded by **R8**, six collision sites collapsed by hand, PLDC surviving only where it names what was retired. **Three failures are the durable content, all the same shape -- something reported success while measuring less than it claimed.** (1) **`git add <path>` stages whole files:** the first slice fused **~570 lines of four other sessions' uncommitted work**, including `PROMOTION_PROCESS.md` doctrine and four untracked files; caught by diffing staged lines against the search term, **not by any gate**; 1207/308 -> 168/162 after unstaging ten paths. (2) **"Zero PLDC remains" was asserted four times and was false every time.** Ripgrep honors `.gitignore`; `:346` and `:323` hid `gptbase_bundle_v1/` -- a bundle packaged **for an outside model**, the reader least able to recover from a dangling pointer -- and 23 escrow snapshots. **The edit and the verification shared a tool, so each pass raised confidence without moving coverage; AIF-092, scoped differently, found the live miss in minutes** (two dangling renamed paths at `:29`/`:101` plus a whole `## PLDC Boundary` heading). Written up as `lesson.career.a_gitignored_path_is_invisible_to_your_sweep`; its `next_gate` asks whether sweep-scope belongs in `prepush_gate.py` rather than in a lesson, given AIF-082 measured unenforced obligations at 33 percent against 83-94 for gated ones. (3) **The expansion was changed when only the acronym was in scope** -- "Project" for "Programming" -- and reverted in `03282baaa` once `PDLC_STUDENT_WORKING_MODEL_LANE_V1.md:27` proved "Programming" load-bearing in the desk-vs-org pedagogy. **The merge widened PDLC's SCOPE; its NAME never changed, and the commit subjects on `c0d3069c5` and `018bd0c9f` still say "Project" and are permanently wrong on that point** -- the doctrine file is authoritative, not the commit subject. Doctrine line 36 carries AIF-092's wording, which is better than the union it replaced: PDLC nests inside an SDLC **when the unit is a change**, and packages reviewed SDLC and LabTalk truth **when the unit is a deliverable**. **Registration is late and recorded as such:** claimed only after `session-log-check` reported the closeout unattributable, and this row exists only because the collision gate then reported a claim with no intake row. Escrow under `manualgen/backups/**` deliberately untouched -- widening a search does not license widening an edit. Open, all other sessions': five tracked files still holding PDLC edits behind their work; `portal_truth_audit_latest.{md,json}` unregenerated because `labtalk/portal/labtalk_portal.py:13` imports tkinter at module scope and `.venv312`'s vcpkg base has no `_tkinter`; `gptbase_bundle_v1/` hand-corrected pending real regeneration; website republish blocked on another session's untracked portal work. Core law: the acting atom cannot remember and the remembering atom cannot act, so identity + state live on the project and a chat's first move is to read it. Frames the identity/`wake` design (member vs run; `wake` = the chat->project read walked first) and explains why a handoff cannot be a quip (quip is chat->chat; a handoff must cross a dead chat, so it routes through the ledger). ## Intake Rules

- Add only distilled candidates, not entire conversations.
- Prefer existing destinations over new documents.
- Mark raw or weak material as `review-needed`.
- Mark obsolete material as `superseded` or `rejected` instead of letting it
  stay ambiguous.
- Keep evidence anchors explicit.

| AIF-108 | Memo Object Challenge: 100 test ideas for the memo object, opened as a governed challenge to external AI agents through the house BBS; Cowork 2026-08-11, owner-directed ("we will make this an open challenge to other ai agents in the bbs, a good controllable exercise in AI cooperations and communications") | test_design, memo_subsystem, bbs, agency_model, ai_cooperation, external_intake, protocol, doc_change, chartered | `docs/maintenance/MEMO_OBJECT_CHALLENGE_LANE_V1.md`; bears on `src/memo/`, `src/cli/cmd_workspace.cpp` (MINIDB containers), `src/bbs/` (the venue), AIF-070 (subject), AIF-083 F5 (post-body width) | `docs/maintenance/MEMO_OBJECT_CHALLENGE_LANE_V1.md` (the hundred, tagged by reachability; protocol; adjudication table; milestones M1-M5) | chartered -- NO engine change proposed; this lane produces tests and a protocol for receiving tests | **THE SUBJECT IS THE MOST LOAD-BEARING AND LEAST DEFENDED SURFACE IN THE ENGINE.** The memo store is payload-agnostic by design, adversarially proven by the zoo on RANDOM payloads, and since 2026-08-11 carries whole databases (MINIDB). It has never been attacked by something that UNDERSTANDS it. The lane opens 100 specific ideas -- payload extremes, lifecycle, nesting/recursion, concurrency, corruption/forensics, scale, portability, workspace/MINIDB internals, adversarial container parsing, and teaching oddities -- each tagged by what gates it: roughly two-thirds are writable TODAY; the blocked third is a MAP OF WHICH LANES GATE WHICH PROOFS (memo-sidecar carriage in containers blocks all ten recursion ideas; the multi-workspace lane blocks the cross-workspace joins; the growth limiter blocks the bomb-analogue cases). That map is the lane's second deliverable. **RECURSION FINDING, RECORDED AT CHARTER TIME:** nested databases are theoretically reachable (a catalog is just a table, so a container can carry one) but would FAIL SILENTLY today -- `build_minidb_container` does not carry memo sidecars, so a nested catalog would arrive as rows with live-looking tokens and no payloads behind them. Hollow database, no error: the exact failure shape this project spends its days hunting. `DEPTH`/`SELF_REF` were reserved for this and are declaration-only; cycles (easy to create by accident via supersede) and size multiplication per level are the two guards that must exist before depth > 0 is allowed. **CROSS-WORKSPACE JOINS, MEASURED CLAIM:** they already work mechanically -- every workspace hydrates into ONE flat area array and the join engine only sees areas, with no notion of workspace membership. What is missing is the QUALIFIER, and that object is already written and unwired in the tree (the five-level address with a workspace level at the top, compile-only, sole consumer a smoke test). Cross-workspace joins are a naming feature wearing a plumbing costume -- which is also why the first-match hazard is sharp: two systems carrying a same-named table join to whichever loaded first and return plausible WRONG ROWS. **COOPERATION EXPERIMENT (the owner's stated purpose):** deliberately controllable -- narrow subject, existing evidence grammar, text-only submissions, mechanical adjudication (a marker reads .T. or it does not), and NOTHING an agent submits executes on the maintainer's machine without the maintainer running it. That last rule is what makes an open challenge safe to hold and preserves the project's zero-AI-executed-git-actions record. Eight participation rules incl. honest tiering (`proposed` / `sandbox-run` / `runtime-proven` with a build stamp), mandatory statement of what was NOT tested, prior-art-first, and permanent member attribution. Five verdicts, and the scoring says out loud that **a RED marker from an outside agent is worth more than a green one**. Precedent this stands on: an external AI's whimsical stress spec was MAPPED NOT ADOPTED into the zoo harness, and an external design intake (Grok) chartered the memo-resident database lane months before it shipped -- both valuable, neither usable as submitted; the open question is whether a stated protocol closes that gap. **KNOWN CONSTRAINT, STATED UP FRONT:** BBS post body is `C(240)`, so submissions must split or wait for the memo-width work three lanes now want -- a cooperation exercise that cannot carry a paragraph is an exercise in something else, and this lane will sharpen that requirement rather than route around it. M2 is deliberately "run every reachable idea IN-HOUSE FIRST -- we do not open a challenge on ground we have not walked." Owner `member.derald`; steward `member.ai.claude.cowork`; claim OWED (`claim-aif`; 108 proposed from a read-only survey, and grep is not an allocator -- AIF-078's lesson). |

Nesting limit confirmed precisely (`g_dotscript_depth >= 2`, "max 1 subscript", self-declared in the contract header); removing it needs call frames, which is the SAME work as parameters and locals -- two of the three critical gaps are one lane. No ON ERROR/TRY/CATCH anywhere; STOP_ON_ERROR is a threshold policy, a different mechanism. **STEWARD CORRECTION, RECORDED:** the steward first called the function surface "clean ground, not chartered anywhere" -- WRONG. `DOTSCRIPT_ARRAYS_SPEC_V1.md` (AIF-038, 37 normative sections, six days prior) already rules the hardest question: s19 fixes parameter semantics (reference values; rebinding a local changes only the local binding; by-reference out of scope) and BINDS ALL FUTURE TYPES to the same rule; s5 anticipates a `callable` value kind; s7 reserves `{\|` for code blocks; s27 gives the limits pattern (caps checked BEFORE allocation -- recursion depth is the same hazard class); s29 gives the catalogued/localizable/SelfDoc error-contract pattern any TRY work must extend, not parallel; s13's cycle policy is implemented (`would_create_cycle`). The sharpest constraint on an unstarted feature was written earlier in a document about something else -- prior-art rule vindicated within the hour it was applied. **FIVE OWNER RULINGS PENDING (sec 6 of the findings note), nothing to be built before them:** parameter declaration surface (PARAMETERS line vs signature); LOCAL declared vs inferred; where a body lives (inline / own file / DEFFN-seam runtime registration); recursion-depth cap number; error model as new construct vs STOP_ON_ERROR extension. **ALSO COMPLETED IN THE SAME PASS:** the owed MINIDB-vs-spec reconciliation (s23/s24: CONSISTENT -- carrier never interprets payloads, DBF stores the reference; two divergences recorded not fixed: content-type home, PORTABLE/TYPED mode vocabulary; s17's ARRAY diagnostic family named as the ready-made shape for WORKSPACE VERIFY); and TUPTALK measured as the tuple side of s20 (process-wide typed row buffer, PUSH ROW schema-aligned fixed-width builder -- an in-memory row builder with no table beneath), with the s21 bridges (TUPTOARRAY / ARRAYTOTUP / TUPMATERIALIZE with mandatory limit) specified and unbuilt. OWED: formal external-intake package for the evaluation text (MANIFEST discipline, verbatim preservation); function-surface charter AFTER the five rulings; site product page gains the gap table with tiers once chartered. Owner `member.derald`; coworker of record + steward `member.ai.claude.cowork`; claim 109 to be allocated via `claim-aif --number 109` (survey says free; grep is not an allocator). |
| AIF-110 | FIELDMGR APPEND silently blank-corrupts x64 tables: the temp-swap rewrite does not preserve X64M vector metadata identity; found, measured, and guarded during AIF-070 Part B; Cowork 2026-08-12 | engine_defect, data_integrity, x64_vector, fieldmgr, schema_mutation, guard, blocked_lane | `src/core/fields_mgr.cpp` (`append_rewrite_table`, the guard); `src/cli/cmd_fieldmgr.cpp` (usage contract); `src/xbase/dbf_create.cpp` (X64M stamping); blocks AIF-070 Part B (`dottalkpp/data/scripts/mcc_add_notes_memo.dts`, banner-BLOCKED v5) | claim `coordination/aif/AIF-110.claim` (run `AIPR-20260812-001`, lane `fieldmgr_x64m_identity`); commits `eeaa41e89` (message misfire, TIER0 only -- a failed pathspec aborted the add) + `083016b3a` (the actual slice) | **FIXED AND PROVEN, guard lifted same session (build 05:04:34): the blank-writer was the rewrite loop's MISSING writeCurrent(), found by full write-path trace after the identity fix alone left records blank; identity fix + long-name enablement landed alongside** | **MECHANISM, CORRECTED BY MEASUREMENT (the row below records the investigation as it unfolded; this sentence records where it ended):** the temp-name X64M stamp was a REAL defect (hex-verified, fixed via an identity-explicit create_dbf overload passing the area's promoted logical name) but NOT the blank cause -- set() stores only into the in-memory _fd vector, appendBlank() writes a space-padded row to disk immediately and the NEXT appendBlank discards pending values, and only writeCurrent() encodes _fd into the record; the rewrite loop never called it, so every set() succeeded into a buffer nobody flushed while its proven sibling (cmd_copy's loop, cmd_copy.cpp:590) always had the call. Corroboration: deleted rows would have carried real bytes (deleteCurrent ends in writeCurrent) -- MCC has none, hence 200/200 blank. Proof of fix: short-name memo append AND 19-char long-name append (EXTENDED_COMMENTARY, descriptor token EXTENDED_C, value round-tripped BY LONG NAME) both green on throwaway copies with real record bytes in the hex. Long-name enablement (owner: "long names were not stressed" -- they were REFUSED): validateFieldName ceiling raised to the X64M authority, append() re-tightens to 10 for legacy flavors. Latents flagged for later: storeFieldsToBuffer swallows encode failures as silent space-padding (same failure shape one layer down); dbarea.cpp:77 stray comment glyph. **THE ORIGINAL INVESTIGATION RECORD FOLLOWS.** THE DEFECT, HEX-VERIFIED: `create_dbf(tempPath)` stamps the X64M vector metadata block with the TEMP filename -- `STUDENTS.__fldtmp` found inside the renamed file's 686-byte header -- and the x64 vector reader then returns BLANK fields for every record while record count, schema, field descriptors, and deleted flags all read correct. Silent data destruction wearing a healthy header. Found on the canonical MCC fixtures (the only tables designed to survive it): STUDENTS/TEACHERS blanked twice across five migration runs, restored twice from the vfp lane, `50000000 Taylor Quinn` verified under a rebuilt SID tag both times -- the og->x32->vfp->x64 chain performed exactly as chartered, THE CHAIN IS THE BACKUP. **FIVE RUNS, FIVE LESSONS (each banked in the v5 script header):** (1) `parseFieldSpec` validates at its own tail, before `append()` can resolve the memo flavor sentinel -- fixed, 0 legal in `validateFieldDef`'s M case; (2) CDX containers are hash-bound to table structure (reclen/fields/schema-hash) and correctly refuse a rewritten table -- rebuild REQUIRED, not recommended; (3) CDX identity lives in a SEPARATE sidecar `<container>.cdx.meta` (`cdx_meta.cpp`) -- deleting only the `.cdx` leaves the stale identity judging the fresh container (reset-rule corollary, now doctrine); (4) the rewrite loop lacked `readCurrent` after `gotoRec` (its prior art `cmd_copy` always had both) -- fixed, necessary but NOT sufficient; (5) the X64M identity itself, the root cause, reachable only by hex after every marker-level theory died. **WHAT ALSO LANDED EN ROUTE:** memo-type enablement for FIELDMGR APPEND (type M, flavor-resolved token slot x64=8/legacy=10, blank tokens ARE the null ref so no sidecar write at append time, DTX autocreates on USE) -- correct and waiting behind the guard; an accidental posture REPAIR (re-saved `mcc_x64.dtschema` attaches `students.cdx`, which the old canonical posture silently omitted). **THE FIX LANE (task #186):** re-stamp or rewrite the X64M identity after the temp->final rename, in `append_rewrite_table` or as a `dbf_create` post-rename helper; then the guard lifts, the v5 migration banner comes off, and AIF-070 Part B completes in one run (expected container: 27 files = 13 tables + 12 CDX + 2 DTX). Regression owed: append a field on a COPY of a vector table, read values back by tag -- the test this defect proves nobody ever ran. Papercut logged: FIELDMGR usage example `APPEND ZIP C10` does not match `parseFieldSpec`'s paren grammar. Owner `member.derald`; finder + steward `member.ai.claude.cowork`. |
| AIF-111 | A Cowork sandbox CAN build and run the engine -- the documented `-fsyntax-only` ceiling measured FALSE, changing the cost model for every sandboxed session; Cowork 2026-08-12, found while proving AIF-070's writeback arm | onboarding, doc_defect, build_environment, sandbox, agent_capability, measurement, doc_change | `AI_README.md` ("A sandbox is not the WSL host" -- the table whose conclusion is wrong); `CLAUDE.md` ("Assume you cannot build or run the engine, and verify rather than trust this line" -- the invitation this lane accepted); `docs/agents/HANDOFF_CLAUDE_COWORK_SANDBOX_BUILD_2026-08-12.md` (the recipe + traps) | claim `coordination/aif/AIF-111.claim` (run `AIPR-20260812-002`, lane `sandbox-build-ceiling`); commit `360203598` (handoff); proof `labtalk/proofs/runs/workspace_writeback_proof_teed_20260812T174605Z.log` (a real regression run from a sandbox-built binary) | **MEASURED, one session, recipe committed -- doc correction OWED to AI_README/CLAUDE.md** | **THE CORRECTION:** `AI_README.md` concludes "the practical ceiling in such a sandbox is per-translation-unit `g++ -fsyntax-only`. Builds and runs are therefore maintainer-operated handoffs." A full `dottalkpp` was built inside the Cowork sandbox and used to run `REGRESSION RUN WORKSPACE_WRITEBACK` against the live data root, WB_T1..WB_T6 green, twice. **BE PRECISE ABOUT SCOPE:** the *staged* `bin-wsl-lean` ELF is still not expected to execute (built against the WSL host's newer glibc/GLIBCXX) -- that row of the table STANDS and was not re-tested; what is false is "cannot build", and the ceiling drawn from it. The sandbox lacks a toolchain BY DEFAULT; it is not prevented from having one. **WHY IT MATTERS BEYOND ONE SESSION:** a sandboxed agent can close its own proof loop instead of handing every build to the maintainer, which is a cost-model change for every future Cowork session and removes the largest single source of "prepared, unproven" handoffs. **THE RECIPE, five steps and four of them non-obvious** (full detail in the handoff): pip-install cmake+ninja (no root needed); build OFF the mount (it dominates the build time and two configure checks want a freely `git init`-able tree); reuse the repo's OWN `build-wsl-lean/vcpkg_installed/x64-linux` rather than fetching vcpkg -- but it must sit at `<root>/vcpkg_installed/x64-linux` AND both `_VCPKG_INSTALLED_DIR` and `VCPKG_TARGET_TRIPLET` must be passed, because the vcpkg-generated `unofficial-sodiumConfig.cmake` interpolates exactly those two variables and otherwise fails with `includes non-existent path "//include"` (the message names sodium; the cause is the variables); `git init` the copy because `build_product_inventory.py` shells to `git ls-files` at CONFIGURE time and dies with a packaging-script error otherwise; and swap liblmdb/libsodium/libsqlite3 `.a` for the distro's own via `apt-get download` + `dpkg -x` (no root), because the vcpkg statics reference `__isoc23_strtol` which this glibc does not export and the link fails on `mdb.c`. **RUNTIME TRAP:** the binary resolves its data root from its own location first (`cmd_init.cpp:140`), so `cd` to `dottalkpp/` -- NOT `dottalkpp/data/`, which resolves DATA to `data/data` and the script is not found. **WHAT THIS DOES NOT LICENSE, stated because capability invites drift:** git mutation from a sandbox is still prohibited (that rule was broken in this same session -- read-only `--no-optional-locks` only); a green here is a green on Ubuntu 22.04 with distro libs, NOT the maintainer's toolchain, so every proof must name which; and deletion may be blocked on the mount until the host grants it, which silently converts a regression's teardown into a stale-fixture false green on the next run. **METHOD NOTE:** this finding exists because `CLAUDE.md` explicitly says to verify rather than trust its own line, and the Tier 1 seed says to MEASURE environment versions rather than cite a doc. The corpus asked to be falsified and was. Owner `member.derald`; finder + steward `member.ai.claude.cowork`. |

| AIF-113 | Lock release and recovery: the engine's entire designed recovery path is dead code, and the user-facing LOCK command never releases; Cowork 2026-08-15, split out of AIF-112 after source access showed the defect does not touch that lane | engine_defect, concurrency, xbase_locks, dead_code, recovery, doc_change, review_needed | `src/xbase/xbase_locks.cpp` (`release_held` :407, `force_unlock_table` :386, `force_unlock_record` :393, `current_owner` :60-63, stale reaper :244/:315/:321); `include/xbase_locks.hpp` :55-59; `src/cli/cmd_lock.cpp` :161/:175/:199; `src/cli/cmd_unlock.cpp` :114; 43 lock call sites across 13 files (I5 review U3) | claim `coordination/aif/AIF-113.claim` (run `COWORK-20260815-001`, lane `lock-release-recovery`); charter `docs/maintenance/LOCK_RELEASE_AND_RECOVERY_LANE_V1.md`; evidence `docs/maintenance/AIF112_SOURCE_REUSE_AUDIT_AND_I5_SCOPING_V1.md` (AIPR-20260815-COWORK-002) | **CHARTERED, AND RE-RANKED 2026-08-15: runtime leg supplied by AIF-116, this lane is now a BLOCKING DEPENDENCY of AIF-116's fix, not housekeeping; still no source change proposed** | **PRIORITY INVERSION, RECORDED 2026-08-15.** This row was written from static analysis and closed with "no runtime probe executed". The probe ran that same day as AIF-112 Phase-1 Step 4 (banner `fb7106e0 dirty`) and became AIF-116: `xbase_locks` writes the owner pid through an un-imbued stream (`pid=16,984`), `std::stoul` reads back `16`, `is_pid_alive` says no, and the stale branch at `:244` force-removes a LIVE owner's lock. So the stale reaper this row describes as correct-but-narrow is in fact **firing constantly, on locks whose owners are alive**. THE BLOCKING RELATIONSHIP, which is the reason for this amendment: leaked Class B locks are currently being cleaned up BY that broken stale detection, so the two defects mask each other. Repair AIF-116's parse alone and orphans become permanently unreclaimable, with no exposed command to clear them, because the three dead functions below are exactly the missing escape hatch -- one abandoned `LOCK TABLE` would wedge that table for every process on the machine. **The enforcement fix and the recovery path must land together**, which promotes design option 2 (permission-gated FORCE verb) from optional to required and keeps option 1 (`release_held` wired into area close) as the leak-prevention half. RELEASE-SIDE SURVEY WIDENED AND CONFIRMED: `CLOSE`, `CLEAR`, `USE`/`OPEN`, `DbArea::close()` (`dbarea.cpp:59`), `~DbArea()` (`:57`) and process exit ALL leave the sidecar in place; explicit `UNLOCK` is the only release path in the system -- which confirms the Class A/B split below rather than revising it, and answers the maintainer's question during the run (entering a new work area does not clear another session's lock, and nothing else does either). ALSO OBSERVED, filed as AIF-116 E2 but belonging to this lane's surface: `UNLOCK` on a record that is not locked reports success, leaving an operator unable to distinguish "released it" from "there was nothing to release" -- the exact state AIF-116 produces. ACCEPTANCE IMPLICATION: at least one criterion now needs a runtime leg -- a two-process test that a leaked Class B lock is recoverable through an exposed command, the same harness AIF-116 owes for refusal-under-contention; build it once, use it for both. METHOD NOTE WORTH KEEPING: the static analysis held on every factual point it made -- three dead functions, Class A/B split, absent FORCE verb -- and was wrong only about urgency, which static analysis could not have reached. **A source-defined charter can be entirely correct and still mis-rank itself; the word that needed a runtime leg was "housekeeping".** THE ORIGINAL STATIC FINDINGS FOLLOW. THREE DEAD FUNCTIONS, MEASURED AT `b04940a78`: `release_held` is declared, defined, and called by NOTHING (two occurrences in the whole tree); `force_unlock_table` and `force_unlock_record` likewise exist and are called by nothing; `cmd_unlock.cpp` handles ALL and TABLE and exposes NO FORCE verb. So invariant I5 of `WORKSPACE_MANAGER_AND_GROUPS_DESIGN_V1.md` is CORRECT to the line number but TOO OPTIMISTIC: it says a leaked lock is clearable by "nothing but FORCE UNLOCK", and FORCE UNLOCK is not reachable from any command. A leaked live-pid lock owned by another process is clearable by no exposed command; recovery means killing the owner or removing the sidecar by hand. THE EXPOSURE IS NARROW AND NAMED: every lock acquisition outside `xbase_locks.cpp` is either CLASS A (transient, released in the same operation -- `bbs_store.cpp` :95/:99 and `cmd_workspace.cpp` :2112/:2117 via RAII destructors, plus paired sites in `append_support.cpp` and the six single-write commands) or CLASS B (held across operations -- `cmd_lock.cpp` ONLY, which contains zero `unlock` calls by design). Class B is the entire exposure. WHY IT WAS NEVER CAUGHT: `WsLock`'s destructor calls `unlock_table` EXPLICITLY rather than relying on area close, so the two most-exercised users route around the defect and it never appears in their proofs; the only surface it bites has no regression exercising hold-then-close-then-contend. DESIGN OPTIONS (charter section, none chosen): wire `release_held` into area close per I5; expose a permission-gated FORCE verb on the `cmd_net.cpp` model; give the owner token a workspace suffix; harden the Class A paired sites to RAII. Options 1 and 2 are independent and both wanted -- one prevents the leak, the other recovers leaks that already exist. TIES: AIF-112 (demoted this out of its Phase-1 gate on the Class A/B evidence), AIF-059 (Hot Potato, same "who holds what and how is it released" family), `proof.agency.model` declared gap "git agency serialized by convention not mechanism". Owner `member.derald`; finder + steward `member.ai.claude.cowork`. |
| AIF-114 | Seven published SET options have no implementation: `set-family.mdx` lists eleven settings under "Current documented surfaces include" and seven do not exist in source; live on x64base.com with no status qualifier; Cowork 2026-08-15, found incidentally during AIF-112 Phase-1 Step 0 | doc_defect, public_site, pdlc, set_family, prove_before_you_claim, gate_candidate, review_needed | `x64base-site` `content/docs/dottalk/set-family.mdx` (the published block); `src/cli/cmd_set*.cpp` (the measurement); `tools/staging/prepush_gate.py` cross-authority normalization gate (the candidate remedy, currently covers dotref/foxref but not the SET family) | claim `coordination/aif/AIF-114.claim` (run `COWORK-20260815-001`, lane `set-family-doc-drift`); charter `docs/maintenance/SET_FAMILY_PUBLISHED_WITHOUT_IMPLEMENTATION_LANE_V1.md` | **CHARTERED -- measured both legs (runtime + source); no fix chosen, aspirational-vs-stale unresolved** | ABSENT FROM SOURCE, ALL SEVEN: `SET SAFETY`, `SET EXACT`, `SET ESCAPE`, `SET CARRY`, `SET CONFIRM`, `SET EXCLUSIVE`, `SET MULTILOCKS`. Present: `SET TABLE BUFFER`, `SET DELETED`, `SET CASE`, `SET NEAR`. Confirmed independently on the live runtime -- `SET EXCLUSIVE` and `SET MULTILOCKS` both fall through to the generic SET usage listing and appear in neither the public nor the developer/transitional option lists. HOW IT SURFACED, which is the cost: those two were typed during AIF-112 discovery BECAUSE the published page said they existed. The doc sent a reader looking for something that was never there. THIS IS THE VDISK CEIL PATTERN one rung thinner -- `BBS_SESSION_EXCHANGE_GUARD_LANE_V1.md` names it ("a runaway RAM cap whose config surface exists but whose enforcement is ABSENT ... do NOT ship the cap as config without the enforcement path proven"); here there is not even a config surface, only a published claim. It is also the cleanest available violation of the house rule "Prove before you claim. Status is PLANNED -> PARTIAL -> SUPPORTED only on runtime proof." SETTLE FIRST, THEN FIX: `git log` on `cmd_set*.cpp` and the mdx answers whether the seven are ASPIRATIONAL (classic xBase, intended, never built) or STALE (once implemented, since removed) -- the answer changes the remedy. Options: delete from the page; split the block under an explicit "not implemented" heading (recommended -- one edit, tells the truth, keeps the record of intent); or implement per-setting where wanted. | AIF-116 | Locale grouping in the lock owner string defeats mutual exclusion: the sidecar records `pid=16,984`, `std::stoul` reads it back as `16`, and every live lock is therefore classified stale and force-removed; Cowork 2026-08-15, found on a live two-process run during AIF-112 Phase-1 Step 4 | engine_defect, concurrency, data_integrity, xbase_locks, locale, aif031_sweep_miss, runtime_observed, blocking_dependency, test_gap, review_needed | `src/xbase/xbase_locks.cpp` (`make_owner_string` :55-57 un-imbued `ostringstream`; lock writer :157-160; `read_lock_meta` :112-117 the `stoul` parse; `is_pid_alive` :181; stale branch :244) -- **zero `imbue` calls in the entire TU**; affected callers `src/bbs/bbs_store.cpp:95`, `src/cli/append_support.cpp` :326/:416/:450/:500, `cmd_commit.cpp:233`, `cmd_delete.cpp:216`, `cmd_recall.cpp:234`, `cmd_calcwrite.cpp:712`, `cmd_lock.cpp` :161/:175/:199; release side `cmd_close.cpp`/`cmd_clear.cpp`/`cmd_use.cpp`/`dbarea.cpp` :57/:59 (none release) | claim `coordination/aif/AIF-116.claim` (run `COWORK-20260815-001`, lane `lock-owner-locale-grouping`); report `docs/maintenance/LOCK_OWNER_STRING_LOCALE_GROUPING_DEFEATS_MUTUAL_EXCLUSION_V1.md` (AIPR-20260815-COWORK-008) | **FIXED AND RE-PROVEN SAME SESSION at build `fe42666e` -- root-caused at `fb7106e0 dirty`, six source changes, cross-process refusal verified with both pids alive; regression test, locale gate, and AIF-113 recovery path all still OWED** | **MUTUAL EXCLUSION DOES NOT HOLD, DETERMINISTICALLY, ON EVERY WRITE PATH IN THE ENGINE.** Two `dottalkpp` processes, same data root, same table: A took `LOCK TABLE` and got `Table: LOCKED (owner GRIMWOOD:49,640:1,786,832,702,834)`; B then READ A's lock correctly and was granted the same lock anyway (`LOCK: table locked.`). Detection works, enforcement does not. **THE CHAIN, each link measured:** `make_owner_string` builds through a bare `std::ostringstream` and the writer does `f << "pid=" << pid`, neither imbued, so under a grouping locale the sidecar literally contains `pid=16,984` -- confirmed by reading the file off disk, not inferred; `read_lock_meta` parses it with `std::stoul(line.substr(4))`, which returns the longest valid prefix and **does not throw** on trailing junk, so the `catch` never fires and `meta.pid = 16` (verified by compiling and running `stoul("16,984")`); `is_pid_alive(16)` returns false; control reaches the stale branch, calls `force_remove`, and the lock is stolen. Only ONE `.lock` file survived the run, carrying B's pid -- the signature of the stale path having executed. The re-entrancy guard one line above compares `meta.owner` as a whole STRING, which is why same-process re-locking still behaves and why this only bites across processes, i.e. the exact case locks exist for. **THIS IS AN UNFINISHED AIF-031 SWEEP.** That lane is the same defect class already named ("no thousands grouping in stored values") and already swept across roughly twenty sites (`cmd_calc`, `cmd_replace`, `cmd_calcwrite`, `row_codec_fixed`, `fn_numeric`, `fn_string`, `shell_api`, `rhs_eval`, `date_utils`, `cmd_aggs`, `shell_eval_utils`, `cmd_replace_multi`), and `SESSION_CLOSEOUT_FIELDTYPE_CODEC_2026-07-19.md` records the follow-up as OPEN: "AIF-031 numeric-formatting sweep (intake task #100): audit other CLI/engine". `xbase_locks.cpp` is what that audit missed. The lesson worth keeping: **the severity of a defect class is not uniform across the sites it touches, and an audit scoped by "formatting" will not find the site where formatting is load-bearing.** **SEQUENCING -- THE TWO DEFECTS ARE MASKING EACH OTHER, DO NOT SHIP THE LOCALE FIX ALONE.** Nothing releases a lock except the explicit `UNLOCK` verb: `CLOSE`, `CLEAR`, `USE`/`OPEN`, `DbArea::close()`, `~DbArea()` and process exit all leave the sidecar in place (`release_held` remains called by nothing -- AIF-113). Leaked locks are currently being cleaned up BY the broken stale-detection, because every orphan looks stale for the same reason every live lock does. Repair the parse in isolation and orphans become permanently unreclaimable, with no exposed FORCE verb to clear them by hand. A single abandoned `LOCK TABLE` would then wedge that table for every process on the machine. **This raises AIF-113 from housekeeping to a blocking dependency, which was not its status when chartered.** **NO TEST EXISTS:** `grep -rln "try_lock_table\|try_lock_record\|LOCK TABLE\|xbase::locks" tests/` returns nothing -- not one test file references the lock subsystem, which is why an every-single-time defect survived in every committed version since `fecc3951e` (2026-07-14). Five cases owed with the fix: second process refused on a table lock; refused on a record lock; a genuinely-dead owner IS reclaimed; an unparseable owner FAILS CLOSED (presumed alive, the opposite of today); and pid round-trips byte-identical under a grouping locale -- that last one generalises and would also catch the next AIF-031 miss. **CAUSE LOCATED, SAME SESSION -- THE REMEDY MOVED.** `include/runtime/utf8_init.hpp:80`, called from `src/cli/main.cpp:233` as one of the first acts of `main()`: `std::locale::global(std::locale(""))`. **The intent was character encoding; `std::locale("")` is the WHOLE native locale and brings `numpunct` with it.** The function is named `init_utf8` and its comment reads "Locale only. Do not force stdout/stderr mode here" -- the author was reasoning about `codecvt`, and nothing in the name, comment or call site hints that it also changes how every integer in the process serialises. Landed **2025-08-31** in `d506195ce` "Alpha 5.0 shake-down baseline", ten and a half months before `xbase_locks.cpp` enters recorded history. **This vindicates the owner's recollection exactly: the lock code did not change, the ground under it did, in 2025, for an unrelated reason, in a header about console encoding.** It also explains AIF-031 in full -- twenty `imbue(classic())` calls are not a coding standard but damage control after a global locale was installed, applied wherever the damage happened to be noticed; `xbase_locks.cpp` was somewhere nobody looked, because nobody reads lock sidecars for pretty numbers. **WINDOWS-ONLY:** the `#else` branch installs `C.UTF-8` (classic `numpunct`, no grouping), so Linux/WSL builds write `pid=16984` and lock CORRECTLY; only the documented `en_US.UTF-8` fallback is exposed. Three consequences -- any regression for this MUST run on Windows (a green WSL suite proves nothing about it, and is a plausible reason it went uncaught); the two platforms have been running different locking semantics, one enforcing and one not, with no note anywhere saying so; and the broken configuration is the one in daily use here, `dottalk_bbsd` plus `dottalkpp` sharing a store on Windows. **REVISED REMEDY, cause before symptoms:** keep the UTF-8 facets and take the `numeric` category from classic -- `std::locale native(""); std::locale::global(std::locale(native, std::locale::classic(), std::locale::numeric));` -- one line that fixes every site in the process at once, including the twenty AIF-031 already patched and every future one. Argue three points first: it changes global numeric formatting (display sites wanting grouping must then imbue deliberately -- a search suggests there are none, since AIF-031 has been removing grouping wherever it appears); it changes PARSING as well as formatting, the direction that matters more and must be tested rather than assumed; and the existing `imbue` calls should STAY, becoming redundant rather than wrong. **The durable version is still a gate**, because a one-line fix at the install point is reversible by anybody who adds another `std::locale::global` for another good local reason: the rule worth enforcing is that no value serialised to disk may depend on the ambient locale, cheaply approximated by failing any new `locale::global` call not accompanied by a `numeric`-category override. **WHAT IT DID TO AIF-112:** Phase-1 Steps 1-3 completed and are reportable; Step 4 FAILS on the substrate, not on the ledger design; Steps 5-6 are unscoreable until enforcement holds. Sharpest consequence: Step 6's mandatory requirement was "EXPAT lease reclaim WITHOUT any force path", and under this defect `force_remove` runs on every acquisition inside `try_lock_*`, so a completed spike would have reported Step 6 GREEN with a force path executing underneath it. That is the second instance this session of the shape recorded in `proof.governance.availability_is_not_adoption` -- a green proof bar over an untouched hole -- arriving by a different route: there the wrong substrate, here the right substrate with an unverified property. **COUNTER-CASE WORTH STATING:** had AIF-112 kept its original SQLite ledger, this defect would still be sitting in the engine unfound; the DBF correction is what put the spike on top of `xbase_locks` at all. **ALL EIGHT AIF-112 PHASE-1 TEMPLATE SECTIONS SUBSEQUENTLY ANSWERED on the fixed build** (evidence return: `docs/maintenance/AIF112_PHASE1_EVIDENCE_AND_STEWARD_HANDOFF_4_V1.md`, AIPR-20260815-COWORK-009), including Step 6's mandatory "EXPAT lease reclaim WITHOUT any force path" -- unmeetable that morning, since `force_remove` ran inside every acquisition. **SCRIBE ERROR RECORDED RATHER THAN QUIETLY FIXED:** an interim report to the owner claimed "all six steps passed" when Step 4 AS SPECIFIED (ledger-level check-and-insert sharing one FLOCK scope) had not been run -- what had been proven was the FLOCK primitive BENEATH it. Caught by re-reading the steward's template, not by the runtime. Step 4 proper was then run as a script, twice unchanged, GRANTED then REFUSED. A proof process that cannot catch its own false positives is the thing this lane already has one registry entry about. Final ledger: the expired lease superseded with a release date and its history retained, the live lease held by a DIFFERENT holder, the whole transition inside one ordinary `LOCK TABLE`/`UNLOCK TABLE` pair. **BOTH DIRECTIONS OF FAIL-CLOSED VERIFIED**, which matters because an over-strict parser converts an enforcement fix into a permanent lock: a live foreign owner is refused, and a provably dead owner IS still reclaimed (a session quit while holding, both sidecars survived process death as section 10 predicted from source, and a fresh session reclaimed both and released them cleanly). METHOD NOTE: the reclaiming process reported pid `3844` where the dead owner had been `38444` -- the same digits less one. On a day spent chasing a number that lost its shape in transit that resemblance was measured, not assumed; `Get-Process` confirmed 3844, a genuine coincidence, both valid Windows pids. **"Expected" and "measured" were one command apart, and this lane exists because someone once settled for expected.** Secondary findings from the same run are tabled in the report (A3 a third surface for the APPEND BLANK catalog drift; A4/A5 permissions are compiled not seeded and `database.mutate` already suffices for Phase 1; C1 `DBAREA` renders X64 `I`/`T` as `Other`; D1 `CREATE` silently overwrites while `ERASE` demands CONFIRM -- inverted safety; E2 `UNLOCK` reports success on a record that was not locked; **F1 a `REPLACE` whose RHS fails to evaluate stores BLANK and reports SUCCESS** -- `DATEADD(TODAY, -2)` silently wrote nothing where `DATEADD(DATE(), -2)` wrote `20260813`, with an "invalid date for field" error path that exists and never fired, a data-integrity hazard for any ledger built on this surface). | AIF-117 | Silent predicate and store failures: a bare field used as a predicate is evaluated as "is this field non-blank", so `COUNT FOR SUP` matches every row whether SUP is T or F, and an unresolvable field name matches nothing and reads as a real answer; Cowork 2026-08-15, found by running the AIF-112 ledger, not by reading the code | engine_defect, data_integrity, expression_eval, predicate, silent_failure, error_surfacing, scan_selector, audit_correctness, gate_candidate, review_needed | `src/cli/expr/eval.cpp:21` (`FieldRef::eval`, the root cause); `include/cli/expr/ast.hpp:19` (`RecordView` -- only `get_field_str` + `get_field_num`, no type, no existence); `src/cli/expr/value_eval.cpp` (wraps any non-literal node as K_Bool without inspecting it, so the strict tail is never reached); `src/cli/scan_selector.cpp` `collect_selected_recnos` (**declares `std::string err`, passes it, never reads it**); sibling sites `cmd_replace.cpp` `validate_field_value_for_store` (F1), `cmd_unlock.cpp` (E2), APPEND_BLANK drift (A3) | claim `coordination/aif/AIF-117.claim` (run `COWORK-20260815-001`, lane `silent-predicate-and-store-failures`); charter `docs/maintenance/SILENT_PREDICATE_AND_STORE_FAILURES_LANE_V1.md` (AIPR-20260815-COWORK-010); discovery evidence `docs/maintenance/AIF112_PHASE1_EVIDENCE_AND_STEWARD_HANDOFF_4_V1.md` (AIPR-20260815-COWORK-009) | **ROOT-CAUSED TO FILE:LINE, NOT STARTED -- runtime-observed at `fe42666e dirty`, source read at `57c2d1634`; no source change proposed, R1 carries an undecided semantic fork** | **THE CAUSE IS EIGHT LINES.** `FieldRef::eval` fetches the field as a STRING and returns true if any character is non-whitespace -- that is the only truth test a bare field reference has. `SUP` holds the byte `T` or `F`, both non-blank, so every row matches; `NOSUCHFIELD` resolves to `""`, so no row matches and the function never asks whether the field exists. It reaches for non-blankness because `RecordView` (ast.hpp:19) exposes exactly two accessors, `get_field_str` and `get_field_num`, with NO type and NO existence check. MEASURED LIVE on a 5-row ledger with SUP true on three rows: `count for sup` -> 4 (wrong), `count for nosuchfield` -> 0 (wrong), `count for sup = T` -> 3 (right), `count for state = "held"` -> 2 (right), `? SUP` -> `T` then `F` (right). **That last pair is the shape of the problem: the expression layer reads the field correctly; only the bare-predicate path does not.** CANDIDATES ELIMINATED, recorded so nobody re-walks them: the predicate-chain fast path is NOT involved (`parse_cond` requires FIELD OP VALUE, a lone identifier fails at the operator and falls through); `logical_to_num` is CORRECT (T->1.0, F->0.0); logical STORAGE is correct (T/F on disk, dotted forms accepted and stripped by `normalize_logical_value`); `eval_bool`'s strict "must evaluate to logical/boolean" guard is correct and simply never reached, because `value_eval.cpp` wraps any non-literal node as a valid K_Bool before it gets there. **THE SECOND DEFECT IS ONE UNREAD VARIABLE:** `scan_selector.cpp` declares `std::string err`, passes its address to `eval_bool_compiled`, never reads it, and `continue`s on failure -- so an unusable predicate silently means NO ROWS MATCHED. This lives in the SHARED SELECTOR, so `LIST FOR`, `SUM` and every other selector-backed command inherit it, not just COUNT. **ONE DEFECT WITH FOUR FACES** (G1a bare logical, G1b unknown field, F1 a REPLACE whose RHS fails stores blank and reports success, E2 UNLOCK reports success on an unlocked record, A3 APPEND BLANK errors then clobbers): the lower layers usually DETECT the problem and the command layer DISCARDS the diagnosis and answers anyway. The contrast that makes it undeniable: `displaay` is refused by name, so the codebase CAN be strict -- it is currently strict about the typo that cannot hurt you and permissive about the ones that can. **WHY THIS IS NOT COSMETIC:** found while building a document-control ledger, where "how many items are still checked out" answered `0` because a column name was misspelled is not a wrong number, it is a wrong number that READS AS GOOD NEWS. REMEDY OPTIONS, NONE CHOSEN: R1 make `FieldRef::eval` type-aware -- the blunt one-line form (try `get_field_num` first) CHANGES CHARACTER-FIELD SEMANTICS because `get_field_num` soft-normalizes C fields, so a C field holding `"0"` flips true->false; the clean form adds `get_field_type` to `RecordView` and dispatches, giving every future AST node the type information it currently lacks. R2 read the `err` already being filled in `scan_selector`. R3 make an unresolvable field an error -- **but R3 DOES NOT WORK WITHOUT R2**, since `Expr::eval` returns a bare bool and a thrown error is caught by the selector and turned back into a silent row exclusion; sequence matters. R4 audit the sibling sites. R5 a gate: no command may discard an error out-parameter it passed, approximable by grepping declared-and-unread error strings at call sites. TIES: AIF-112 found it and is why it matters; AIF-116 is the same session's other defect and the same shape one layer down (a value that could not be trusted, and no gate); AIF-114 is the documentation face of "stated but not enforced". CANDIDATE SECOND ENTRY for `proof.governance.availability_is_not_adoption`: **this was found by an oracle the scribe had openly described as weak and hand-transcribed** -- an argument for the value of a second opinion even when the second opinion is a poor one. Owner `member.derald`; finder + steward `member.ai.claude.cowork`. |
