It was LEGACY-only, so it surfaced within minutes of LEGACY becoming the default preset, having been unreachable for the whole of its prior life; blast radius measured as nil (zero public headers consult it, only `src/cli`, which the binding never compiles) so the fix changed not one byte. (4) `DOTTALK_INDEX_MODE` LMDB, LEGACY and NONE ship a BYTE-IDENTICAL module (665336 B, sha256 `54cb15eb...`, three times) because `module.cpp` references the index zero times, so `libxindex.a` resolves nothing and the linker discards it, `lmdb_backend.o` included. **Instances 2, 3 and 4 are AIF-118's shape in the build system**, and instance 3's only symptom was a compiler warning riding a green exit code through a 52-step log. ROOT CONFIRMED AGAINST THE AI PORTAL on the owner's instruction: indexing has NEVER been tested through the binding -- the portal names pydottalk once, as a closeout FILENAME; the four `launcher_pydottalk` proof runs are 356-389 B with zero index hits; the sole registered pydottalk proof is APPEND BLANK. Owner, same day: "so far we have only used crud." REMEDY LANDED: standalone lean build anchored by `DOTTALK_ROOT`; `CMakePresets.json` for the binding with LEGACY as default (identical artifact, one fewer dependency) and the measurement recorded in each preset description; `DOTTALK_HAS_XINDEX` keyed off the MODE; the product chartered, registered and given this lane. OPEN: OI-002 dead `src/bindings/`, OI-003 `build.ps1 -PyOnly`, OI-005 execution route, OI-006 root-build PIC, OI-007 re-measure when an index API lands. Owner `member.derald`; finder + steward `member.ai.claude.cowork`. |
| AIF-120 | Application UI DSL: a published page called itself a lane with no claim, charter or intake row -- and the GUI it proposes building already exists; Cowork 2026-08-17 | dsl, ui, tui, gui, foxpro, turbovision, language_design, publication_drift, review_needed | `include/tv/foxtalk_app.hpp` and 13 sibling `foxtalk_*` headers; `include/palette/foxtalk_palette.hpp`; `include/foxref.hpp` (553 lines); `include/foxpro_go.hpp`; `include/foxpro_header.hpp`; `include/tv/foxtalk_menu.hpp` (`buildMenuBar(TRect)`); x64base-site `content/docs/dev/application-ui-dsl-lane.mdx` | claim `coordination/aif/AIF-120.claim` (run `COWORK-20260817-001`, lane `application-ui-dsl`); lane charter `docs/maintenance/APPLICATION_UI_DSL_LANE_V1.md` (AIPR-20260817-COWORK-006) | **PRECONDITIONS CLOSED 2026-08-19, review-needed; syntax still not started.** Both blocking gates are ruled: **R11** threading (gate 9) adopts the UI-thread contract already shipped in `src/gui/` and `docs/ui/`; **R12** coordinates (gate 8) chooses layout intent as primary with absolute geometry quarantined. **R13** adds a rule found only by WRITING the format: fields required on output that are optional on input. Runtime-proven: VFP 9 reads an x64base-written table, and opens, RUNS and round-trips an x64base-GENERATED `.SCX` (`tools/vfp/write_vfp_binary.py`). Evidence base is 24 hash-verified fixtures plus a 170-form external corpus at `D:\dev\vfp-corpus` (not vendored, licence undetermined). Still open: gate 10 (the design table as a standalone contract, the actual deliverable), gate 11 (a second backend from the TABLE), no syntax, no parser, no registry entries, and `RESERVED4` undecoded. Rulings R1-R13 all review-needed; the author does not self-approve. Documents: `AIF120_THREADING_RULING_V1.md`, `AIF120_COORDINATE_RULING_V1.md`, `AIF120_GENERATED_SCX_ACCEPTED_V1.md`, `AIF120_CORPUS_SCAN_V1.md`, `AIF120_VCX_SPECIMEN_V1.md`, `AIF120_STUDENTS_SCX_SPECIMEN_V1.md`, `AIF120_VFP_READS_X64BASE_OUTPUT_V1.md`, `AIF120_LANE_STATUS_AND_FIXTURES_V1.md`, `AIF120_VFP_SCX_EMPIRICAL_BASELINE_V1.md`. | **THE PUBLISHED PAGE UNDERSTATES WHAT EXISTS AND OVERSTATES WHAT IS TRACKED.** Measured 2026-08-17: the public README says the lane is "tracked here", and there were ZERO intake rows, ZERO charters and NO claim file -- the word "lane" was doing work it had not earned, which in this project means a claimed number, a charter, an intake row and coordination. Meanwhile the target surface is BUILT: FoxTalk is a working Turbo Vision application layer (14 headers) with a FoxPro palette, and `include/foxref.hpp` is a 553-line FoxPro-to-DotTalk reference catalogue. What does NOT exist is the LANGUAGE: `DEFINE WINDOW`, `DEFINE MENU`, `DEFINE POPUP`, `DEFINE BAR` and `ON SELECTION` appear NOWHERE in src/, include/ or docs/, and `include/tv/foxtalk_menu.hpp` exposes exactly one function -- `TMenuBar* buildMenuBar(TRect bounds)` -- so menus are hardcoded C++. **So the lane is not "build a GUI", it is "give the GUI that exists a language"**, which is a far smaller and better-bounded problem. MAINTAINER'S PROPOSAL, and the charter agrees: use the old FoxPro window/menu syntax as the source vocabulary, for three structural reasons rather than nostalgia -- (1) it is NAME-BASED, not handle-based (`DEFINE WINDOW cust` then `ACTIVATE WINDOW cust`), so the script never holds an object, handle or sizer and nothing of the target's model leaks in, where Turbo Vision would leak an ownership tree, Win32 would leak HWNDs and wx would leak layout objects; (2) it is statement-oriented and flat, which DotScript already is, so there is no new evaluation model to invent; (3) it is already half-catalogued in `foxref.hpp` and the TUI it would drive is already dressed as FoxPro. A full FoxPro/TurboVision/Win32 mapping matrix is in the charter and the correspondence is close enough on all three columns that one source form is realistic. **THE DISAGREEMENTS ARE THE LANE, AND THERE ARE THREE.** (a) COORDINATES, the fork that decides everything else: `@ row,col` is character-cell geometry and Turbo Vision is ALSO character-cell, which is exactly the trap -- the easiest target will silently make the language TUI-shaped and every GUI backend afterwards will fight it, so the coordinate model must be ruled on BEFORE any syntax is fixed (keep cells and scale / abstract units with cells as a backend / declare layout intent and derive cells). (b) `READ` is a modal cycle over pending `GET`s -- maps beautifully to `execView` and `DialogBox`, and not at all to a web or event-driven surface. (c) FoxPro has NO layout manager, which is (a) seen from the other end. GATES: the seven from the published seed, plus two added here -- a recorded coordinate-model ruling, and a SECOND BACKEND SPIKED (not shipped), because without it a passing TUI proof says nothing about portability and the vocabulary could be Turbo Vision in a FoxPro hat. STOPPING RULE on the AIF-119 model: if a construct cannot be expressed without exposing the target's object model to the script, it does not belong in v1 -- hiding that model is the entire argument for the syntax. NOT IN SCOPE: building GUI surfaces; wx, Arctic and web are existing or separately chartered lanes and this one emits INTO them. HOUSEKEEPING INHERITED: the public README's "tracked here" sentence pointed at nothing until this claim, and `edu_eco` is in the identical published-but-unchartered state and should be ruled on at the same time. Owner `member.derald`; steward `member.ai.claude.cowork`. |
| AIF-121 | USE silently ignores arguments it does not understand, so the documented `USE <table> IN <n>` clause is swallowed and the table opens into the CURRENT area, destroying its occupant with no message; Cowork 2026-08-22, found by a probe that was looking for something else | engine_defect, silent_failure, argument_validation, doc_defect, foxpro_parity, data_loss, use_command, aif118_shape, review_needed | `src/cli/cmd_use.cpp` (`contains_noindex` :176, `contains_again` :200, `parse_alias_clause` :226 -- three non-consuming scans, nothing enumerates the tail; AGAIN lands in the current area :743); `src/cli/command_argchk.cpp` :47-48 (the CMDREL recipe, printed in every REGRESSION ALL run); `src/cli/fox_standard_catalog.cpp` :78 (`USE TEACHERS IN 2`, advertised as FoxPro-standard) | claim `coordination/aif/AIF-121.claim` (run `COWORK-20260822-001`, lane `use-argument-validation`) | **RUNTIME-MEASURED, NOT STARTED -- both legs reproduced live at build `baada444 dirty`; no source change yet; parse shape agreed, `IN FREE` ruled** | **THE PARSER CANNOT SEE UNKNOWN TOKENS, BY CONSTRUCTION.** `cmd_use.cpp` reads its tail with THREE independent non-consuming scans -- each saves the stream position, sweeps for its own keyword, rewinds. Nothing ever enumerates the tail as a whole, so no token is ever unaccounted for. Unknown arguments are not ignored by oversight; nothing is in a position to notice them. MEASURED, two commands: `USE dbf\x64\students` then `USE dbf\x32\students IN 1` -> both report `Opened`, `WORKSPACE REGISTRY` reports **members 1**, and `AREA` shows slot 0 holding the x32 table. The second open replaced the first and printed nothing. The membership registry (AIF-078 stage 2) is what made it visible -- it reported one area because one area was the truth. **THE HOUSE'S OWN RECIPE CANNOT WORK.** `command_argchk.cpp` prints `USE help\commands IN 1` / `USE help\cmd_args IN 0` / `SELECT 1` / `INDEX ON CMDKEY TAG CMDP` in every REGRESSION ALL run. Run verbatim: both tables land in area 0, `SELECT 1` lands on an empty area, and INDEX dies with `No table is currently open.` INDEX is area-current by design (owner, same session: "you have to be in the area to create indexes"), so the recipe is not wrong on one line -- without `IN <n>` it is structurally unbuildable. `fox_standard_catalog.cpp:78` separately advertises the clause as FoxPro-standard, which it is; it was never implemented here. **SAME SHAPE AS THE SIBLING DEFECT ONE CLAUSE OVER:** `AGAIN` opens its second cursor into the CURRENT area (:743), so it too destroys the occupant unless the caller SELECTs a free area first. USE_AGAIN's spec selects first every time, which is why the property was exercised around rather than tested. `IN <n>` is therefore not a companion to AGAIN, it is the half AGAIN shipped without. OWNER RULINGS 2026-08-22: `IN FREE`, not `IN NEXT` -- NEXT implies forward adjacency and the allocator reuses the LOWEST free slot, so the name would promise an order the code does not keep; and AGAIN/IN compose with no interaction rule, because AGAIN carries no placement opinion to arbitrate (`target = has_IN ? n : current`). `IN 0` is area 0 literally -- no magic zero, per the same session's 0-based ruling. REMEDY, agreed shape: replace the three scanners with ONE pass that consumes and classifies every token and refuses anything unrecognized BY NAME -- the gate then cannot be forgotten, because the parser is required to account for every token, and a fifth clause later cannot reopen the hole. `USE <table> [IN <n>\|FREE] [AGAIN] [ALIAS <name>] [NOINDEX\|NOIDX]`. NOT YET MEASURED, and it gates the change: the blast radius of refusing unknown tokens across the script and doc corpus -- a stray token after USE anywhere starts erroring where it used to open. TIES: AIF-118 (a check that answers the same for absent and fine -- this is that shape in an argument parser, and it recurred inside `next_aif.py`, the tool written the same hour to prevent AIF collisions, whose first regex could not see a two-digit number); AIF-117 (silent predicate and store failures, the same discard-the-diagnosis family); AIF-078 stage 2 (the registry that detected it). The house rule stated this session -- all commands and functions should validate field names -- is this one level out: validate ARGUMENTS. Owner `member.derald`; finder + steward `member.ai.claude.cowork`. |
| AIF-122 | One edit rebuilds every .cpp: the git SHA is applied as a `-D` to every translation unit, so any commit recompiles ~400 files; plus two smaller configure-churn mechanisms; Cowork 2026-08-22, raised by the steward from the build loop itself ("your rebuilds are usually costly, a single change and I am rebuilding every single .cpp") | build_system, cmake, rebuild_cost, build_stamp, configure_depends, generated_source, measurement, aif079_shape, review_needed | `CMakeLists.txt` (:394-404 the deleted `dottalk_apply_version_metadata`; :646-651 the `CONFIGURE_DEPENDS` registration; the new `version_stamp` `configure_file`); `src/CMakeLists.txt` (:484-496 the deleted `file(WRITE)` stub generation); `include/dottalk/version.hpp`; new `config/version_stamp.hpp.in`; new `src/cli/cmd_palette_stub.cpp`; `src/palette/cmd_palette_shim.cpp` (sec 7, reported not fixed) | claim `coordination/aif/AIF-122.claim` (run `COWORK-20260822-001`, lane `build-stamp-rebuild-cost`); ruling `docs/maintenance/AIF122_BUILD_STAMP_REBUILD_COST_RULING_V1.md` (AIPR-20260822-COWORK-112) | **LANDED, review-needed -- three mechanisms measured and fixed; MSVC build + REGRESSION ALL still OWED, and the decisive new evidence is the SECOND build: commit anything, rebuild, count compiled files, expect 2 not ~400** | **THE STEWARD'S HYPOTHESIS WAS CLOSE AND THE CORRECTION IS THE FINDING: not two stamps disagreeing, ONE STAMP ON FOUR HUNDRED COMMAND LINES.** `dottalk_apply_version_metadata()` put `DOTTALKPP_GIT_SHA` and three siblings on the compiler invocation of every TU in every target, via `dottalk_apply_common_settings`. A changed SHA changes every command line, so a one-line doc commit recompiles the tree -- observed twice, `83f5032e` -> `3908abed`, for change sets of 8 and 4 files. **The readership is TWO FILES:** the macros are consumed only by `include/dottalk/version.hpp`, whose includers are `src/cli/cmd_version.cpp` and `src/gui/wx/main_frame.cpp`. Four hundred TUs carried a value two of them read. **THE MEASUREMENT THAT SEPARATED THE CAUSES:** with HEAD unmoved (SHA identical) and the regression suite just run, a reconfigure fired and compiled **1** file -- so reconfigure is nearly free and the SHA was the whole bill. **THAT ONE FILE PROVED THE MECHANISM BY ACCIDENT:** it was `cmd_palette_stub.cpp`, generated by `file(WRITE)`, which is NOT copy-if-different and rewrote identical bytes with a fresh mtime every configure -- while the SAME configure regenerated `build_vectors.hpp` through `configure_file` (copy-if-different) at a cost of ZERO. One log, two CMake commands, 1 against 0, which is the entire argument for the fix. The steward then searched Explorer and surfaced two more facts: the generated body was a CMake BRACKET argument (`[=[ ... ]=]`, no variable expansion) containing nothing to expand -- **a constant, generated** -- and **thirteen copies existed across eight build trees at TWO byte counts**, 219 in Windows trees and 213 in WSL ones, because `file(WRITE)` writes native line endings. **THIRD MECHANISM: VERIFYING THE BUILD DIRTIED THE BUILD.** 254 of the 257 `CMAKE_CONFIGURE_DEPENDS` entries are runtime data under `dottalkpp/data/`, including the memo sidecar `dottalkpp/data/dbf/x64/STUDENTS.dtx` that the regression suite writes; CMake named it as the trigger every time, and it was measured moving 12:22:02 -> 12:36:10 across one `REGRESSION RUN`. **REMEDIES, all landed:** the stamp becomes `generated/dottalk/version_stamp.hpp` via `configure_file`, included behind `__has_include` so `version.hpp` still stands alone, and the metadata function is DELETED rather than emptied (an emptied function with two live call sites is the AIF-079 shape, now six instances); the palette stub becomes a tracked source file under `src/cli`, caught by the existing `GLOB_RECURSE`; the data file list comes off `CONFIGURE_DEPENDS` while the manifest and its reader stay. **VERIFIED BEHAVIOURALLY, not just syntactically:** the stamp path compiled and RUN prints `v0.6 (2026-08-22, 3908abed dirty)`, byte-identical to the real banner, and the fallback path prints `v0.0-unconfigured (Aug 22 2026)` -- degrading to an implausible version rather than a plausible lie, as `version.hpp`'s own comment demands. **A CORRECTION TO MY OWN FRAMING, same session:** before measuring I told the steward the three mechanisms "compound" into full-tree cost; measured, one costs 13 seconds, one costs a single file, and the third costs everything. The compounding story would have sent the fix at the wrong file -- the house's own measure-the-claim rule catching its author. **TIES:** R118 fixed the `dirty` flag's blindness to untracked files and left the SHA half unclaimed; this is that number, and the stamp staleness and the rebuild cost turn out to be **the same line of CMake seen from two ends** -- captured at configure time so it goes stale, applied per-TU so refreshing it rebuilds everything. One narrow generated header addresses both. **FOUND, NOT FIXED (sec 7):** `src/palette/cmd_palette_shim.cpp` defines a real `cmd_PALETTE` forwarding to `cmd_FOX_PALETTE`, lives in an excluded directory, and the only CMake reference to that basename is a prune entry for a different, NONEXISTENT path -- compiled by nothing. Checked because a second `cmd_PALETTE` would have been a duplicate-symbol error; it is not, and the check is the finding. Owner `member.derald`; finder + steward `member.ai.claude.cowork`. |
| AIF-123 | SET DELETED is honoured by some read paths and not others, so "is this record visible" has different answers depending on which verb asks; Cowork 2026-08-24, found when a WORKSPACE PURGE spec arm asserted the opposite and inverted on its first run | engine_defect, silent_failure, deleted_records, set_deleted, read_paths, locate, r5_shape, measured_one_path_only | `src/xbase/dbf_file.cpp:439` `deleteCurrent()` sets `_del = IS_DELETED` and writes; `src/xbase/record_view.cpp:178` `isDeleted()` reads `_recbuf[0]`; NOTHING between them filters -- no skip in `gotoRec`, and `recCount64()` counts flagged rows. MEASURED for LOCATE: with deleted visibility HIDE (ON) the shell printed `Located.` and moved the cursor onto a purged row (`WSPURGE` arm `PG_T3`, 2026-08-24). The flag HAD landed -- `purge_durable_workspace` re-reads `isDeleted()` and aborts otherwise, and the verb reported both rows purged. | OPEN -- one fixture, one deleted row, every read verb | **MEASURED FOR `LOCATE` ONLY, and the scope is the point.** `LIST`, `SMARTLIST`, `COUNT`, the FOR-scan path and `SEEK` are UNMEASURED and nothing is claimed about them either way. `SMARTLIST DELETED` exists as a documented form, which implies at least one consumer DOES distinguish deleted rows -- so this is very likely a PER-PATH INCONSISTENCY rather than a setting that does nothing, and that is the shape worth the lane: one question, *is this record visible*, with possibly different answers by verb. R5, in the scan paths. WHAT IT ALREADY COST: `WORKSPACE PURGE` was designed on the belief that the delete flag HIDES a purged row, with `SUPERSEDED=1` merely stopping the catalog scan electing it. The first half is false, so the design's rationale was corrected in four places (function comment, verb output, spec summary, spec header) -- the verb itself is unaffected because its safety rests on `SUPERSEDED` and on flagged rows still being COUNTED toward `max(WS_ID)`. SHAPE OF THE WORK: one fixture table, one deleted row, and every read verb run over it -- bounded, and it converts a suspicion into a table. Finding: `claude/FINDING_SET_DELETED_DOES_NOT_HIDE_FROM_LOCATE.md`. Tripwire already in place: `WSPURGE` `PG_T3` asserts the MEASURED behaviour, so if `LOCATE` ever gains delete-filtering the arm reds and says so rather than quietly starting to pass for a new reason (the IDXSTALE precedent). |
| AIF-124 | A workspace posture is TWO things wearing one file: the canonical DEFINITION (which tables, which indexes, which relations, which keys) and the SESSION STATE (where each cursor stood, which area was current). Nothing separates them, so a definition cannot be diffed or version-controlled without churning on state that legitimately changes every save; Cowork 2026-08-24, raised by the owner from a discussion about whether two posture saves could be compared byte for byte -- "canonical workspace vs workspace state, we need to make those two different things, kinda like vmware but simpler" | file_format, workspace, dtschema, canonical_vs_state, sidecar, diffability, migration, two_writers_disagree | `src/cli/cmd_workspace.cpp` emits EIGHT line kinds -- `DTSHEMA`, `FLAVOR`, `DBFROOT`/`IDXROOT`/`LMDBROOT`, `AREA`, `RELATION`, `KEY`, and then `CURSOR <area> <recno>` (:1861) and `CURRENT` (:1866), the last two carrying a comment that already names them "Session state (owner requirement 2026-08-11)". `src/gui/core/session.cpp:2250` writes `AREA` and `RELATION` AND NOTHING ELSE -- so the two writers have silently disagreed about what a posture IS, and the GUI has been writing canonical-only files the whole time | OPEN -- taxonomy first, then the sidecar | **THE DISTINCTION IS ALREADY HALF-DRAWN IN WORKING CODE AND WAS NEVER MADE STRUCTURAL, AND THE ANSWER TURNED OUT TO BE A NAMING ACT RATHER THAN A FILE FORMAT.** **v2 IS THE CANONICAL WORKSPACE SCHEMA; v3 IS THE SESSION** (owner, 2026-08-24). Both artifacts ALREADY EXIST and are ALREADY CORRECT: `CURSOR`/`CURRENT` and the `FLAVOR`/`DBFROOT`/`IDXROOT`/`LMDBROOT` roots are gated behind `version == 3` (cmd_workspace.cpp:1790, :1850) and the default is 2 -- so `WORKSPACE SAVE <file>` has been writing canonical-only, portable, relative-path files all along, and `... V3` writes the session. Confirmed live 2026-08-24: a v2 save reads `DTSHEMA 2` / `AREA 0 | dbf=STUDENTS.dbf` / `RELATION STUDENTS ENROLL ON SID` -- no absolute paths, no cursor. THE FORMAT IS ALSO SELF-DESCRIBING AT EVERY LAYER: `DTSHEMA 2`/`3` is line one and the loader knows 3 is a superset of 2 (:2100-2102); the catalog carries the kind in its `FMT` field (:3032); and `WORKSPACE LOAD` prints a cursor count only for a session (:2310). **A SIDECAR WAS PROPOSED AND WITHDRAWN, and so was a follow-on `ENV`/`STATE` clause design** -- both were grammar and filing invented to house a category (machine-specific path roots) that does not exist: a session HAPPENED ON A MACHINE, so where it resolved from is part of what makes it a session, and the roots are already in the right artifact. The withdrawn designs and the reasoning that killed them are recorded in claude/AIF124_DESIGN_POSTURE_TAXONOMY.md rather than deleted. **WHAT REMAINS IS SMALL AND IS NOT STARTED:** the two names are written down nowhere a user looks -- the help (cmd_workspace.cpp:89-91) documents `MEMO [V3]` as a bare flag, never says what V3 MEANS, and does not mention that `WORKSPACE SAVE <file> V3` is accepted for a plain file too (the parser strips the keyword regardless, :5049-5057). Optional sugar: accept `SESSION` as a spelling of `V3`, since the version number is an implementation fact and 'session' is the meaning. **BYTE STABILITY IS REJECTED AS A REQUIREMENT FOR A POSTURE -- owner ruling 2026-08-24, "reject byte stable except for minidb".** The steward raised it as an open tension: every posture carries `WSID F<utc-stamp>` on line 2, injected by `stamp_ws_id()` WRAPPING the serializer (:1937) rather than written by it -- so two saves of an unchanged workspace differ on line 2. IT IS NOT A TENSION. A posture is the record of an INSTANCE, instances have identities, and the identity differs per save because it IS a different save. Diffability means the CANONICAL BODY is stable, which it is; it never meant the file was. **WHERE BYTE STABILITY DOES APPLY, AND ONLY THERE: MINIDB**, which is a different carrier with a different job -- `DTSHEMA 2/3 carry a posture, MINIDB 1 carries the table BYTES` (:128). For a byte carrier byte-identity is the entire point, and it is already enforced: WORKSPACE WRITEBACK does an oracle re-read plus byte-compare on every landed file. RECORDED BECAUSE THE STEWARD IMPORTED THE ASSUMPTION TWICE -- first proposing to verify a consolidation by diffing two posture saves, corrected by the owner on the spot ("I'm not sure where byte for byte came from except maybe minidb testing, of course .dtschemas mutate"), then again as this open issue. The correction is the durable part: byte-identity is a property of the BYTE CARRIER, not of every serialized artifact, and reaching for it elsewhere is a standard borrowed from the wrong layer. WSID therefore STAYS, in the canonical file, unremarked. NOTE `DTSHEMA` is misspelled in the shipped header with its own finding (claude/AIF120_FINDING_DTSHEMA_NONE_SENTINEL.md); this lane must not silently fix it -- every posture on disk carries the misspelling, and fixing the writer without the reader makes them unreadable. STATUS 2026-08-24: DOCUMENTED, NOT DONE. Zero code. Claim and this row are the only tree artifacts. Owner `member.derald`; steward `member.ai.claude.cowork`. |
| AIF-125 | Grep does not scale as the tree grows, and an agent navigating this repo has been using it for DISCOVERY when the project already carries its own command registry as x64base tables; Cowork 2026-08-24, raised by the owner -- "grep is becoming a weak point as the project grows, you need a neural network view... we already have source code indexing and usage contracts in each cmd file??? we need to work smarter, using dotscript features to feed you your data to disk" | agent_navigation, metadata, syscmd, index, dogfood, staleness, prior_art, tooling | `dottalkpp/data/metadata/` holds a RELATABLE STAR SCHEMA with two spines: SYSCMD (212 rows, CAN_NAME + HANDLER) and SYSFUNC (75); SYSSUBCMD.PARENT, SYSARGS.OWNER_NAM and SYSENTVAR.CAN_TARG all join to CAN_NAME, with OWNER_KND disambiguating command from function. SYSFLDDIC is the data dictionary. `SET RELATIONS ADD` wires the whole graph with no new code -- the engine indexes and relates its own metadata | PART 1 DONE (command->source hop); DRIFT NOW MEASURED (see notes) and the import gate is actionable; the graph walk, the SRC_FILE column and the freshness gate remain OPEN | **THE INDEX EXISTS AND IS ACCURATE; IT IS INCOMPLETE AND STALE, AND BOTH ARE MEASURABLE.** MEASURED 2026-08-24: 205 of 212 SYSCMD rows resolve to a source file across 185 files, and ZERO handlers are named that the tree does not define -- the registry never lies about a handler it records. SEVEN ROWS ARE ACTIVE WITH A BLANK HANDLER and they are omissions AT THE CENTRE: `USE`, `SELECT`, `CLOSE`, `BBS`, `NET`, `STUDENTECHO`, `STUDENTHELLO`. Omitting beats misleading, but USE and SELECT are the two most fundamental verbs in xBase. **STALENESS IS STRATIFIED AND SELF-REPORTING.** Every DBF header carries its own generation date in bytes 1-3 -- a freshness stamp for free, needing no VER_AT column (SYSCMD has none): SYSFUNC 7 days, SYSCMD/SYSSUBCMD/SYSARGS 28 (the 2026-07-27 full-stack push), SYSHELP/SYSENTVAR/SYSFLDDIC/SYSMSG 112 -- and SYSMSG is EMPTY. Owner's caveat, which is the governing rule for this lane: "the truth will be as old as the latest fullstack document push". **SO STALENESS IS MEASURED IN CHANGE, NOT DAYS.** 78 command files changed since SYSCMD was generated, against 212 rows -- AT MOST 37% unverified, an UPPER BOUND on drift and not measured drift, since a commit touching a file need not change its CAN_NAME or HANDLER. This matters because AIF-101 measured weekly commits going from single digits to 82/178/217: **the staleness budget shrinks as the project accelerates**, so a day-count means less each month while a change-count does not. It also gives the full-stack automation a CONTRACT rather than a schedule -- keep the unverified fraction under N% -- which self-tightens as velocity rises. **THE RULE: NAVIGATE, NEVER ASSERT.** This is the existing house rule (a cached copy for NARRATIVE, never for a FACT about the tree) applied to a new artifact. Grep is not replaced, it is DEMOTED TO VERIFICATION -- it was doing discovery and verification both, and it is poor at the first and good at the second. **DRIFT MEASURED 2026-08-24, AND IT IS ACTIONABLE.** `metacollect --syscmd-import-out` run against today's tree yields **228 candidate rows against 212 live**: 16 commands the catalog does not know exist, **ZERO live rows the extraction no longer finds**, and 7 rows where the live HANDLER is BLANK and the candidate carries the symbol. **THE ZERO IS THE IMPORTANT NUMBER.** The candidate is a STRICT SUPERSET of the live catalog, which is exactly the property `tools/fullstack_docs/export_syscmd_mirror.ps1` exists to check -- it records a past candidate that "is NOT a superset of the live 203 -- importing it would add 28 rows and silently drop 15 deliberate maintainer entries". That data-loss shape does NOT apply to this candidate: every live row survives. That is what makes the reviewed import gate worth firing rather than merely overdue. **CROSS-CHECKED BY TWO INDEPENDENT METHODS.** The 7 blank-handler rows -- `USE`, `SELECT`, `CLOSE`, `BBS`, `NET`, `STUDENTECHO`, `STUDENTHELLO` -- were found first by `tools/coordination/cmd_source_index.py` reading the DBF directly, and again hours later by metacollect's own reflection, which supplies the correct symbol for each (`cmd_USE`, `cmd_SELECT`, ...). Two methods, same seven; not a tool agreeing with itself. Among the 16 missing are real user-facing commands -- `DDICT` (the data-dictionary inspector the catalog has never heard of), `EVALDIFF` (already in `dotref.hpp`), `APPGUI`, `SMTP`, `SMARTBROWSER`. **NOT 16 CLEAN ADDS, and the difference matters.** `SET RELATION` and `SET UNIQUE` carry spaces and ALREADY EXIST in SYSSUBCMD as `PARENT=SET`; importing them into SYSCMD blind would create double entries. Whether a qualified subcommand belongs in both tables is a MODELLING DECISION, not a gap. The unqualified `SETCASE`/`SETCDX`/`SETFILTER` family are distinct real handlers and look like genuine adds. Actionable shape: ~7 blank handlers to fill (pure repair, no risk), ~10 genuine new commands, ~6 needing a taxonomy call first. **NEGATIVE RESULT WORTH KEEPING: `metacollect --compare` IS NOT A DRIFT INSTRUMENT.** Run with `--metadata-root`, it emitted 192 WARN rows -- 189 METADATA_ONLY and 3 SOURCE_ONLY -- and NONE are drift. It compares SYSCMD against `src/cli/command_catalog.cpp`, the CURATED DOC subset, so the 189 restate per-row what the prepush gate already prints in one line ("command_catalog curated-doc coverage: 25/239 registered commands -- curated subset by design"). The 3 SOURCE_ONLY (`SET FILTER`, `SET INDEX`, `SET ORDER`) are FALSE POSITIVES: all three exist in SYSSUBCMD with those exact QUAL_NAMEs, and the comparator only consults SYSCMD. The Gate-5 record calling `--compare` optional was right. Candidate-versus-live is the measure; `--compare` is not. **AND `cmdhelp build` CANNOT REFRESH THE CATALOG, BY CONSTRUCTION.** Measured after running it: SYSCMD's header date did not move. CMDHELP writes only `dottalkpp/data/help/`; the strings SYSCMD / SYSFUNC / SYSARGS appear ZERO times in its write chain. The two pipelines read the same `@dottalk.usage` contracts and then diverge permanently. SYSCMD is fed by `metacollect --syscmd-import-out` or `tools/fullstack_docs/generate_syscmd.py --write` -> a CSV plus a SEED `.dts` the engine runs. So the catalog is not stale because the pipeline is slow: **the last stage is a manual reviewed gate that has never fired.** The 2026-08-05 candidates (`runs/DOCFLUSH-20260805-001/metacollect_phase/`, decision `PASS_CANDIDATE_ONLY`) have sat unimported for 19 days. This bounds the owner's automation thesis precisely: automating the full-stack push makes CANDIDATES fresher and moves catalog freshness not at all unless the import gate is inside the automation -- or stays manual by design, in which case freshness is bounded by REVIEW CADENCE, not tooling. **COMMIT-RECORD REPAIR: `83cf2c250` CARRIES SIX FILES ITS MESSAGE DOES NOT DESCRIBE, and the explanation is recorded here because the commit is pushed to a shared branch and amending published history is worse than an incomplete message.** Both `git add` lines ran before the first `git commit`, so one commit took two changesets: the five AIF-125 paths its message covers, plus six unrelated files. THE SIX ARE ONE DEFECT CLASS -- statements the tree made about itself that had stopped being true and that no test could falsify: (a) `src/datadict/{ddict_catalog_paths, ddict_dbf_reader, ddict_object_resolver, ddict_read_helpers}.cpp` carried the banner "DD-089C extraction preview only. This generated candidate is not installed or wired by DD-089C", which stopped being true the day the wiring landed. They are compiled THREE WAYS -- `src/CMakeLists.txt`'s GLOB_RECURSE, the DD-089H block re-adding four of them BY NAME via `target_sources`, and root `CMakeLists.txt` linking two into the `dt_meta` library `metacollect` uses. The build carrying the fix printed the proof: all four compiled TWICE, into `dottalkpp` and `dottalk_gui_core`. (b) `tools/staging/prepush_gate.py` runs at COMMIT time, not push time -- `.git/hooks/pre-commit` invokes it, `.git/hooks/pre-push` does not. The wiring is CORRECT (a change set refused before it is ever a commit is stopped in the better place); only the name misleads, and the name is historical because the AI_PORTAL rule it enforces is called the Pre-Push Gate and the gate moved earlier than its rule's name did. NOT renamed -- the name is cited by that rule and by run records -- corrected in the docstring, which is also the only place inside the repository where the wiring can be documented at all, `.git/hooks/` being untracked. (c) `src/cli/cmd_workspace.cpp`'s help listed `MEMO [V3]` as a bare flag and never said what V3 MEANS; it now carries AIF-124's answer (v2 = canonical schema, v3 = session, what each holds), that `V3` is accepted for a PLAIN FILE and not only the MEMO form, and the ruling that a posture is not byte-stable and is not meant to be. FIVE INSTANCES OF THIS CLASS SURFACED ON 2026-08-24 -- these three, plus a `src/tests/CMakeLists.txt` comment describing pinned defects long since fixed and repointed, plus an AIF-124 intake row still recommending a sidecar design withdrawn an hour after it was written. EVERY ONE WAS FOUND BY READING, NONE BY A GATE, which is the standing argument for the cross-file citation gate in the open items -- the only member of this class a machine could catch. ALSO RECORDED: adopting a long-untracked file is NOT free. Staging one makes EVERY line an added line, so `FULLSTACK_DOCUMENTATION_FLUSH_COMPLETE_HANDOFF_V1.md` failed `house-style` on 16 em-dashes it has carried since July. It was DROPPED from the commit rather than normalised: it is a RUN RECORD, and rewriting historical evidence to satisfy a style gate is the wrong trade when the advisory it would silence was never blocking. The citation stands and is accurate; the advisory reports a true fact. Owner `member.derald`; steward `member.ai.claude.cowork`. |
| AIF-126 | Nine percent of the HELP store could not be retrieved by any operator, and the store had been reporting the reason in every capture this lane ever took; Cowork 2026-08-25, found while re-validating Gate 4 of flush v5 -- the owner asked "have you/can we resolve shared msg" after the count was measured | help_store, join, r5, r6, silent_truncation, pseudo_memo, dogfood, x64, dbase3, preview_column, instrument_over_inference | `src/help/helpdata_export_dbf.cpp` builds three tables from ONE artifact list: HELP_TOPIC (:346) and HELP_SECTION (:419) derive the topic key through `topic_key_for()`, HELP_LINE (:630) read the raw `artifact.cmdkey`. `helpdata_artifacts.cpp:75-88` deliberately CLEARS `command` and `cmdkey` on a shared message and puts the identity in the OWNER, which only `topic_key_for` knows -- its last branch falls through to `owner_to_string()` (`helpdata_model.cpp:36-49`), returning exactly `GLOBAL`, `COMMAND:<n>`, `SUBSYSTEM:<n>`, `MINER:<n>` | RESOLVED and runtime-proven; four follow-on questions OPEN (see notes) | **SHARED_MSG WAS 2,637 HELP_LINE ROWS WITH A BLANK TOPICKEY -- EVERY ROW.** With MINER:SOURCE's 120 that is 2,757 unreachable lines, 9.4% of 29,206, FROZEN AT EXACTLY 139 HEADERS across six backup stores from 2026-08-05 to 2026-08-24 and five full rebuilds. **THE STORE SAID SO IN PLAIN SIGHT.** `CMDHELP SOURCE` printed `SHARED_MSG [lines=2637, topics=0]` in the 08-13 baseline, the 08-21 capture and the 08-24 capture -- a bucket with 2,637 lines and ZERO topics beside seven buckets with positive counts. Three of this steward's own validation records quote the table it sits in. Nobody asked the instrument the question it was already answering. **CAUSE IS R5: ONE KEY, COMPUTED TWO WAYS, IN ONE FILE.** Headers got a real key; their lines got the empty string; `cmdhelp.cpp:1176` drops them with `if (!topic.empty())` and the guard is CORRECT -- given a blank key there is nothing else it could do. That is R6: 2,757 rows present in the table and absent from every surface, with no count anywhere reporting the difference. CMDHELPCHK reported `OK no structural issues found` over exactly this condition, because it checks ONE TABLE AT A TIME and the defect lives in the JOIN. **THE FIX IS ONE LINE** -- `row.topickey = topic_key_for(artifact)` -- and its output was already on disk: HELP_TOPIC is a written record of what that helper returns for these artifacts. `topic_key_for` returns `cmdkey` unchanged when set, so every row that had a key keeps it; the change can only fill blanks. VERIFIED: reachable topics 526 -> 665, blank keys 2,757 -> 0, orphan headers 139 -> 0, topic-set diff **+139 GAINED, 0 LOST**. Zero LOST is the load-bearing half -- it is what would have falsified the reasoning. RETRIEVAL PROVEN END TO END: `CMDHELP COMMAND:AUTODBF` renders 35 rows, every failure AUTODBF can emit, on one page, reachable by name. **COST CLAIM CORRECTED IN THE RECORD RATHER THAN EDITED AWAY.** The finding first said nine percent of the OPERATOR'S help was unreachable and named `SET` and `ABOUT` as headline losses -- which reads as missing SET documentation. It is not: those rows are the runtime MESSAGE CATALOG, `{placeholder}` templates that CMDHELP excludes from rendering ON PURPOSE (`:1705` drops STATUS as validator material; MESSAGE is absent from `render_kind_default` at `:1580`). The sentence was written before a row was sampled. TRUE FIGURE: of 2,757 owner-keyed rows, **530 are operator-facing** (ERROR 478, WARNING 32, HINT 19, DEPRECATION 1) across 83 of 139 topics; 2,227 stay hidden by design. Real, and smaller than claimed. **THE COUNTER THAT CAME OUT OF IT FOUND A SECOND DEFECT ON ITS FIRST RUN.** `write_fixed` now counts truncations and all 26 character columns pass their name. NAME was widened 40 -> 64 (84 message keys were losing their tails) expecting silence; the run reported **83 values cut in HELP_TOPIC.SUMMARY, C200, mid-word, on every build the store has ever had** -- `DOT\|CDX ...operate on the in-RA`. The steward had estimated 69 by inference from disk and was wrong in BOTH directions. **THE INSTRUMENT BEAT THE REASONING, which is the argument for building instruments.** **SUMMARY IS NOW A DECLARED PREVIEW (owner ruling).** Its full text already lives in HELP_LINE as KIND=SUMMARY rows, split across parts by the pseudo-memo -- one fact in two homes, the short one silent about it. Widening alone moves the cut, so the preview STAYS and ADMITS what it is: word boundary, ellipsis, its own report line. Truncation now means ONLY "a value was lost and nothing else holds it". Measured after: 83 over the old width, 65 over the new, 18 now whole, 65 ellipsis rows on disk matching the report exactly, 0 exceeding 255, truncation line ABSENT. **255, NOT THE RULED 256 -- A dBASE III FIELD-LENGTH DESCRIPTOR IS ONE BYTE.** Asked for as 256 it wrapped to 0; the build carrying it wrote a ZERO-WIDTH SUMMARY column and reported `500 value(s) truncated`, every summary reduced to nothing. MSVC caught it (C4305/C4309) and **the OWNER read the warnings while the steward was trusting his own test** -- a Python port of the preview logic, six cases, all green, and structurally incapable of finding it because Python integers do not wrap. A proxy that cannot answer the question put to it: the same family as the three Gate 4 assertions withdrawn earlier in this flush, and the first one COMMITTED rather than merely warned about. `V6_HINTS` gains 8e2. **AND IT IS THE SHARPEST ENTRY ON THE x64 BILL: the owner asked for one more character than the format can count.** **WHY THE STORE IS dBASE III AT ALL, recorded because it existed nowhere but the owner's memory** -- "this help system was the first one I wrote so it was x32, also I had no real memo of any kind, so I made a pseudo memo with help lines... then I forgot about it, because it works". HELP_LINE splits one logical line into 240-byte PARTS across numbered rows and reassembles by LINE_NO + PART_NO: a memo field written in the vocabulary the format had before the engine had memo. It does not strain -- PART_NO 1: 29,255 rows, 2: 6, 3: 1, carrying a 719-char VDISK summary. The steward had traced `split_parts()` through that same function two days earlier while chasing THIS finding and read it as an implementation quirk; an agent told to "modernize HELP" would delete a working design. **DOGFOODING EXCEPTION RULED: it STANDS, on purpose** -- documentation databases are x64 and manualgen's MAN* catalog is, but HELP is the subsystem every other lane measures itself through and converting it buys conformance in a store showing no functional strain. Of 38 live dBase III tables under `dottalkpp/data`, the six HELP tables are the only ones that are not deliberate flavour coverage. CONVERSION IS ALSO NOT MERELY A COPY: `COPY TO <n> AS X64 VECTOR` would convert the files in minutes (the pattern `mcc_build_x64.dts` already uses), but `helpdata_export_dbf.cpp:36` hardcodes `version {0x03}` and the exporter HAND-ROLLS ITS OWN DBF WRITER rather than using the engine's -- so the next `CMDHELP BUILD` reverts it. **The second DBF writer is the real dogfooding gap; the format byte is the symptom.** **OPEN, NOT RULED:** (1) 56 of the 139 owner topics have ZERO renderable rows and can never render -- `topics=0` inverted, and they look like MSGMGR's surface rather than CMDHELP's; (2) whether shared messages should key as `COMMAND:SET` (what the store already believes) or `DOT\|SET`, a modelling call that must not ride along on a defect fix; (3) 167 rows are `STATUS=pending` and `CONFID=AUTHORITATIVE` at once -- the store saying the same content is settled and unwritten; (4) porting the exporter onto the engine's writer. **INSTRUMENTS LEFT BEHIND:** `tools/coordination/help_store_check.py` (reads both tables directly, no engine and no build, checks the JOIN every single-table gate was blind to, and diffs topic SETS rather than gating on a count -- a count floor scored a REPAIR as a regression the same day) and `tools/coordination/help_build_order_check.py`, now steps 4 and 5 of `tools/fullstack_docs/docpush_preflight.py`. Owner `member.derald`; steward `member.ai.claude.cowork`. |
| AIF-127 | An x64 table with thirteen rows cannot be read by the project's own shared X64/DBF reader, and the same table with fourteen rows reads fine; Cowork 2026-08-25, found opening the manualgen MAN catalog for flush v5 Phase 6 -- seven of eight tables read and matched their MANUAL CATALOG COUNTS exactly, MANHASH declined with "no real field descriptors found" | doc_tooling, dbf, x64, reader_defect, data_dependent, periodic_failure, false_terminator, integrity_table, manualgen, r6_shape | FIXED in `tools/fullstack_docs/dbfread.py` -- one helper `_descriptor_end()` replacing the same flawed scan in both places it appeared | `tools/fullstack_docs/dbfread.py:175` scans for the classic dBase descriptor terminator starting at offset 32 -- INSIDE the x64 phantom block, whose first field is the row count. `13 == 0x0D`, so byte 32 IS the terminator byte on the very first probe: `term` stays at 32, `ext` begins at 33, and the `X64M` marker (offset 257 in every MAN table, 289 in MANSECTION) is never found. The classic path at `:113` applies the same test and yields zero descriptors. File proven INTACT by arithmetic without parsing a field: 13 x 317 reclen + 431 hdrlen = 4,552 vs 4,553 bytes on disk (+1 EOF marker). | **FIXED 2026-08-25** (`6a131dac8`), review-needed. Option 3 was taken -- identify positively AND validate -- in `tools/fullstack_docs/dbfread.py`: one helper `_descriptor_end()` replacing the same flawed scan in both places it appeared (`:113` and `:175`). The module's deliberate refusal to key on the block version number is untouched; the defect was never the choice to derive, it was deriving from a byte whose value is CONTENT rather than structure. PROVEN on 206 files, old reader against new: 205 identical, 1 FIXED (MANHASH, 5 fields / 13 rows), 0 BROKE, 0 differ, 0 error-both. Periodicity demonstrated rather than argued -- a real table re-stamped to 12/13/269/270/525 fails on the old reader at exactly 13, 269 and 525 and reads on the new at all five. MANHASH now verifies through the SHARED reader with no one-off parser, reproducing the hand-rolled result exactly (match 12, drift 1, missing 0). | **THE FAILURE IS DATA-DEPENDENT AND PERIODIC.** Any x64 table whose row count carries `0x0D` in its low byte is affected -- 13, 269, 525, 781, every 256 rows. A table that reads today becomes unreadable when it grows to the wrong size and readable again one row later, with no change to schema, writer or file. **It is silent in the worst way that is still honest:** the reader declines rather than returning wrong values -- correct behaviour, and why this is a bug report and not a data-loss report -- but the caller sees "this table has no fields", which reads as a malformed file, so the natural next move is to suspect the data. **The affected table is the integrity table:** MANHASH is where the manualgen catalog records the hashes it verifies its own published artifacts against, so the one MAN table the doc tooling cannot open is the one that proves the others are what they claim to be. Measured across the accepted catalog, byte 32 is the low byte of the row count in EVERY table: MANRUN 3 / MANREVIEW 3 / MANPUB 4 / MANAPPX 6 / MANMEDIA 9 / MANANCHOR 9 / MANSECTION 25 all ok, MANHASH 13 unreadable. Full record: `docs/maintenance/lanes/full_stack_documentation/AIF127_FINDING_X64_FALSE_TERMINATOR_V1.md`. |
| AIF-128 | A precondition guard names the command registry in its error message but tests the UNION of the registry with two alias sources, so when the registry is the thing that failed the guard passes and the program blames 270 innocent reference-catalog entries instead; Cowork 2026-08-25, found while making two adjacent authority labels agree in the same gate output -- the guard was four lines below the line being edited | doc_tooling, guard_defect, aif118_shape, precondition, fails_open, misdirected_blame, count_discipline, refcheck, proven_by_fault_injection | FIXED in `tools/fullstack_docs/refcheck_v1.py` -- guard the registry, report the alias sources rather than counting them toward the verdict | `tools/fullstack_docs/refcheck_v1.py` -- the guard read `commands = registry \| shortcut_aliases \| routed_aliases` then `if not commands: ... could not resolve the command registry ... return 2`. The message names ONE authority; the test covers THREE sources. Alias sources contribute 107 names on this tree, so the registry could vanish entirely and the union would still be non-empty. | **FIXED 2026-08-25**, review-needed -- test `commands_reg`, report `commands_alias` rather than counting it toward the verdict. Union check subsumed: non-empty registry implies non-empty union, so no case is lost. Finding: `docs/maintenance/lanes/full_stack_documentation/AIF128_FINDING_GUARD_TESTS_THE_UNION_V1.md` | **IT DOES NOT FAIL QUIET -- IT FAILS LOUD AND AT THE WRONG FILE, WHICH IS WORSE.** Proven by injecting an empty `registry_map` with the alias sources intact: the "could not resolve the command registry" message NEVER PRINTED, and refcheck instead reported `GUARDED phantoms (dotref+foxref): 270` and `FAIL: a native/legacy reference entry names no command, function, or sub-form`, exit 1. Every one of those 270 hand-authored entries was correct. **Exit 1 says "your catalogs are wrong"; exit 2 says "I could not measure". The guard was silently converting the second into the first**, sending the next reader to edit six reference catalogs while the actual casualty went unmentioned. After the fix: exit 2, zero stdout, the registry named. **THE DISCRIMINATOR, because a union-then-guard is not wrong by itself:** the sweep that found this also found two instances that are CORRECT -- `tools/coordination/next_aif.py:66` and `next_r.py:122` both guard `reg \| cite` style unions, and there the union IS the authority (a number taken in EITHER place is taken, as next_aif's docstring rules explicitly), with each source additionally reporting its own absence by name. In refcheck the union is NOT the authority: the registry is, and the alias sources are supplementary spellings OF registry entries. **The rule: guard the authority you are about to NAME in the error message, not the working set you happened to build from it.** **NOT CARELESSNESS -- ONE VARIABLE SERVING TWO QUESTIONS.** The union was assembled for the RESOLUTION step (an entry resolves if it is a command, an alias, a sub-form or a function) and the guard was then written against the variable in hand. "Can I measure at all?" and "does this name exist?" take different inputs. **HOW IT WAS FOUND IS THE ARGUMENT FOR THE COUNT DISCIPLINE.** It surfaced during two cosmetic label commits (`3d85320d3`, `0edaa1ef9`) that split the same unions into named parts -- `commands_reg`/`commands_alias`, `fn_seed`/`fn_ext`/`fn_core` -- so the summary line could say what was in each number. Once the parts had names, the guard testing the wrong one was visible on sight. **Naming the parts of a count made a control-flow defect legible**, which is a benefit of the discipline beyond honest reporting and is the fourth thing the COUNT_KINDS work has now paid for. VERIFIED: healthy tree pre-fix vs post-fix is byte-identical on stdout AND stderr with exit 0 both -- no finding, count or verdict moves on a working tree; fault injection recorded above; `py_compile` clean. **FOUND, NOT FIXED:** the function half has no precondition guard at all. Currently ACCEPTABLE, not ignored -- `function_state()` three-states SYSFUNC and the label prints `SYSFUNC ABSENT` rather than a number, so the absence is reported by name; but "reported somewhere" is weaker than "guarded", and if that reporting is ever simplified this becomes the same defect. Owner `member.derald`; finder + steward `member.ai.claude.cowork`. |

Several `risk:` "values" are not values at all -- they are the first word of a wrapped prose sentence (`mutates_table_data: depends on ...` parses as `depends`). **NOT CLAIMED: that the content is wrong** -- 206 authors wrote real safety notes about real commands, the material is good and the container is not; **nor that `risk:` is a defect TODAY** -- it has zero consumers so it misleads nobody yet, and the defect is that it is READY to be consumed wrongly. `status=` is different: R127(b) already made it decide whether a command is supported, so that half is REPAIR and `risk:` is PREVENTION. **GATE 1 Q3'S ANSWER, WHICH THIS LANE INHERITS:** owner ruled harvest `risk:` as PROSE now and close the vocabulary later -- mine it into HELP as a NOTE-like kind with NO key semantics, so 206 files of safety notes stop being invisible while claiming nothing about what the keys mean. **THAT ACT IS BLOCKED ON COORDINATION, NOT ON A DECISION:** it needs `src/help/helpdata_source_miner.cpp` changed and the HELP store rebuilt, and `dottalkpp/data/help/*` belongs to a CONCURRENT session -- reads only for this lane. It must be scheduled with that session, not taken. **WORK SHAPE:** enumerate both (re-measuring at start, because a corpus measurement is invalidated by the act of adding to that corpus -- R126 s11), close each set with `status=` first since it is already load-bearing, make load-bearing only what is closed, and gate it so a new spelling fails rather than silently joining the tail. Owner `member.derald`; finder + steward `member.ai.claude.cowork`. |
| AIF-130 | An entry document told every arriving agent that a sandbox cannot build or run the engine, thirteen days after that was measured false, and three agents in a row paid for it; Cowork 2026-08-25, found when the owner asked "why aren't you running dottalkpp in your sandbox? it compiles there" -- the steward had been repeating the claim as doctrine without ever testing it | onboarding, entry_document, stale_authority, reachability, prior_art, unsound_assertion, sandbox, routing, re_derivation, count_discipline | CORRECT the entry document, since the correcting document already exists and is well cited; do NOT write a third sandbox-build doc | `AI_README.md:446-463` (the false ceiling) vs `docs/agents/HANDOFF_CLAUDE_COWORK_SANDBOX_BUILD_2026-08-12.md` (the correction), `labtalk/registries/portal_recall_graph.yaml:105-112` (`trigger.work_in_sandbox`), `.github/workflows/ci.yml` job `ubuntu-core` | CORRECTED 2026-08-25, review-needed. `AI_README.md` rewritten; prior-art tooling fixed in `3db5c9f65` | **THE TRUTH WAS REACHABLE; THE FALSEHOOD WAS UNAVOIDABLE.** `AI_README.md` carried a 2026-07-31 table showing `cmake / ninja` ABSENT and concluded "the practical ceiling in such a sandbox is per-translation-unit `g++ -fsyntax-only`. Builds and runs are therefore maintainer-operated handoffs." Measured false 2026-08-12 by a full build plus `REGRESSION RUN WORKSPACE_WRITEBACK` green twice, and again 2026-08-25 in a different sandbox shape. **ELEVEN DOCUMENTS CITE THE CORRECTION AND THE CORRECTED DOCUMENT IS NOT ONE OF THEM** -- so an agent onboarding CORRECTLY, in the mandatory order, reaches "cannot build" and stops. Only an agent who happens to fire `trigger.work_in_sandbox` learns otherwise, and nothing fires a trigger on an agent's behalf. **THAT ASYMMETRY, NOT OBSCURITY, IS THE DEFECT.** **THREE INDEPENDENT RE-DERIVATIONS.** The 2026-08-12 handoff recorded the recipe; a second agent re-derived it by trial anyway, which is why the trigger exists (the recall graph says so in its own comment); this session was the third and re-derived it again. Cost each time: roughly an hour, plus a near-miss on writing a duplicate seed -- the same R5 two-answers-to-one-question defect as the two `docpush_preflight.py` files folded in `9fa5b78d6`. **THE PRIOR-ART CHECK ANSWERED PASS FIVE TIMES AND WAS WRONG FIVE TIMES**, fixed in `3db5c9f65`: it searched three files inside one lane and reported "no prior mention" -- an unbounded claim from a bounded search, the unsound-assertion family again. Widening it to 26 sources was NOT sufficient: `--prior-art "sandbox build"` still read clean because the answering document is titled "a Cowork sandbox CAN build and run the engine" and those two words never appear adjacent in the tree. A substring test on a multi-word subject asks whether someone happened to phrase it your way. It now requires every TOKEN in any order and a PASS names its scope. **WHAT REMAINS TRUE AND IS KEPT:** the staged `bin-wsl-lean` ELF still will not execute in a sandbox -- but that is a property of THAT BINARY, not a ceiling on the sandbox, and conflating the two is what produced the false rule. A green in a sandbox is still not a green on the maintainer's toolchain; name the platform every time. **FOUND WHILE CLAIMING THIS NUMBER, NOT YET RULED:** `taken()` in `session_coordinator.py` and `next_aif.py` disagree -- union 134 vs 127, highest `AIF-999999` vs `AIF-129`. `docs/maintenance/R126_IDENTITY_NUMBERS_ARE_INTEGERS_RULING_V1.md:64,182` mentions AIF-998/999/1000/999999 as EXAMPLES OF THE ALLOCATOR RANGE and the prose scan reads them as citations, permanently reserving four numbers. This is R126 s11 recurring on the AIF side after being fixed on the R side in the same session, and THE COUNT DISCIPLINE exactly: one authority holding two KINDS (real citations, arithmetic examples) with no discriminator. Harmless today -- both allocators still agree the lowest free number is 130, which is why this claim is safe -- and `idcite.py`'s `id-cite:ignore` marker already exists to fix it, since `git_committed_aifs` honours it and DECLARATIONS are unsuppressible by construction. Not applied without a ruling. Owner `member.derald`; finder + steward `member.ai.claude.cowork`. **THE SWEEP WAS INCOMPLETE, AND THAT IS THIS LANE'S OWN LESSON RECURRING -- corrected 2026-08-26.** AIF-130 fixed the ENTRY document and did not look for siblings, so the same false ceiling stayed live in TWO more places for a day: `docs/maintenance/lanes/full_stack_documentation/METACOLLECT_RUNBOOK_V1.md:23` -- a heading reading "Build (host; not buildable in the mounted sandbox)" -- and `tools/coordination/help_build_order_check.py:44` -- "works in a sandbox that cannot compile". The second had ALREADY been identified and corrected in a PROJECT DOC and never in the SOURCE, which is the AIF-130 defect itself: **a correction that lands somewhere other than where the reader arrives is not a correction.** **IT COST A PHASE.** Flush v6 Phase 5 was filed OWNER-BLOCKED on "metacollect is a Windows exe", the runbook's own heading agreeing, until the block was tested: `dt_meta` at `CMakeLists.txt:771` is fully enumerated (11 TUs, two include dirs, cxx_std_17), needs no CMake, and BUILT UNDER g++ 11.4 IN UNDER FORTY SECONDS, then ran a full source scan in one call. Both files now carry the measured recipe instead of the ceiling. **AND A THIRD CEILING OF THE SAME FAMILY, FOUND THE SAME NIGHT AND NOT YET RULED:** `tools/manualgen/build_postbaseline_supported_command_pages.py:391` reads `if sys.version_info[:2] != (3, 12): raise SystemExit("Python 3.12.x is required")` -- an EQUALITY, so it refuses 3.13 as well as 3.10, and it is what made Phase 6's page generator read as blocked too. **MEASURED, with the guard neutralised in a COPY and nothing in the tree touched:** byte-identical output on 3.10, 3.11, 3.12 and 3.13 (`selected 1 key(s)`, `DOT\|BUILD`, `PASS_CANDIDATE_ONLY`). **The code contradicts its own guard:** all 35 files carry `from __future__ import annotations`, so the `dict[str, ...]` and `str \| None` annotations are never evaluated, and the whole set parses clean under 3.8 grammar. The real floor is **3.9**, not 3.12 -- `str.removeprefix`/`removesuffix` (3.9+) at `manualgen_lib/validation.py:58`, `gate4_acceptance.py:150`, `publication_structure_candidate.py:61` are what would actually stop 3.8. NOT CHANGED HERE -- a version guard is a contract and loosening it is a ruling, not a cleanup. NOT CLAIMED: that one dry-run invocation proves the tool is 3.9-safe on every path. **THE GENERAL RULE THE THREE SHARE:** a version guard is a fact about an INTERPRETER and "it is a Windows exe" is a fact about a FILE; neither is a fact about the QUESTION, and neither is a reason to file an item blocked while a compiler or an interpreter is one command away. **THE SWEEP WAS STILL INCOMPLETE AFTER THE FIRST RE-SWEEP -- and the one it missed was the WORST PLACED. Corrected 2026-08-26 on the owner's question "did you update the ai portal so the next claude chat does not have to rediscover it can compile and run all of the apps and scripts in the sandbox".** The answer was no. Two documents had been corrected and a lane doctrine written, but the ROUTE an arriving agent walks had not been touched. `CLAUDE.md:79` was headed **"Sandbox agents: NO git, and you cannot build"**, with a bullet reading "Assume you cannot build or run the engine" and the `g++ -fsyntax-only` ceiling drawn from the same retired 2026-07-31 table. **CLAUDE.md is where a Claude session STARTS**, it is tier 1 in the recall graph, and `doctrine.sandbox_git_and_build` -- whose label ALSO ended "assume you cannot build" -- is the FIRST node `trigger.work_in_sandbox` returns. **So the trigger AIF-130 pointed to as the fix was leading with the falsehood it was meant to route around.** That is this lane's defect twice compounded: a correction that lands somewhere other than where the reader arrives is not a correction, and a router is only as true as its labels. **FIVE agents have now re-derived this**, the fifth being the session writing this row, which filed two v6 items OWNER-BLOCKED on "it is a Windows exe" and "it requires Python 3.12" while the corrected `AI_README.md` sat one hop away. **CORRECTED IN FIVE PLACES, and the route is now checked rather than asserted:** `CLAUDE.md` heading and bullet, rewritten with a MEASURED per-program table (dottalkpp ~9 min; metacollect UNDER 40 SECONDS with no cmake, `dt_meta` at CMakeLists.txt:771 enumerating every TU; store rebuild 2.9 s; manualgen, the harvest exporter and the R127 page generator all running, on a sandbox carrying 3.10/3.11/3.12/3.13 side by side) plus what remains true (the `bin-wsl-lean` ELF, and that a sandbox green is never a green on the maintainer's toolchain); `portal_recall_graph.yaml` node label AND anchor -- the anchor had to move because the heading changed, which is worth naming: **a label is a claim and an anchor is a coupling, and both go stale when the target is corrected elsewhere**; a new node `doctrine.sandbox_changes_the_push` wired to the same trigger; `RECALL_FALLBACK_TABLE_V1.md` REGENERATED from the graph (it is generated by contract, and it had been carrying the old label verbatim -- proof that the generated mirror is the right design, since one regeneration fixed it); `AI_TIER1_SEED_V1.md` given a routing row for building IN A SANDBOX, because its existing "Build and run" row pointed only at WSL (7,326 B of the 8,192 B ceiling, 866 B headroom); and `AI_README.md` widened from the engine to the whole doc stack. VERIFIED: `recall.py work_in_sandbox` now leads with "you CAN build and run", every anchor resolves, and regenerating the fallback table is idempotent. **THE STANDING LESSON, which is bigger than this claim:** correcting a false ceiling means correcting the ROUTE, not the document you happened to trip over -- and the route includes router LABELS and generated MIRRORS, not just prose. Owner `member.derald`; finder + steward `member.ai.claude.cowork`. |
| AIF-131 | A registry key containing a space can never be dispatched, because the shell reads ONE token and looks that up -- so multiword registrations read as working wiring and are dead on arrival, and the catalogs then publish them as implemented; Cowork 2026-08-25/26, found when `BUILD VECTORS` answered `Unknown command: BUILD` at the prompt while `dotref` and the HELP store both said supported=yes | dispatch, registry, multiword_key, unsound_assertion, reads_as_working, catalog_disagreement, count_discipline, aif118_shape, selfdoc, metacollect, r5_shape | PARTLY FIXED. `BUILD` became a ROUTER in `90e5dce0b`; FIVE more multiword registrations remain, in two grades, and the field that should have caught all of them cannot answer | `src/cli/shell_api.cpp:296-306` and `:343-375` -- both executors do `tok >> cmd; U = up(cmd); registry().run(area, U, tok)`. `cli::preprocess_for_dispatch` is the only rewrite ahead of the lookup and handles exactly two forms (`SET RELATIONS ...` and `RELATIONS ...` -> `REL ...`); nothing joins two tokens into a key. Surviving multiword registrations: `src/cli/shell_commands.cpp:562-564` (`ERROR CLEAR`, `ERROR STATUS`, `ERROR TEST`), `:276` (`SET UNIQUE`), `:331` (`SET RELATION`). `dottalkpp/data/help/COMMANDS.dbf` ids 386/387/388 publish the three ERROR spellings as `implemented=yes; supported=yes` | **ROUTER LANDED 2026-08-26** (`90e5dce0b`), review-needed; the five survivors are FOUND, NOT FIXED and no prescription is made here. Records: `docs/maintenance/lanes/full_stack_documentation/runs/DOCFLUSH-20260825-001/GATE4_ADDENDUM_ROUTER_CYCLE_V1.md` (the content diff that saw it) and `.../metacollect_phase/PHASE5_PREFLIGHT_AND_FINDING_V1.md` sections 3 and 7 (the survivors, and `dispatch_reachable`). Claim `coordination/aif/AIF-131.claim` | **THE OLD `implemented=yes` WAS NOT A LIE, IT WAS AN ANSWER TO A DIFFERENT QUESTION.** `IMPLEMENT` reports whether a REGISTRATION EXISTS; every reader takes it to mean whether the command CAN BE TYPED. Those two questions have the same answer for every single-token command in the catalog, which is why the divergence survived until a multiword key was added -- the proxy family that has now cost this lane four withdrawn or corrected assertions. So the `yes -> no` flip on `BUILD VECTORS` and `BUILD INFO` is a CORRECTION scored as a regression by anyone reading the count. **TWO GRADES, AND COUNTING THEM TOGETHER WOULD BE THE ERROR.** Dead AND UNSERVED: `ERROR CLEAR` / `ERROR STATUS` / `ERROR TEST` -- there is NO `registry().add("ERROR", ...)` anywhere in the tree, so no parent reads the next token and the typed form dies exactly as `BUILD VECTORS` did; the underscore spellings at `:556-558` are single tokens and DO work, so the capability is reachable and the spelling the help publishes is not. Dead BUT SERVED: `SET UNIQUE` and `SET RELATION` -- `SET` IS registered at `:275` and `cmd_SET` reads its own next token (`cmd_set.cpp:1848` UNIQUE, `:1969` RELATION), which is the mechanism the router comment names; these two spellings WORK and the multiword keys are inert duplicates. Inert is not free: they occupy registry slots, emit catalog rows for keys that can never be typed, and read to the next maintainer as the pattern to copy -- which is how `ERROR CLEAR` and `BUILD VECTORS` came to exist. **THE TWO CATALOGS DISAGREE ABOUT THE SAME THREE COMMANDS (R5).** `metacollect`'s `compact_command_name` strips space, underscore AND hyphen, so `ERROR CLEAR` compacts to the same key as `ERROR_CLEAR` and the fold picks the underscore spelling as canonical: the three spaced ERROR registrations DO NOT APPEAR IN SYSCMD AT ALL (0 of 229 candidate rows), while `COMMANDS.dbf` publishes all three as working. A reader checking the metadata catalog finds nothing wrong. **The fold is not a fix; it is concealment that happens to point the right way.** `SET RELATION` and `SET UNIQUE` survive as the only two multiword `CAN_NAME`s in 229 rows, because they have no underscore twin to fold into. `command_catalog.cpp` is a THIRD catalog with the same shape -- `--compare` reports `SET FILTER`, `SET INDEX`, `SET ORDER` as SOURCE_ONLY. **THE CHECK THAT SHOULD HAVE PRINTED THIS AS A ROW CANNOT ANSWER.** `metacollect`'s facts CSV carries a column named `dispatch_reachable` -- the exact question this lane is about. It is `false` on ALL 1,083 rows emitted: ABOUT false, LIST false, COUNT false. `src/meta/metacollect.cpp:1329` is its ONLY assignment and it sits inside `add_metadata_row_fact`, so every source-derived fact keeps the struct default; and `SYSCMD.dbf` has six fields (`CMD_ID`, `CAN_NAME`, `TYPE`, `VIS`, `HANDLER`, `ACTIVE`) with no `DISP_REACH` / `DISPATCH` / `HAS_HDLR`, so the metadata path cannot set it either. **False in every invocation this repository can currently produce, and emitted anyway** -- a consumer cannot distinguish `not reachable` from `not computed`. It is the sharpest instance of the proxy family precisely because it is NAMED after the right question; had it worked, it would have printed this row itself. **PROVEN AT RUNTIME BEFORE THE ROUTER LANDED** (`BUILD VECTORS` -> `Unknown command: BUILD`); the five survivors are SOURCE-EVIDENCED only, and the one-line settlement is written down so they are QUEUED not blocked: type `ERROR STATUS` and expect `Unknown command: ERROR`. Owner `member.derald`; finder + steward `member.ai.claude.cowork`. |
| AIF-133 | A FIELDMGR restructure backup was opened as a work area and SORTED FIRST, so it took area 0 and shifted every area after it; Cowork 2026-08-26, found while auditing why STUDENTS.__fldbak.dbf was a 22,425-byte member of a live MINIDB container | engine_scratch, directory_scan, convention_without_readers, area_allocation, minidb, writeback, r5, search_shape, self_caught | Give the naming convention ONE home both the writer and every scanner can reach, then exclude it in both scans | `src/core/fields_mgr.cpp` make_backup_dbf_path / make_temp_dbf_path; `src/gui/core/session.cpp` is_dbf_file (extension-only); `src/cli/cmd_workspace.cpp` is_dbf (ieq_ext); new `include/dottalk/scratch_sidecar.hpp` | FIXED 2026-08-26, review-needed. Verified on BOTH toolchains: Linux g++ 13 by before/after run, then MSVC compiled and RUN by the owner -- `WORKSPACE OPEN` on a directory holding a real table plus a backup opened 1 table at area 0 and skipped the scratch | **THE SORT IS WHAT TURNS UNTIDY INTO WRONG.** Scratch keeps the `.dbf` extension, scans test extension only, and the scan sorts by filename -- and `.__fldbak` sorts BEFORE `.dbf`. Measured with two binaries against one directory holding REALTAB.dbf plus both scratch forms: BEFORE `Area 0: REALTAB.__fldbak.dbf` / `Area 1: REALTAB.__FLDTMP.dbf` / `Area 2: REALTAB.dbf`, 3 areas; AFTER `Area 0: REALTAB.dbf`, 1 area. **The backup did not tag along at the end, it became the ACTIVE area.** A posture saved from that state records a backup as the active table and a shifted numbering; a MINIDB save carries its bytes; WRITEBACK returns them to disk. Already happened -- `STUDENTS.__fldbak.dbf` is a 22,425-byte member of a live container, 17 percent of that payload. Live today, not historical: an `FMGRTST` restructure backup sits in the x64 DBF root and is not gitignored. **Named, not cited as a path** -- a tracked document pointing at an untracked scratch file is a WIDOW, and `cited-paths` flagged this row for exactly that on the commit that added it. The advisory offers two fixes and only one is right here: staging it would TRACK a scratch file, which is the thing this AIF exists to stop treating as real. **THE CAUSE: A CONVENTION WITH EXACTLY ONE PARTICIPANT.** `git grep fldbak -- src/` returned one line outside a test comment -- the line that CREATES it. The rule lived as two string literals inside `fields_mgr.cpp`, so no scanner could honour a rule it had no way to read. The fix gives it a home both sides reach; `fields_mgr` now BUILDS from the constants the scanners TEST against, because a second copy of the strings beside the new reader would be the same defect one layer along. **THE SECOND SCANNER, AND HOW IT WAS ALMOST MISSED.** I grepped for the GUI's spelling `extension() == ".dbf"`, found nothing in `src/cli`, and REPORTED that the CLI might not share the defect. It shares it exactly, spelled `ieq_ext(de.path(), ".dbf")`. A search shaped by the object you have cannot find an object with a different schema -- the house rule, applied to me, one message after I had quoted it at the prior-art tool. Vary the query form before concluding absence. **CASE-INSENSITIVE ON PURPOSE.** These live on a case-insensitive filesystem; a guard that missed `.__FLDBAK.DBF` would have a hole in exactly the case nobody tests, so the verification directory used one upper-case form. **NOT CLAIMED:** that this is every scanner. Two were found and fixed; a third spelling could exist under a form neither query matched, and the header is where it gets pointed rather than copied. **NOT DONE:** no existing scratch file was deleted -- the fix stops them being OPENED, and what to do with the ones on disk is a separate decision for the owner. **OWED:** Windows re-run; a sandbox green is not a green on the maintainer's toolchain. Owner `member.derald`; finder + steward `member.ai.claude.cowork`. |
