Report residue and the tuple-stream mode selector closed in a follow-up commit the same day; MSVC clean. NOT MEASURED: the arms have never been run against the pre-fix binary | **PHYSICAL IS AN ORDER IN THIS HOUSE** (owner: "it has been our house usage"), so the remedy could not be a second boolean that can mean "no order". isNaturalOrder() returns true for natural -- nothing attached, OR a tag container with no tag -- and false for the container's order. Both values are an answer; there is no third state. hasOrder() was NOT redefined: about seventy callers genuinely mean "is a container attached", including the detach logic in cmd_cdx, cmd_cnx and cmd_rebuild and the invalidation keyed on container path. **GO 5 KEPT WORKING WHILE GO TOP DID NOT**, which is R121 already ruled -- addressing is absolute, traversal is filtered. **SKIP IS THE SEVERITY.** The other six REFUSED, which a reader can see in a transcript; SKIP ANSWERED, and the answer was false. **WHY SIXTY REGISTERED SPECS SAID NOTHING, and this is the transferable half.** (a) 345 markers across the tracked corpus and NOT ONE mentions RECNO -- not laziness but a LANGUAGE LIMIT already recorded four times (cnx_persist_proof, cnx_realtime_buffer_proof, cnx_realtime_index_proof, index_maintenance_failure_proof all say RECNO() renders EMPTY in a `?` marker and STR() does not rescue it), so no arm in the tree can say "the cursor is at 1". (b) Every navigation line in the corpus is followed by a marker that reads a FIELD, and USE, SELECT and CREATE have already parked the cursor on record 1 -- so a failed TOP and a working TOP READ IDENTICALLY. workspace_load_shortfall.dts:146-149 is the shape exactly. (c) STOP_ON_ERROR is opt-in, so a printed refusal sits above green markers and the run continues. **THE COUNTERMEASURE IS STRUCTURAL, NOT DILIGENCE**: every arm in NAV_NATURAL parks the cursor elsewhere with GO <n> first and then reads a field, because an arm that reads row 1 on a table already standing on row 1 cannot fail. The fixture sorts backwards on purpose so natural TOP and tag TOP name different rows, and two arms assert the tag direction -- a fix that forced natural ALWAYS would have passed the six failing arms and been exactly as wrong. **THE RESIDUE WAS MIS-CLASSIFIED WHEN IT WAS FIRST LISTED.** Four sites were called report-only. One of them, db_tuple_stream.cpp:267, is a NAVIGATION MODE SELECTOR: it entered OrderVector mode on the container predicate, and what kept it honest was an empty-vector fallback further down that demotes to Physical when the tagless collect returns nothing. Correctness by luck rather than by the predicate -- a collector returning a non-empty wrong vector would have navigated wrongly with no guard. The fallback is kept and the question corrected. **A SECOND DEFECT WAS FOUND IN THE SAME TRANSCRIPT AND FIXED SEPARATELY** (commit a17e24488): cmd_use.cpp closed the current area before attempting the open, so a MISTYPED FILENAME DESTROYED THE OPEN TABLE -- `use peoplle` closed area 0 holding BIGCHAR_TEST. The existence check is now hoisted above the reset and USE reports that the area is untouched. Worth recording that the allocator was BLAMED FIRST and the blame was wrong; the displaced area was explained entirely by the earlier USE. |
| AIF-149 | SET RELATION CAN CROSS WORKSPACES, AND THE CLOSE PATH'S SCOPING ARGUMENT ASSUMES IT CANNOT. The relation store has been partitioned by workspace handle since AIF-078 I1.2 (2026-08-23), and the close path justifies its scoping with: "the set of areas this close emptied is exactly the closed workspaces' members, so clearing exactly those workspaces' maps leaves nothing dangling" (`src/cli/cmd_workspace.cpp`). That sentence is only true if edges never cross. Owner ruling 2026-08-30: they can, and that is INTENDED -- "set relation can cross workspace, we just have told x64base it has the tools". Raised while answering whether links spanning workspaces survive a save; Claude/Cowork 2026-08-30 | relations, workspaces, scoping, close_path, dangling_edge, owner_ruled, unmeasured, aif078 | DotTalk++ `src/cli/set_relations.hpp` (partitioned store; `clear_all_relations_for(ws)` and `clear_all_relations_everywhere()` as "the other two honest answers"), `src/cli/cmd_workspace.cpp` (the close-path scoping argument and the contract notes), `src/cli/relations_boot.cpp` | claim `coordination/aif/AIF-149.claim`; contract note committed in `a83d8f9e2`; run `COWORK-20260830-001` | OWNER-RULED that crossing is intended capability. NOT MEASURED: whether `SET RELATION` accepts a cross-workspace pair today, what a scoped close does to a crossed edge, or what LOAD does with one. No fix authorized | THE RULING INVERTS THE QUESTION. It was raised as a HAZARD -- an edge that might dangle -- and the owner ruled it a CAPABILITY. So the work is not to prevent crossing; it is to make the close path correct when it happens. THE UNSOUND STEP, PRECISELY: a crossed edge lives in the map of the workspace that OWNS THE EDGE while pointing at an area another workspace owns. Closing that other workspace clears ITS map, not this one, and empties the area the surviving edge points at. The scoping argument's own premise -- that the emptied areas are exactly the closed workspaces' members -- still holds; what fails is the inference that clearing those maps therefore leaves nothing dangling, because the DANGLING EDGE IS IN A MAP THAT WAS NOT CLEARED. The argument is unsound as written even if the behaviour turns out to be acceptable. ON SAVE, WHICH IS WHERE THE QUESTION STARTED: a relation belongs to exactly one workspace -- "the key never leaves its workspace", which is also why same-named parents in two workspaces cannot collide -- so a scoped save writes that workspace's map and A SAVE CANNOT LOSE A SPANNING LINK, because the link is not spanning in the storage sense. A crossed edge would be saved in the owning workspace's map referencing an area the posture may not contain; what LOAD does with that reference is unknown and is the second thing to measure. ADJACENT AND ALREADY CORRECTED: `a83d8f9e2` fixed a contract paragraph in the same file that still claimed relations were NOT workspace-scoped -- false since 2026-08-23 and contradicted by that file's own implementation comment about 1600 lines away. THREE OF THE FOUR STALE STATUS CLAIMS FOUND IN THAT FILE THAT DAY WERE SURFACED BY THE OWNER ASKING A QUESTION, NONE BY A GATE: `cited-paths` audits paths, `house-style` audits characters, the normalization guards audit catalog agreement, and NOTHING AUDITS A SENTENCE THAT ASSERTS A LIMITATION. OWED IMMEDIATELY: the contract note added by `a83d8f9e2` frames crossing as an untested hazard, which the ruling supersedes -- it should read as intended capability with the close-path consequence named. Recorded here so that revision is traceable to the ruling rather than looking like drift. **CORRECTED 2026-08-30, SAME DAY, AND THE CORRECTION REVERSES THIS ROW'S CENTRAL CLAIM.** Everything above was written before `add_relation` was read. MEASURED at `set_relations.cpp:608`: it resolves BOTH endpoints with `find_open_area_in_workspace_ci(..., xbase::workspace::current_handle(), ...)` and returns false with `RelDiagAddFailedNotOpenText` if either is absent. **A CROSS-WORKSPACE EDGE CANNOT BE CREATED TODAY, SO IT CANNOT DANGLE, AND THE CLOSE PATH'S SCOPING ARGUMENT IS SOUND RATHER THAN UNSOUND.** The steward filed a safe design as a hazard and published that reading twice before measuring it. **THE OWNER'S RULING STANDS AND IS ABOUT INTENT, NOT ABOUT CURRENT BEHAVIOUR:** crossing is wanted, and the tools are CHARTERED rather than live -- `set_relations.cpp:498` names "the wider split of `find_open_area_by_name_ci` into scoped / given-handle / EXPLICIT-CROSS across its 36 call sites. That is its own lane." **THIS IS THAT LANE.** **THE SUBSTANCE MOVES FROM A HAZARD TO TWO SILENCES**, both on the road to enabling crossing and both worth fixing whatever is decided about the split: (1) `add_relation` says "not open" for a child that IS open in another workspace -- THE SAME WORDS FOR "DOES NOT EXIST" AND FOR "EXISTS ELSEWHERE", the AIF-118 shape, and the sibling AIF-147 already recorded as a correct refusal delivered as an internal error; (2) `refresh_from_parent_name` does `if (!child) continue;`, so an edge that DID span would be skipped by traversal without a word. A user enabling cross-workspace relations would meet both silences before meeting the feature. NOT CLAIMED: that the resolver split is small, that `explicit-cross` is the right shape for it, or any sizing. Owner `member.derald`; steward `member.ai.claude.cowork`. |
